Proof of the geometric Langlands conjecture IV: ambidexterity
D. Arinkin, D. Beraldo, L. Chen, J. Færgeman, D. Gaitsgory, K. Lin, S. Raskin and N. Rozenblyum

Abstract
. This paper performs the following steps toward the proof of GLC in the de Rham setting: (i) We deduce GLC for G = G ​ L n G=GL_{n} ; (ii) We prove that the Langlands functor 𝕃 G {\mathbb{L}}_{G} constructed in [ GLC1 ] , when restricted to the cuspidal category, is ambidextrous ; (iii) We reduce GLC to the study of a classical vector bundle with connection , denoted 𝒜 G , irred {\mathcal{A}}_{G,\operatorname{irred}} , on the stack LS G ˇ irred \operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}}^{\operatorname{irred}} of irreducible local systems; (iv) We prove that GLC is equivalent to the contractibility of the space of generic oper structures on irreducible local systems; (v) Using [ BKS ] , we deduce GLC for classical groups.



Introduction

This paper is the fourth in the series of five papers, whose combined content will prove the geometric
Langlands conjecture (GLC), as it was formulated in
[
GLC1
, Conjecture 1.6.7]
.

0.1.
What is done in this paper?

0.1.1.

In the papers
[
GLC1
,
GLC2
,
GLC3
]
we constructed the
Langlands functor

(0.1)

{\mathbb{L}}_{G}:\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\to{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}}),

and GLC says that (
0.1
) is an equivalence.

0.1.2.

The main result of
[
GLC3
]
says that (
0.1
) induces an equivalence

\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{Eis}}\to{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}})_{\operatorname{red}},

where:

•

\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{Eis}}\subset\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
is the
full subcategory generated by Eisenstein series from proper Levi subgroups;

•

{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}})_{\operatorname{red}}\subset{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}})
is the
full subcategory consisting of objects, set-theoretically supported on the locus
of reducible local systems.

0.1.3.

As one of the first steps in this paper we will show that GLC is equivalent to the statement that the
induced functor

(0.2)

{\mathbb{L}}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}:\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{cusp}}\to{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}}),

is an equivalence (see Corollary
1.3.10
). (Note also that
{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}})
is the same as the usual

{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}})
category).

Thus, the proof of GLC amounts to the study of the functor
{\mathbb{L}}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}
.

0.1.4.

Before we even begin the discussion of the main results of this paper, we observe (see Sect.
1.7
)
that the above considerations already allow us to deduce GLC for
G=GL_{n}
.

Namely, the fact that
{\mathbb{L}}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}
is fully faithful for
GL_{n}
follows from
[
Ga2
]
(or, in a more modern language,
from
[
Be1
]
).

We then show that its essential surjectivity is equivalent to the existence of (non-zero) Hecke eigensheaves
attached to irreducible local systems, which was established in
[
FGV
]
using geometric methods (or, alternatively, in

[
BD1
]
using localization at the critical level).

0.1.5.

The main result of this paper, Theorem
3.1.4
, which we call the Ambidexterity Theorem, says that the left and right adjoints
of the functor
{\mathbb{L}}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}
are isomorphic.

This already gets us pretty close to the statement that
{\mathbb{L}}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}
is an equivalence. Yet, we will need to “milk” the
ambidexterity statement some more in order to obtain the actual proof. Some of this milking will be preformed in this paper,
and some will be delegated to its sequel.

0.1.6.

An additional crucial input comes from the paper
[
FR
]
(combined with
[
Be2
]
), which says that the functor
{\mathbb{L}}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}
is conservative.
This implies that in order to prove GLC, it is sufficient to show that the monad

(0.3)

{\mathbb{L}}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}\circ{\mathbb{L}}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}^{L}

acting on
{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}})
is isomorphic to the identity functor.

We observe (see Sect.
1.6.2
) that the monad (
0.3
) is given by tensor product with an associative algebra object

(0.4)

{\mathcal{A}}_{G,\operatorname{irred}}\in{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}}).

The monad (
0.3
) is an equivalence if and only if the unit map

(0.5)

{\mathcal{O}}_{\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}}}\to{\mathcal{A}}_{G,\operatorname{irred}}

is an isomorphism in
{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}})
.

0.1.7.

Now, the Ambidexterity Theorem tells us something about the structure of
{\mathcal{A}}_{G,\operatorname{irred}}
. Namely, it implies
that
{\mathcal{A}}_{G,\operatorname{irred}}
is self-dual as an object of
{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}})
. In particular, it is
perfect, and hence compact.

However, we prove more: we show (assuming that
G
is semi-simple) that

{\mathcal{A}}_{G,\operatorname{irred}}
is a
classical vector bundle
, equipped with a flat connection
(see Theorem
3.1.8
).

Thus, we can view
{\mathcal{A}}_{G,\operatorname{irred}}
as a classical local system on
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}}
.
We also show that this local system has a finite monodromy (see Proposition
4.2.8
);
this latter statement will play a role in the final step of the proof of GLC in the next paper in this
series.

0.1.8.

The above additional pieces of information concerning
{\mathcal{A}}_{G,\operatorname{irred}}
result from Corollary
4.2.5
, which says that the fiber
of
{\mathcal{A}}_{G,\operatorname{irred}}
at a given irredicuble local system
\sigma
is isomorphic to the homology of
the space of
generic oper structures
on
\sigma
.

We will explain the mechanism for this in Sect.
0.3
.

0.2.
How is ambidexterity proved?

0.2.1.

The proof of the Ambidexterity Theorem is obtained by essentially staring at what we call the Fundamental Commutative Diagram
(see
[
GLC2
, Diagram (18.14)]
):

(0.6)

\begin{CD}\operatorname{Whit}^{!}(G)_{\operatorname{Ran}}@>{\operatorname{CS}_{G}}>{\sim}>{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{G}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}\\
@A{\operatorname{coeff}_{G}}A{}A@A{}A{\Gamma^{\operatorname{spec},{\operatorname{IndCoh}}}_{\check{G}}}A\\
\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})@>{{\mathbb{L}}_{G}}>{}>{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}})\\
@A{\operatorname{Loc}_{G}}A{}A@A{}A{\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{spec}}_{{\check{G}},*}}A\\
\operatorname{KL}(G)_{{\operatorname{crit}},{\operatorname{Ran}}}@>{\operatorname{FLE}_{G,{\operatorname{crit}}}}>{\sim}>{\operatorname{IndCoh}}^{*}({\operatorname{Op}}^{\operatorname{mon-free}}_{\check{G}})_{\operatorname{Ran}},\end{CD}

where we ignore some cohomological shifts and twists by constant lines.

Remark 0.2.2
.

In fact, (
0.6
) is a special case at levels (
{\operatorname{crit}}
for
G
,
\infty
for
{\check{G}}
) of an analogous diagram that
is expected to exist in the quantum case:

(0.7)

\begin{CD}\operatorname{Whit}^{!}(G)_{\kappa,{\operatorname{Ran}}}@>{\operatorname{FLE}_{{\check{G}},\check{\kappa}}^{\vee}}>{\sim}>\operatorname{KL}({\check{G}})_{-\check{\kappa},{\operatorname{Ran}}}\\
@A{\operatorname{coeff}_{G}}A{}A@A{}A{\Gamma_{{\check{G}},-\check{\kappa}}}A\\
\operatorname{D-mod}_{\kappa}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})@>{{\mathbb{L}}_{G,\kappa}}>{}>\operatorname{D-mod}_{-\check{\kappa}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{\check{G}})_{\operatorname{co}}\\
@A{\operatorname{Loc}_{G,\kappa}}A{}A@A{}A{\operatorname{Poinc}_{{\check{G}},*}}A\\
\operatorname{KL}(G)_{\kappa,{\operatorname{Ran}}}@>{\operatorname{FLE}_{G,\kappa}}>{\sim}>\operatorname{Whit}_{*}({\check{G}})_{-\check{\kappa},{\operatorname{Ran}}}.\end{CD}

A remarkable feature of the quantum diagram is that it is
self-dual
: i.e., if we dualize all categories
and arrows in (
0.7
) we obtain a similar diagram, but for
((G,\kappa),({\check{G}},-\check{\kappa}))
replaced by

(({\check{G}},\check{\kappa}),(G,-\kappa))
.

0.2.3.

We break (
0.6
) into the upper and lower portions, i.e.,

(0.8)

\begin{CD}\operatorname{Whit}^{!}(G)_{\operatorname{Ran}}@>{\operatorname{CS}_{G}}>{\sim}>{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{G}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}\\
@A{\operatorname{coeff}_{G}}A{}A@A{}A{\Gamma^{\operatorname{spec},{\operatorname{IndCoh}}}_{\check{G}}}A\\
\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})@>{{\mathbb{L}}_{G}}>{}>{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}})\end{CD}

and

(0.9)

\begin{CD}\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})@>{{\mathbb{L}}_{G}}>{}>{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}})\\
@A{\operatorname{Loc}_{G}}A{}A@A{}A{\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{spec}}_{{\check{G}},*}}A\\
\operatorname{KL}(G)_{{\operatorname{crit}},{\operatorname{Ran}}}@>{\operatorname{FLE}_{G,{\operatorname{crit}}}}>{\sim}>{\operatorname{IndCoh}}^{*}({\operatorname{Op}}^{\operatorname{mon-free}}_{\check{G}})_{\operatorname{Ran}},\end{CD}

and we combine (
0.8
) (resp., (
0.9
)) with the inclusion of (resp., projection to) the
cuspidal subcategory:

\begin{CD}\operatorname{Whit}^{!}(G)_{\operatorname{Ran}}@>{\operatorname{CS}_{G}}>{\sim}>{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{G}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}\\
@A{\operatorname{coeff}_{G}}A{}A@A{}A{\Gamma^{\operatorname{spec}}_{\check{G}}}A\\
\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})@>{{\mathbb{L}}_{G}}>{}>{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}})\\
@A{{\mathbf{e}}}A{}A@A{}A{\jmath_{*}}A\\
\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{cusp}}@>{{\mathbb{L}}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}}>{}>{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}})\end{CD}

and

\begin{CD}\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{cusp}}@>{{\mathbb{L}}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}}>{}>{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}})\\
@A{{\mathbf{e}}^{L}}A{}A@A{}A{\jmath^{*}}A\\
\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})@>{{\mathbb{L}}_{G}}>{}>{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}})\\
@A{\operatorname{Loc}_{G}}A{}A@A{}A{\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{spec}}_{{\check{G}},*}}A\\
\operatorname{KL}(G)_{{\operatorname{crit}},{\operatorname{Ran}}}@>{\operatorname{FLE}_{G,{\operatorname{crit}}}}>{\sim}>{\operatorname{IndCoh}}^{*}({\operatorname{Op}}^{\operatorname{mon-free}}_{\check{G}})_{\operatorname{Ran}},\end{CD}

respectively.

0.2.4.

Thus, we obtain the diagrams

(0.10)

\begin{CD}\operatorname{Whit}^{!}(G)_{\operatorname{Ran}}@>{\operatorname{CS}_{G}}>{\sim}>{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{G}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}\\
@A{\operatorname{coeff}_{G}\circ{\mathbf{e}}}A{}A@A{}A{\Gamma^{\operatorname{spec},{\operatorname{IndCoh}}}_{\check{G}}\circ\jmath_{*}}A\\
\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{cusp}}@>{{\mathbb{L}}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}}>{}>{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}})\end{CD}

and

(0.11)

\begin{CD}\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{cusp}}@>{{\mathbb{L}}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}}>{}>{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}})\\
@A{{\mathbf{e}}^{L}\circ\operatorname{Loc}_{G}}A{}A@A{}A{\jmath^{*}\circ\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{spec}}_{{\check{G}},*}}A\\
\operatorname{KL}(G)_{{\operatorname{crit}},{\operatorname{Ran}}}@>{\operatorname{FLE}_{G,{\operatorname{crit}}}}>{\sim}>{\operatorname{IndCoh}}^{*}({\operatorname{Op}}^{\operatorname{mon-free}}_{\check{G}})_{\operatorname{Ran}},\end{CD}

respectively.

The key feature of the latter diagrams is that in (
0.10
) the right vertical arrow is fully faithful,
and in (
0.11
) the left vertical arrow is a
Verdier quotient
(a.k.a., is a localization).

0.2.5.

Starting from diagrams (
0.10
) and (
0.11
), the ambidexterity assertion is proved
as follows.

Consider the functor
dual
to
{\mathbb{L}}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}
(with respect to the natural self-dualities of the two
sides, see Sect.
2
).

The point now is that the vertical arrows in (
0.10
) admit
left
adjoints, and these left adjoints
are
essentially
1
1

1

Essentially:=up to some twists.
isomorphic to the duals of the original functors.
Combined with the fact that the right vertical arrow is fully faithful, this implies that the dual of
{\mathbb{L}}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}
is isomorphic to
the left adjoint of
{\mathbb{L}}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}
.

Similarly, the vertical arrows in (
0.11
) admit
right
adjoints, and these right adjoints
are
essentially
isomorphic to the duals of the original functors.
Combined with the fact that the left vertical arrow is a Verdier quotient,
this implies that the dual of
{\mathbb{L}}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}
is isomorphic to the right adjoint of
{\mathbb{L}}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}
.

Thus, we have identified both the left and right adjoints of
{\mathbb{L}}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}
with its dual.

0.3.
Relation to opers

We now turn to the statements announced in Sect.
0.1.8
that relate the fiber of the object
{\mathcal{A}}_{G,\operatorname{irrred}}

at a given
\sigma\in\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}}^{\operatorname{irred}}
to the homology of the space
{\operatorname{Op}}^{\operatorname{gen}}_{{\check{G}},\sigma}

of generic oper structures on
\sigma
.

0.3.1.

Let

{\mathcal{B}}^{{\operatorname{Op}}}_{G,\operatorname{irred}}\in{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}}),

be the object obtained by applying the
left
forgetful functor

{\mathbf{oblv}}^{l}:\operatorname{D-mod}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}})\to{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}})

to the object

(\pi^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\operatorname{Ran}})_{!}(\omega_{{\operatorname{Op}}^{\operatorname{mon-free,irred}}_{\check{G}}(X^{\operatorname{gen}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}}),

where:

•

{\operatorname{Op}}^{\operatorname{mon-free}}_{\check{G}}(X^{\operatorname{gen}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}
is the space of pairs
(\sigma,o)
, where
\sigma\in\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}}
,
and
o
is a generic oper structure on it;

•

\pi_{\operatorname{Ran}}:{\operatorname{Op}}^{\operatorname{mon-free}}_{\check{G}}(X^{\operatorname{gen}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}\to\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}}
is the tautological map
(\sigma,o)\mapsto\sigma
;

•

{\operatorname{Op}}^{\operatorname{mon-free,irred}}_{\check{G}}(X^{\operatorname{gen}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}
and
\pi^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}
is the base change of the above objects
along the inclusion
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}}\to\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}}
.

By construction,
{\mathcal{B}}^{{\operatorname{Op}}}_{G,\operatorname{irred}}
is naturally a co-commutative coalgebra in
{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}})
.

Note that since the map
\pi^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}
is pseudo-proper (see Sect.
3.3.11
),
the fiber of
{\mathcal{B}}^{{\operatorname{Op}}}_{G,\operatorname{irred}}
at a given
\sigma\in\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}}

is indeed given by the homology of the space

{\operatorname{Op}}^{\operatorname{gen}}_{{\check{G}},\sigma}:=\{\sigma\}\underset{\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}}}{\times}{\operatorname{Op}}^{\operatorname{mon-free}}_{\check{G}}(X^{\operatorname{gen}})

of generic oper structures on
\sigma
.

0.3.2.

The point of departure is Theorem
4.6.3
, which says that the comonad on

{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}})\simeq{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}})

given by

\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{spec}}_{{\check{G}},*,\operatorname{irred}}\circ(\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{spec}}_{{\check{G}},*,\operatorname{irred}})^{R},\quad\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{spec}}_{{\check{G}},*,\operatorname{irred}}:=\jmath^{*}\circ\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{spec}}_{{\check{G}},*}

is isomorphic to the comonad given by tensoring with
{\mathcal{B}}^{{\operatorname{Op}}}_{G,\operatorname{irred}}
.

0.3.3.

Consider the comonad

{\mathbb{L}}_{G}\circ{\mathbb{L}}_{G}^{R}

acting on
{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}})
. Since it is
{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}})
-linear,
it is given by tensor product with a co-associative coalgebra object, denoted

{\mathcal{B}}_{G,\operatorname{irred}}\in{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}}).

The fact that the left vertical arrow in (
0.11
) is a Verdier quotient implies that we have an isomorphism of comonads

{\mathbb{L}}_{G}\circ{\mathbb{L}}_{G}^{R}\simeq\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{spec}}_{{\check{G}},*,\operatorname{irred}}\circ(\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{spec}}_{{\check{G}},*,\operatorname{irred}})^{R}.

Combining with Theorem
4.6.3
, we obtain an isomorphism
2
2

2

One can show that this isomorphism respects the
co-associative coalgebra structures, but we will neither prove nor use this fact.

{\mathcal{B}}_{G,\operatorname{irred}}\simeq{\mathcal{B}}^{{\operatorname{Op}}}_{G,\operatorname{irred}}.

However, the Ambidexterity Theorem implies that
{\mathcal{A}}_{G,\operatorname{irred}}\simeq{\mathcal{B}}_{G,\operatorname{irred}}
(as plain objects of
{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}})
).
Combining, we obtain an isomorphism

(0.12)

{\mathcal{A}}_{G,\operatorname{irred}}\simeq{\mathcal{B}}^{{\operatorname{Op}}}_{G,\operatorname{irred}},

also as plain objects of
{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}})
.

From here we obtain the desired statements relating the fiber of
{\mathcal{A}}_{G,\operatorname{irred}}
at a given irreducible local system
\sigma

with the homology of
{\operatorname{Op}}^{\operatorname{gen}}_{{\check{G}},\sigma}
.

0.3.4.

Note, however, that the isomorphism (
0.12
) gives us more. Namely, since we already know that
{\mathcal{A}}_{G,\operatorname{irred}}
is concentrated
in cohomological degree
0
, we obtain that the connected components of
{\operatorname{Op}}^{\operatorname{gen}}_{{\check{G}},\sigma}

are
homologically contractible
.

And since GLC is equivalent to the fact that the map (
0.5
) is an isomorphism, we obtain that it is equivalent to either
of the following:

•

For every irreducible
\sigma
, the space
{\operatorname{Op}}^{\operatorname{gen}}_{{\check{G}},\sigma}
is homologically contractible;

•

For every irreducible
\sigma
, the space
{\operatorname{Op}}^{\operatorname{gen}}_{{\check{G}},\sigma}
is connected.

0.3.5.

Recall now that a recent result of
[
BKS
]
proves the homological contractibility of the spaces
{\operatorname{Op}}^{\operatorname{gen}}_{{\check{G}},\sigma}
,
whenever
G
(and hence
{\check{G}}
) is classical.

Hence, we obtain that GLC is a theorem for classical
G
.

0.4.
Contents

We now briefly review the contents of this paper section-by-section.

0.4.1.

In Sect.
1
we review the contents of
[
GLC1
,
GLC2
,
GLC3
]
relevant for this paper, and
draw some consequences. In particular:

•

We show that the functor
{\mathbb{L}}_{G}
is an equivalence if and only if the corresponding functor
{\mathbb{L}}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}
is;

•

We show that
{\mathbb{L}}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}
is an equivalence if and only if the object
{\mathcal{A}}_{G,\operatorname{irred}}
is isomorphic
to the structure sheaf;

•

We deduce GLC for
GL_{n}
.

0.4.2.

In Sect.
2
we review the self-duality identifications on the two sides of (
0.2
), and we show that
the
left
adjoint of
{\mathbb{L}}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}
identifies with its dual, up to a twist. This uses the compatibility of the functor
{\mathbb{L}}_{G}
with the
Whittaker model, i.e., the upper portion of (
0.6
).

In Sect.
3
, we show that the
right
adjoint of
{\mathbb{L}}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}
also identifies with its dual (up to the
same twist). This uses the compatibility of the functor
{\mathbb{L}}_{G}
with localization at the critical level, i.e., the lower portion of (
0.6
).

Combining, we deduce the Ambidexterity Theorem, which says that the left and right adjoints of
{\mathbb{L}}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}
are isomorphic.

From here, we deduce that the object
{\mathcal{A}}_{G,\operatorname{irred}}\in{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}})
is self-dual, and hence
compact.

0.4.3.

In Sect.
4
we express
{\mathcal{A}}_{G,\operatorname{irred}}
via the space of generic oper structures.

As a result, we prove that
{\mathcal{A}}_{G,\operatorname{irred}}
is a classical vector bundle (when
G
is semi-simple).

And we deduce GLC for classical groups.

0.4.4.

In Sect.
5
we reduce Theorem
4.1.5
stated in the previous section to the combination
of two general assertions about the space of rational maps. These assertions are proved in Sect.
A
and Sects.

B
+
C
, respectively.

0.4.5.
Conventions and notation

The conventions and notation in this paper follow those in
[
GLC3
]
.

1.
Summary of the Langlands functor

In the papers
[
GLC1
]
, a functor

{\mathbb{L}}_{G}:\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\to{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}})

was constructed.

The geometric Langlands conjecture, a.k.a. GLC (
[
GLC1
, Conjecture 1.6.7]
), says that the functor
{\mathbb{L}}_{G}
is an equivalence.
For the duration of this paper, we will assume the validity of GLC for proper Levi subgroups of
G
.

In this section we will summarize the properties of
{\mathbb{L}}_{G}
relevant for this paper, and draw some consequences.

1.1.
The functor
{\mathbb{L}}_{G}
via the Whittaker model

In this subsection we will recall the “main” feature of the functor
{\mathbb{L}}_{G}
; its compatibility with the Whittaker model.

1.1.1.

Recall (see
[
GLC2
, Sects. 9.3 and 9.4]
) that the category
\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
is related to the

Whittaker category
by a pair of adjoint functors

\operatorname{Poinc}_{G,!}:\operatorname{Whit}^{!}(G)_{\operatorname{Ran}}\rightleftarrows\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}):\operatorname{coeff}_{G}.

1.1.2.

Recall also that the category
{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}})
is related to the category
{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{G}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}
by a pair of adjoint functors

\operatorname{Loc}_{\check{G}}^{\operatorname{spec}}:{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{G}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}\rightleftarrows{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}}):\Gamma^{\operatorname{spec},{\operatorname{IndCoh}}}_{\check{G}},

see
[
GLC2
, Sect. 17.6]
.

Note, however, that the functor
\operatorname{Loc}_{\check{G}}^{\operatorname{spec}}
factors as

{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{G}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}\to{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}})\overset{\Xi_{\{0\},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}}{\hookrightarrow}{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}}),

and the functor
\Gamma^{\operatorname{spec},{\operatorname{IndCoh}}}_{\check{G}}
factors as

{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}})\overset{\Psi_{\{0\},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}}{\twoheadrightarrow}{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}})\overset{\Gamma^{\operatorname{spec}}_{\check{G}}}{\longrightarrow}{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{G}})_{\operatorname{Ran}},

where

\Xi_{\{0\},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}:{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}})\rightleftarrows{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}}):\Psi_{\{0\},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}

are the natural embedding and projection, respectively.

1.1.3.

We record the following (see Sect.
C.1.9
for the proof):

Proposition 1.1.4
.

The functor

\Gamma^{\operatorname{spec}}_{\check{G}}:{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}})\to{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{G}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}

is fully faithful.

1.1.5.

The Langlands functor
{\mathbb{L}}_{G}
is

essentially
3
3

3

See
[
GLC1
, Sects. 1.4 and 1.6]
for what the word “essentially” refers to.

determined by the property that it makes the diagram

(1.1)

\begin{CD}\operatorname{Whit}^{!}(G)_{\operatorname{Ran}}@>{\operatorname{CS}_{G}}>{\sim}>{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{G}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}\\
@A{\operatorname{coeff}_{G}[2\delta_{N_{\rho(\omega_{X})}}]}A{}A@A{}A{\Gamma^{\operatorname{spec},{\operatorname{IndCoh}}}_{\check{G}}}A\\
\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})@>{{\mathbb{L}}_{G}}>{}>{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}})\end{CD}

commute, where
\operatorname{CS}_{G}
is the geometric Casselman-Shalika equivalence (see
[
GLC2
, Sect. 1.4]
),
and

\delta_{N_{\rho(\omega_{X})}}=\dim(\operatorname{Bun}_{N_{\rho(\omega_{X})}}).

Remark 1.1.6
.

The commutation of (
1.1
) is the point of departure for any of the constructions of the
Langlands functor.

1.1.7.

It is shown in
[
GLC3
, Theorem 16.1.2]
that the functor
{\mathbb{L}}_{G}
admits a left adjoint, to be denoted
{\mathbb{L}}_{G}^{L}
.
Passing to the left adjoints in (
1.1
), we obtain a commutative diagram

(1.2)

\begin{CD}\operatorname{Whit}^{!}(G)_{\operatorname{Ran}}@<{\operatorname{CS}^{-1}_{G}}<{\sim}<{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{G}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}\\
@V{\operatorname{Poinc}_{G,!}[-2\delta_{N_{\rho(\omega_{X})}}]}V{}V@V{}V{\operatorname{Loc}^{\operatorname{spec}}_{\check{G}}}V\\
\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})@<{{\mathbb{L}}_{G}^{L}}<{}<{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}}).\end{CD}

1.2.
Langlands functor and Eisenstein series

In this subsection we will summarize the properties of
{\mathbb{L}}_{G}
relevant for this paper that have to do with the
Eisenstein series and constant term functors.

1.2.1.

A key property of the functor
{\mathbb{L}}_{G}
is that it commutes with the Eisenstein functors, i.e., for a parabolic
P

with Levi quotient
M
, the diagram

(1.3)

\begin{CD}\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{M})@>{{\mathbb{L}}_{M}}>{}>{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{M}})\\
@V{\operatorname{Eis}_{!,\operatorname{twk}}}V{}V@V{\operatorname{Eis}^{\operatorname{spec}}}V{}V\\
\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})@>{{\mathbb{L}}_{G}}>{}>{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}})\end{CD}

commutes, where
\operatorname{Eis}_{!,\operatorname{twk}}
is a “tweaked” !-Eisenstein functor, where the
tweak involves a translation along
\operatorname{Bun}_{M}
and a cohomological shift (the precise
details of the tweak are irrelevant for this paper), see
[
GLC3
, Theorem 14.2.2]
.

1.2.2.

Passing to the right adjoints along the vertical arrows in (
1.3
), we obtain
a diagram

(1.4)

\begin{CD}\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{M})@>{{\mathbb{L}}_{M}}>{}>{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{M}})\\
@A{\operatorname{CT}_{*,\operatorname{twk}}}A{}A@A{\operatorname{CT}^{\operatorname{spec}}}A{}A\\
\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})@>{{\mathbb{L}}_{G}}>{}>{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}}),\end{CD}

which
a priori
commutes up to a natural transformation (in the above diagram,

\operatorname{CT}_{*,\operatorname{twk}}
is a “tweaked” Constant Term functor, set to be the right adjoint of

\operatorname{Eis}_{!,\operatorname{twk}}
).

However, one of the main results of the paper
[
GLC3
]
, namely, Theorem 15.1.2 in
loc. cit.
, says
that the natural transformation in (
1.4
) is an isomorphism. I.e., the diagram
(
1.4
) commutes.

1.2.3.

Note that by passing to left adjoints along all arrows in (
1.4
), we obtain a commutative diagram

(1.5)

\begin{CD}\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{M})@<{{\mathbb{L}}^{L}_{M}}<{}<{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{M}})\\
@V{\operatorname{Eis}_{!,\operatorname{twk}}}V{}V@V{\operatorname{Eis}^{\operatorname{spec}}}V{}V\\
\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})@<{{\mathbb{L}}^{L}_{G}}<{}<{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}}).\end{CD}

1.2.4.

Let

\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{Eis}}\subset\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})

be the full subcategory generated by the essential images of the functors
\operatorname{Eis}_{!}
(equivalently,
\operatorname{Eis}_{!,\operatorname{twk}}
) for proper
parabolics,

Let

{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}})_{\operatorname{red}}\subset{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}})

be the full subcategory consisting of objects, set-theoretically supported on the locus

\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{red}}_{\check{G}}\subset\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}}

consisting of
reducible
local systems, i.e.., the union of the images of the (proper) maps

\operatorname{LS}_{\check{P}}\to\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}}

for proper parabolic subgroups.

Combining diagrams (
1.3
) and (
1.5
) we obtain that the functors

{\mathbb{L}}_{G}
and
{\mathbb{L}}_{G}^{L}
send the subcatergories

\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{Eis}}\text{ and }{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}})_{\operatorname{red}}

to one another, thereby inducing a pair of adjoint functors

(1.6)

{\mathbb{L}}_{G,\operatorname{Eis}}:\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{Eis}}\rightleftarrows{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}})_{\operatorname{red}}:{\mathbb{L}}^{L}_{G,\operatorname{Eis}}.

The main result of
[
GLC3
]
, namely, Theorem 17.1.2 in
loc. cit.
says:

Theorem 1.2.5
.

The adjoint functors in (
1.6
) are (mutually inverse) equivalences.

1.3.
The Langlands functor on the cuspidal part

In this subsection we will study the restriction of
{\mathbb{L}}_{G}
to the cuspidal subcategory.
We will show that GLC is equivalent to the statement that the resulting functor

{\mathbb{L}}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}:\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{cusp}}\to{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}})\simeq{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}})

is an equivalence.

1.3.1.

Let

\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{cusp}}:=\left(\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{Eis}}\right)^{\perp}

be the cuspidal subcategory.

Tautologically,
\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{cusp}}
is the intersection of the kernels of the functors

\operatorname{CT}_{*}
(equivalently,
\operatorname{CT}_{*,\operatorname{twk}}
) for all proper parabolics.

1.3.2.

Denote by
{\mathbf{e}}
the tautological embedding

\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{cusp}}\hookrightarrow\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}).

Since
\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{Eis}}
is generated by objects that are compact in
\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
,
the functor
{\mathbf{e}}
admits a left adjoint, to be denoted
{\mathbf{e}}^{L}
.

1.3.3.

Let

\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}}\overset{\jmath}{\hookrightarrow}\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}}

be the embedding of the irreducible locus. We can regard

{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}})
as a full subcategory of
{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}})
of
{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}})

via
\jmath_{*}
, and as such it identifies with

\left({\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}})_{\operatorname{red}}\right)^{\perp}.

Tautologically,

{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}})_{\operatorname{red}}=\operatorname{ker}(\jmath^{*}).

1.3.4.

From the commutation of (
1.4
), we obtain that the functor
{\mathbb{L}}_{G}
sends

\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{cusp}}
to
{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}})
.
Denote the resulting functor by

(1.7)

{\mathbb{L}}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}:\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{cusp}}\to{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}}).

I.e., we have a commutative diagram

(1.8)

\begin{CD}\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})@>{{\mathbb{L}}_{G}}>{}>{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}})\\
@A{{\mathbf{e}}}A{}A@A{}A{\jmath_{*}}A\\
\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{cusp}}@>{{\mathbb{L}}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}}>{}>{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}}).\end{CD}

Note that from the commutation of (
1.3
) we obtain a commutative diagram

(1.9)

\begin{CD}\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})@>{{\mathbb{L}}_{G}}>{}>{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}})\\
@V{{\mathbf{e}}^{L}}V{}V@V{}V{\jmath^{*}}V\\
\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{cusp}}@>{{\mathbb{L}}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}}>{}>{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}}).\end{CD}

1.3.5.

Note also that when we view
\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{cusp}}
and
{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}})

as quotient categories of
\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
and
{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}})
via
{\mathbf{e}}^{L}
and
\jmath^{*}
, respectively, from
the commutation of (
1.5
), we obtain that there exists a well-defined functor

{\mathbb{L}}^{L}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}:{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}})\to\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{cusp}}

that makes the diagram

(1.10)

\begin{CD}\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})@<{{\mathbb{L}}^{L}_{G}}<{}<{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}})\\
@V{{\mathbf{e}}^{L}}V{}V@V{}V{\jmath^{*}}V\\
\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{cusp}}@<{{\mathbb{L}}^{L}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}}<{}<{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}}).\end{CD}

commute.

Taking into account (
1.9
), we obtain that the functors

(1.11)

{\mathbb{L}}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}:\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{cusp}}\rightleftarrows{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}})_{\operatorname{red}}:{\mathbb{L}}^{L}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}

are mutually adjoint, i.e.,

{\mathbb{L}}^{L}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}\simeq({\mathbb{L}}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}})^{L}.

1.3.6.

We claim, however:

Proposition 1.3.7
.

The functor
{\mathbb{L}}^{L}_{G}
sends
{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}})
to
\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{cusp}}
.

Proof.

Follows from
[
GLC2
, Proposition 17.2.2]
. We will supply an alternative argument in Sect.
1.4.8
.

∎

Corollary 1.3.8
.

The following diagram commutes:

(1.12)

\begin{CD}\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})@<{{\mathbb{L}}^{L}_{G}}<{}<{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}})\\
@A{{\mathbf{e}}}A{}A@A{}A{\jmath_{*}}A\\
\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{cusp}}@<{{\mathbb{L}}^{L}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}}<{}<{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}}).\end{CD}

1.3.9.

Taking into account Theorem
1.2.5
, we obtain:

Corollary 1.3.10
.

The functor
{\mathbb{L}}_{G}
is an equivalence if and only if so is the functor
{\mathbb{L}}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}
.

1.4.
Spectral action

In this subsection we will recall another crucial feature of the functor
{\mathbb{L}}_{G}
: its
compatibility with the
{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}})
-actions on the two sides.

1.4.1.

Recall (see e.g.,
[
GLC1
, Theorem 1.2.4]
) that the Hecke action gives rise to an action of the monoidal
category
{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}})
on
\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
.

1.4.2.

We have:

Proposition 1.4.3
.

With respect to the
{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}})
-action on
\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
, the full subcategory

\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{Eis}}\subset\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})

is set-theoretically supported on
\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}}^{\operatorname{red}}
, i.e.,

(1.13)

\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{Eis}}\underset{{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}})}{\otimes}{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}})=0.

This proposition is probably well-known. We will supply a proof for completeness in Sect.
1.4.9
.

1.4.4.

As a formal consequence of Proposition
1.4.3
, we obtain:

Corollary 1.4.5
.

We have an inclusion

(1.14)

\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\underset{{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}})}{\otimes}{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}})\subset\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{cusp}}

as full subcategories of
\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
.

In fact, a stronger assertion is true (to be proved in Sect.
1.5.6
):

Theorem 1.4.6
.

The inclusion (
1.14
) is an equality.

1.4.7.

By the construction of the functor
{\mathbb{L}}_{G}
, it is
{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}})
-linear, where

{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}})
acts on
{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}})
naturally.

Since the symmetric monoidal category
{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}})
is rigid, we obtain that the functor

{\mathbb{L}}_{G}^{L}
is also equipped with a natural
{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}})
-linear structure.

Moreover, the monad

{\mathbb{L}}_{G}^{L}\circ{\mathbb{L}}_{G},

acting on
{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}})
, is
{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}})
-linear.

1.4.8.
Second proof of Proposition
1.3.7

By
{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}})
-linearity, the functor
{\mathbb{L}}^{L}_{G}
sends

{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}})\underset{{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}})}{\otimes}{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}}),

viewed as a full subcategory of
{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}})
, to

\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\underset{{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}})}{\otimes}{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}}),

viewed as a full subcategory of
\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
.

However, the former is tautologically the same as
{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}})
, while the latter is
contained in
\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{cusp}}
by Corollary
1.4.5
.

∎[Proposition
1.3.7
]

1.4.9.
Proof of Proposition
1.4.3

According to
[
BG
]
4
4

4

The paper
[
BG
]
only treats the case of
P=B
. The general case will be treated
in a forthcoming paper of J. Fægerman and A. Hayash.
, the functor
\operatorname{Eis}_{!}
can be factored as a composition

\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{M})\to\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{M})\underset{{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{M}})}{\otimes}{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{P}})\overset{\operatorname{Eis}_{!}^{\operatorname{part.enh}}}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}),

where the functor
\operatorname{Eis}_{!}^{\operatorname{part.enh}}
is
{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}})
-linear.

This implies the required assertion.

∎[Proposition
1.4.3
]

1.5.
Conservativity

In this subsection we recall a crucial result from
[
FR
]
, which says that the functor
{\mathbb{L}}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}

is conservative.

In a sense, this unveils the main reason why GLC holds: that the functor
{\mathbb{L}}_{G}
does not lose
information (in a very coarse sense, by sending some objects to zero).

1.5.1.

We now import the following result from
[
FR
, Theorem A]
(which is a combination of
[
FR
, Theorem B]

and
[
Be2
, Theorem A]
):

Theorem 1.5.2
.

The functor
{\mathbb{L}}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}
is conservative.

Remark 1.5.3
.

Note that in the case when
G=GL_{n}
, the assertion of Theorem
1.5.2
follows immediately
from (the much more elementary) Theorem
1.7.2
below.

1.5.4.

We now claim:

Theorem 1.5.5
.

The functor
{\mathbb{L}}_{G}
is conservative.

Proof.

The follows immediately by combining Theorems
1.5.2
and
1.2.5
.

∎

1.5.6.
Proof of Theorem
1.4.6

The assertion of the theorem is equivalent to

(1.15)

\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{cusp}}\underset{{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}})}{\otimes}{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}})_{\operatorname{red}}=0,

where
{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}})_{\operatorname{red}}=\operatorname{ker}(\jmath^{*})
.

By Theorem
1.5.2
, it suffices to show that the functor
{\mathbb{L}}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}
annihilates the subcategory
(
1.15
). However,
{\mathbb{L}}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}
sends this category to

{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}})\underset{{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}})}{\otimes}{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}})_{\operatorname{red}},

and the latter is obviously
0
.

∎[Theorem
1.4.6
]

1.6.
The algebra
{\mathcal{A}}_{G,\operatorname{irred}}

In this subsection we will introduce an object

{\mathcal{A}}_{G,\operatorname{irred}}\in\operatorname{AssocAlg}({\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}}),

which encodes the monad
{\mathbb{L}}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}\circ{\mathbb{L}}^{L}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}
.

We will show that the validity of GLC is equivalent to the fact that the unit map (
1.16
)
is an isomorphism.

The proof of GLC that will be presented in the sequel to this paper will amount to the showing that the algebraic
geometry and topology of
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}}
essentially force this map to be an isomorphism
(modulo a certain computation on the automorphic side).

1.6.1.

Consider the monad
{\mathbb{L}}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}\circ{\mathbb{L}}^{L}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}
on
{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}})
corresponding to the adjoint functors
(
1.11
). By Sect.
1.4.7
, this monad is
{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}})
-linear.

Since the action of
{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}})
on
{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}})
factors through

\jmath^{*}:{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}})\to{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}}),

we obtain that
{\mathbb{L}}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}\circ{\mathbb{L}}^{L}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}
is
{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}})
-linear.

1.6.2.

Note that
{\operatorname{Nilp}}|_{\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}}}=\{0\}
, so

{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}})={\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}}).

Hence, the monad
{\mathbb{L}}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}\circ{\mathbb{L}}^{L}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}
is a
{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}})
-linear
monad on
{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}})
itself, and thus corresponds to a unital associative algebra,
to be denoted

{\mathcal{A}}_{G,\operatorname{irred}}\in{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}}).

1.6.3.

The unit of the
({\mathbb{L}}^{L}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}},{\mathbb{L}}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}})
-adjunction corresponds to the unit map

(1.16)

{\mathcal{O}}_{\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}}}\to{\mathcal{A}}_{G,\operatorname{irred}}.

Tautologically, the functor
{\mathbb{L}}^{L}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}
is fully faithful if and only if the map (
1.16
)
is an isomorphism.

1.6.4.

Given Theorem
1.5.2
and Corollary
1.3.10
, we obtain:

Corollary 1.6.5
.

The functor
{\mathbb{L}}_{G}
is an equivalence if and only if the map (
1.16
) is an isomorphism in

{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}})
.

1.7.
Proof of GLC for
G=GL_{n}

In this subsection, we will show how Theorem
1.2.5
allows us to prove GLC in the case when
G=GL_{n}
.

1.7.1.

The point of departure is the following result, established in
[
Be1
]
(or, in a slightly different language, in
[
Ga2
]
):

Theorem 1.7.2
.

The restriction of the functor
\operatorname{coeff}_{G}
to the subcategory

\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{cusp}}\subset\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})

is fully faithful.

1.7.3.

From Theorem
1.7.2
we will now deduce:

Corollary 1.7.4
.

The functor
{\mathbb{L}}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}
is fully faithful.

Proof.

From (
1.1
) and (
1.8
), we obtain a commutative diagram

\begin{CD}\operatorname{Whit}^{!}(G)_{\operatorname{Ran}}@>{\operatorname{CS}_{G}}>{\sim}>{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{G}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}\\
@A{{\operatorname{coeff}_{G}}[2\delta_{N_{\rho(\omega_{X})}}]}A{}A@A{}A{\Gamma^{\operatorname{spec},{\operatorname{IndCoh}}}_{\check{G}}}A\\
\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})@>{{\mathbb{L}}_{G}}>{}>{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}})\\
@A{{\mathbf{e}}}A{}A@A{}A{\jmath_{*}}A\\
\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{cusp}}@>{{\mathbb{L}}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}}>{}>{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}}).\end{CD}

It is sufficient to show that the composite right vertical arrow in the above diagram is fully faithful. Indeed,
this would imply that the fully-faithfulness of the functors

\operatorname{coeff}_{G}[2\delta_{N_{\rho(\omega_{X})}}]\circ{\mathbf{e}}\text{ and }{\mathbb{L}}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}

are logically equivalent.

Note that since

{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}})={\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}}),

the right vertical arrow in the above diagram can be identified with

(1.17)

{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}})\overset{\jmath_{*}}{\hookrightarrow}{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}})\overset{\Gamma^{\operatorname{spec}}_{\check{G}}}{\longrightarrow}{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{G}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}.

Now, in the composition (
1.17
) both arrows are fully faithful: this is obvious for

\jmath_{*}
, and for
\Gamma^{\operatorname{spec}}_{\check{G}}
this is the content of Proposition
1.1.4
.

∎

Remark 1.7.5
.

Note that the proof of Corollary
1.7.4
shows that it is actually logically equivalent to
Theorem
1.7.2
. Hence, once we establish GLC, we will know that
Theorem
1.7.2
also holds for any
G
.

1.7.6.

By Corollary
1.6.5
, in order to prove GLC, we need to show that the map (
1.16
)
is an isomorphism in
{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}})
. Since
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}}
is eventually
coconnective, it is sufficient to show that for any field-valued point

\sigma:\operatorname{Spec}(K)\to\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}},

the resulting map

(1.18)

K\to{\mathcal{A}}_{G,\sigma}

is an isomorphism, where
{\mathcal{A}}_{G,\sigma}
denotes the fiber of
{\mathcal{A}}_{G,\operatorname{irred}}
at
\sigma
.

Applying base change to the functor
{\mathbb{L}}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}
along
\sigma
, we obtain a functor

{\mathbb{L}}_{G,\sigma}:\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{cusp}}\underset{{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}})}{\otimes}\operatorname{Vect}_{K}\to\operatorname{Vect}_{K}.

Since the functor
{\mathbb{L}}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}
is fully faithful and admits a left adjoint, we obtain that
{\mathbb{L}}_{G,\sigma}
is also
fully faithful. In particular,
{\mathbb{L}}_{G,\sigma}
is conservative.
Hence, by Barr-Beck, it can be identified with the forgetful functor

{\mathcal{A}}_{G,\sigma}{\operatorname{-mod}}\to\operatorname{Vect}_{K}.

Such a functor can be fully faithful either when (
1.18
) is an isomorphism (which is what we want to show), or when

{\mathcal{A}}_{G,\sigma}=0
. Thus, it remains to rule out the latter possibility.

1.7.7.

We need to show that the category

\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\sigma}:=\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{cusp}}\underset{{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}})}{\otimes}\operatorname{Vect}_{K}

is non-zero. For this, we can further replace
K
by its algebraic closure.

Performing base change
k\rightsquigarrow K
, we can assume that
K=k
. Then the category
\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\sigma}

is, by definition, the category of
Hecke eigen-sheaves
with respect to
\sigma
.

However, it was shown in
[
FGV
]
that for
G=GL_{n}
and
\sigma
irreducible, the category
\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\sigma}
contains a non-zero object.

Remark 1.7.8
.

Alternatively, the proof of the existence of a non-zero Hecke eigensheaf for a given irreducible local system, valid for any
G
,
follows by combining the
[
BD1
]
construction of Hecke eigensheaves via localization at the critical level and the result of
[
Ari
]
,
which says that any irreducible local system carries a generic oper structure.

∎[GLC for
G=GL_{n}
]

2.
Left adjoint as the dual

In this section we will establish the ”first half” of the Ambidexterity Theorem, namely that the functor
left adjoint

to
{\mathbb{L}}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}
is, up to a certain twist, is canonically isomorphic to its dual.

In order to do so, we will first have to show that the source and the target of
{\mathbb{L}}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}
are canonically self-dual.

2.1.
The dual automorphic category

In this subsection we recall, following
[
DG
]
or
[
Ga1
]
, the description of the dual of the category
\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
.

2.1.1.

Recall the category
\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}
. It is defined as the
colimit

\underset{U}{\operatorname{colim}}\,\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(U),

where
U
runs over the poset of quasi-compact open substacks of
\operatorname{Bun}_{G}
, and for

U_{1}\overset{j_{1,2}}{\to}U_{2}

the corresponding functor

\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(U_{1})\to\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(U_{2})

is
(j_{1,2})_{*}
.

For a given quasi-compact open

(2.1)

U\overset{j}{\hookrightarrow}\operatorname{Bun}_{G},

we let

j_{\operatorname{co},*}:\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(U)\to\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}

denote the tautological functor.

2.1.2.

The category
\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}
is endowed with a tautologically defined functor

\operatorname{Ps-Id}^{\operatorname{nv}}:\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}\to\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}),

characterized by the following property:

For a quasi-compact open as in (
2.1
), we have

(2.2)

\operatorname{Ps-Id}^{\operatorname{nv}}\circ j_{\operatorname{co},*}\simeq j_{*},

as functors

\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(U)\to\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}).

2.1.3.

Note that Verdier duality on
\operatorname{Bun}_{G}
gives rise to a canonical identification

(2.3)

\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})^{\vee}\overset{{\mathbf{D}}^{\operatorname{Verdier}}}{\simeq}\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}.

It is characterized by the requirement that for (
2.1
), we have

j_{co,*}\simeq(j^{*})^{\vee},

where we identify

\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(U)^{\vee}\simeq\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(U)

via usual Verdier duality, also denoted
{\mathbf{D}}^{\operatorname{Verdier}}
.

2.2.
The dual of the cuspidal category

In this subsection we will use Sect.
2.1
to show that the cuspidal automorphic category is canonically self-dual.

2.2.1.

Let

\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co,Eis}}\subset\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}

be the full subcategory, generated by the essential images of the functors

\operatorname{Eis}_{\operatorname{co},*}:\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{M})_{\operatorname{co}}\to\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}

(see
[
Ga1
, Sect. 1.4]
) for
proper
parabolic subgroups.

Set

\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co,cusp}}:=\left(\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co,Eis}}\right)^{\perp}.

Let

{\mathbf{e}}_{\operatorname{co}}:\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co,cusp}}\hookrightarrow\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}

denote the tautological embedding. It admits a
left adjoint
, making
\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co,cusp}}

into a
localization
of
\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}
.

2.2.2.

The identification (
2.3
) gives rise to an identification

(2.4)

\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{cusp}}^{\vee}\overset{{\mathbf{D}}^{\operatorname{Verdier}}_{\operatorname{cusp}}}{\simeq}\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co,cusp}},

so that

{\mathbf{e}}_{\operatorname{co}}\simeq({\mathbf{e}}^{L})^{\vee}\text{ and }{\mathbf{e}}^{L}_{\operatorname{co}}\simeq{\mathbf{e}}^{\vee}.

2.2.3.

Recall now that the category
\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co,cusp}}
has the following property
(see
[
Ga1
, Proposition 2.3.4]
): there exists a quasi-compact open substack

U_{0}\overset{j_{0}}{\hookrightarrow}\operatorname{Bun}_{G},

such that the functor

{\mathbf{e}}_{\operatorname{co}}:\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co,cusp}}\to\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}

factors as

(j_{0})_{\operatorname{co,*}}\circ{\mathbf{e}}_{U_{0},\operatorname{co}},

for (an automatically uniquely defined fully faithful functor)

{\mathbf{e}}_{U_{0},\operatorname{co}}:\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co,cusp}}\to\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(U_{0}).

2.2.4.

Furthermore, according to
[
Ga1
, Theorem 2.2.7]
, the functor
\operatorname{Ps-Id}^{\operatorname{nv}}
sends

\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co,cusp}}
to
\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{cusp}}
, and the resulting functor

\operatorname{Ps-Id}^{\operatorname{nv}}_{\operatorname{cusp}}:\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co,cusp}}\to\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{cusp}}

is an equivalence.

We automatically have

(2.5)

{\mathbf{e}}\circ\operatorname{Ps-Id}^{\operatorname{nv}}_{\operatorname{cusp}}\simeq(j_{0})_{*}\circ{\mathbf{e}}_{U_{0},\operatorname{co}}.

2.2.5.

Thus, combining (
2.4
) with the equivalence
\operatorname{Ps-Id}^{\operatorname{nv}}_{\operatorname{cusp}}
we obtain a self-duality

(2.6)

\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{cusp}}^{\vee}\overset{\operatorname{Ps-Id}^{\operatorname{nv}}_{\operatorname{cusp}}\circ{\mathbf{D}}^{\operatorname{Verdier}}_{\operatorname{cusp}}}{\simeq}\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{cusp}}.

2.2.6.

For later use we introduce the following notation:

Let

(2.7)

{\mathbf{e}}_{U_{0}}:\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{cusp}}\to\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(U_{0})

be the uniquely defined functor, so that

{\mathbf{e}}_{U_{0},\operatorname{co}}\simeq{\mathbf{e}}_{U_{0}}\circ\operatorname{Ps-Id}^{\operatorname{nv}}_{\operatorname{cusp}}.

The functor
{\mathbf{e}}_{U_{0}}
is automatically fully faithful and

{\mathbf{e}}\simeq(j_{0})_{*}\circ{\mathbf{e}}_{U_{0}}.

Let
{\mathbf{e}}^{L}_{U_{0}}
denote the left adjoint of
{\mathbf{e}}_{U_{0}}
. We have:

(2.8)

{\mathbf{e}}^{L}\simeq{\mathbf{e}}^{L}_{U_{0}}\circ j_{0}^{*}.

2.3.
Duality and the Poincaré functors

In this section we will recall the result of
[
Lin
]
that says that the !- and *-versions of the geometric Poincaré functor become
isomorphic, once we project to the cuspidal automorphic category.

2.3.1.

Recall (see
[
GLC2
, Sect. 9.5]
) that in addition to the functor

\operatorname{Poinc}_{G,!}:\operatorname{Whit}^{!}(G)_{\operatorname{Ran}}\to\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}),

there exists a naturally defined functor

\operatorname{Poinc}_{G,*}:\operatorname{Whit}_{*}(G)_{\operatorname{Ran}}\to\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}.

2.3.2.

By construction, with respect the duality (
2.3
) and the canonical duality

(2.9)

(\operatorname{Whit}^{!}(G)_{\operatorname{Ran}})^{\vee}\simeq\operatorname{Whit}_{*}(G)_{\operatorname{Ran}},

we have

(2.10)

(\operatorname{coeff}_{G})^{\vee}\simeq\operatorname{Poinc}_{G,*}.

2.3.3.

Recall now (see
[
GLC2
, Theorem 1.3.13]
) that, in addition to the duality (
2.9
), there exists a canonical
equivalence

(2.11)

\Theta_{\operatorname{Whit}(G)}:\operatorname{Whit}_{*}(G)_{\operatorname{Ran}}\simeq\operatorname{Whit}^{!}(G)_{\operatorname{Ran}}.

The following assertion is a “baby” version of the main result of
[
Lin
]
(see Lemma 1.3.4 in
loc. cit.
):

Theorem 2.3.4
.

The functors

{\mathbf{e}}^{L}\circ\operatorname{Poinc}_{G,!}\circ\Theta_{\operatorname{Whit}(G)}\text{ and }{\mathbf{e}}^{L}\circ\operatorname{Ps-Id}^{\operatorname{nv}}\circ\operatorname{Poinc}_{G,*}[2\delta_{N_{\rho(\omega_{X})}}],

\operatorname{Whit}_{*}(G)_{\operatorname{Ran}}\rightrightarrows\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{cusp}}

are canonically isomorphic.

2.4.
Self-duality on the spectral side

In this short subsection we set up our conventions regarding the self-duality of the category
{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}})
.

2.4.1.

Let us identify
{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}})
with its own dual via the
naive duality

(2.12)

{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}})^{\vee}\overset{{\mathbf{D}}^{\operatorname{naive}}}{\simeq}{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}}).

I.e., the corresponding anti-self equivalence of

{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}})^{c}={\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}})^{\operatorname{perf}}

is given by monoidal dualization.

2.4.2.

The self-duality (
2.12
) induces a self-duality

(2.13)

{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}})^{\vee}\overset{{\mathbf{D}}^{\operatorname{naive}}}{\simeq}{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}}).

2.4.3.

Recall now (see, e.g.,
[
AGKRRV1
, Sect. 11.3]
) that the canonical self-duality on
{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{G}})
gives rise to a self-duality of the category

{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{G}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}
.

2.4.4.

We have:

Lemma 2.4.5
.

With respect to the above self-dualities of
{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}})
and
{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{G}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}
, the
functors

\operatorname{Loc}_{\check{G}}^{\operatorname{spec}}:{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{G}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}\leftrightarrow{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}}):\Gamma^{\operatorname{spec}}_{\check{G}}

identify with each other’s duals:

(\operatorname{Loc}_{\check{G}}^{\operatorname{spec}})^{\vee}\simeq\Gamma^{\operatorname{spec}}_{\check{G}}\text{ and }(\Gamma^{\operatorname{spec}}_{\check{G}})^{\vee}\simeq\operatorname{Loc}_{\check{G}}^{\operatorname{spec}}.

2.5.
Left adjoint vs dual

In this subsection we finally formulate and prove the main result of this section, Theorem
2.5.3
, which says that
the left adjoint and the dual of
{\mathbb{L}}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}
are isomorphic, up to a twist.

2.5.1.

Consider the functor
dual
to
{\mathbb{L}}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}

{\mathbb{L}}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}^{\vee}:{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}}^{\operatorname{irred}})^{\vee}\to\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{cusp}}^{\vee}.

Using the identifications (
2.13
) and (
2.6
), we can think of
{\mathbb{L}}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}^{\vee}
as a functor

(2.14)

{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}}^{\operatorname{irred}})\to\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{cusp}}.

Let

\Phi_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}:{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}}^{\operatorname{irred}})\to\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{cusp}}

denote the composition of the functor (
2.14
) with the Chevalley involution and the shift
[-2\delta_{N_{\rho(\omega_{X})}}]
, i.e.,

\Phi_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}:=\tau_{G}\circ{\mathbb{L}}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}^{\vee}[-2\delta_{N_{\rho(\omega_{X})}}].

2.5.2.

We are going to prove:

Theorem 2.5.3
.

The functor
\Phi_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}
identifies canonically with
{\mathbb{L}}^{L}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}
.

This theorem is a particular case of
[
GLC3
, Theorem 16.2.3]
, and its proof is much simpler in that it only uses
Theorem
2.3.4
, rather than the full force of the result from
[
Lin
]
. We will supply a proof for the sake of
completeness, and it occupies the rest of this subsection.

2.5.4.

By Proposition
1.1.4
, it suffices to establish an isomorphism

{\mathbb{L}}^{L}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}\circ\jmath^{*}\circ\operatorname{Loc}^{\operatorname{spec}}_{\check{G}}\simeq\Phi_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}\circ\jmath^{*}\circ\operatorname{Loc}^{\operatorname{spec}}_{\check{G}}

as functors

{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{G}})\rightrightarrows\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{cusp}}.

We will do so by showing that both diagrams

(2.15)

\begin{CD}\operatorname{Whit}^{!}(G)_{\operatorname{Ran}}@<{\operatorname{CS}^{-1}_{G}}<{\sim}<{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{G}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}\\
@V{\operatorname{Poinc}_{G,!}[-2\delta_{N_{\rho(\omega_{X})}}]}V{}V@V{}V{\operatorname{Loc}^{\operatorname{spec}}_{\check{G}}}V\\
\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})&&{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}})\\
@V{{\mathbf{e}}^{L}}V{}V@V{}V{\jmath^{*}}V\\
\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{cusp}}@<{{\mathbb{L}}^{L}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}}<{}<{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}})\end{CD}

and

(2.16)

\begin{CD}\operatorname{Whit}^{!}(G)_{\operatorname{Ran}}@<{\operatorname{CS}^{-1}_{G}}<{}<{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{G}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}\\
@V{\operatorname{Poinc}_{G,!}[-2\delta_{N_{\rho(\omega_{X})}}]}V{}V@V{}V{\operatorname{Loc}^{\operatorname{spec}}_{\check{G}}}V\\
\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})&&{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}})\\
@V{{\mathbf{e}}^{L}}V{}V@V{}V{\jmath^{*}}V\\
\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{cusp}}@<{\Phi_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}}<{}<{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}})\end{CD}

commute.

2.5.5.

The commutation of (
2.15
) is immediate from (
1.2
) and
(
1.10
). Thus, it remains to
deal with (
2.16
).

First, according to
[
GLC2
, Lemma 1.4.12]
, we have

\tau_{G}\circ\operatorname{CS}^{-1}_{G}\simeq\Theta_{\operatorname{Whit}(G)}\circ\operatorname{CS}_{G}^{\vee}.

Combining with Theorem
2.3.4
, this allows us to replace (
2.16
) by

(2.17)

\begin{CD}\operatorname{Whit}_{*}(G)_{\operatorname{Ran}}@<{\operatorname{CS}^{\vee}_{G}}<{}<{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{G}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}\\
@V{\operatorname{Poinc}_{G,*}}V{}V@V{}V{\operatorname{Loc}^{\operatorname{spec}}_{\check{G}}}V\\
\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}&&{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}})\\
@V{{\mathbf{e}}^{L}\circ\operatorname{Ps-Id}^{\operatorname{nv}}}V{}V@V{}V{\jmath^{*}}V\\
\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{cusp}}@<{{\mathbb{L}}^{\vee}_{G,\operatorname{irred}}[-2\delta_{N_{\rho(\omega_{X})}}]}<{}<{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}}).\end{CD}

2.5.6.

Using

{\mathbf{e}}^{L}\circ\operatorname{Ps-Id}^{\operatorname{nv}}\simeq\operatorname{Ps-Id}^{\operatorname{nv}}_{\operatorname{cusp}}\circ{\mathbf{e}}^{\vee},

we can rewrite (
2.17
) as

\begin{CD}\operatorname{Whit}_{*}(G)_{\operatorname{Ran}}@<{\operatorname{CS}^{\vee}_{G}}<{}<{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{G}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}\\
@V{\operatorname{Poinc}_{G,*}}V{}V@V{}V{\operatorname{Loc}^{\operatorname{spec}}_{\check{G}}}V\\
\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}&&{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}})\\
@V{\operatorname{Ps-Id}^{\operatorname{nv}}_{\operatorname{cusp}}\circ{\mathbf{e}}^{\vee}}V{}V@V{}V{\jmath^{*}}V\\
\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{cusp}}@<{{\mathbb{L}}^{\vee}_{G,\operatorname{irred}}[-2\delta_{N_{\rho(\omega_{X})}}]}<{}<{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}}),\end{CD}

and further by

(2.18)

\begin{CD}\operatorname{Whit}_{*}(G)_{\operatorname{Ran}}@<{\operatorname{CS}^{\vee}_{G}}<{}<{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{G}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}\\
@V{\operatorname{Poinc}_{G,*}}V{}V@V{}V{\operatorname{Loc}^{\operatorname{spec}}_{\check{G}}}V\\
\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}&&{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}})\\
@V{{\mathbf{e}}^{\vee}}V{}V@V{}V{\jmath^{*}}V\\
\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co,cusp}}@<{{\mathbb{L}}^{\vee}_{G,\operatorname{irred}}[-2\delta_{N_{\rho(\omega_{X})}}]}<{}<{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}}),\end{CD}

where we now think of
{\mathbb{L}}^{\vee}_{G,\operatorname{irred}}
as a functor

{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}})\to\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co,cusp}}

via (
2.13
) and (
2.4
).

2.5.7.

Passing to the dual functors in (
2.18
), we obtain that it is equivalent to

\begin{CD}\operatorname{Whit}_{*}(G)_{\operatorname{Ran}}@>{\operatorname{CS}_{G}}>{}>{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{G}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}\\
@A{\operatorname{coeff}_{G}}A{}A@A{}A{\Gamma^{\operatorname{spec}}_{\check{G}}}A\\
\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})&&{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}})\\
@A{{\mathbf{e}}}A{}A@A{}A{\jmath_{*}}A\\
\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{cusp}}@>{{\mathbb{L}}_{G,\operatorname{irred}}[-2\delta_{N_{\rho(\omega_{X})}}]}>{}>{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}}).\end{CD}

However, the commutativity of the latter diagram follows from (
1.1
) and (
1.8
).

∎[Theorem
2.5.3
]

3.
Right adjoint as the dual

In this section we will assume that
G
is semi-simple
5
5

5

This assumption is just a convenience. The statement holds
for any reductive
G
, just the proof would involve slightly more notation.
.

We will prove the “second half” of the ambidexterity theorem, namely, that the functor
right adjoint
to
{\mathbb{L}}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}

is isomorphic to the (twist of) the dual of
{\mathbb{L}}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}
.

The full Ambidexterity Theorem says that the left and right adjoints of
{\mathbb{L}}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}
are isomorphic. Of course, this statement

a posteriori
follows from GLC, but in the current strategy, it constitutes a step in its proof.

3.1.
The ambidexterity statement

3.1.1.

We continue to regard the categories
\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{cusp}}
and
{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}})
as self-dual
via the identifications (
2.13
) and (
2.6
), respectively.

Recall the functor
\Phi_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}
, see Sect.
2.5.1
. We will prove:

Theorem 3.1.2
.

The functor
\Phi_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}
identifies canonically with the right adjoint of
{\mathbb{L}}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}
.

3.1.3.

Before we prove the theorem, let us draw some consequences. First, by combining Theorems
2.5.3
and
3.1.2
,
we obtain what we call the Ambidexterity Theorem:

Main Theorem 3.1.4
.

The left and right adjoints of
{\mathbb{L}}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}
are isomorphic.

Corollary 3.1.5
.

The endofunctor

{\mathbb{L}}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}\circ{\mathbb{L}}^{L}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}

of
{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}})
is isomorphic to its own left and right adjoint.

3.1.6.

Recall (see Sect.
1.6.2
) that the functor
{\mathbb{L}}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}\circ{\mathbb{L}}^{L}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}

is given by tensoring with the associative algebra object

{\mathcal{A}}_{G,\operatorname{irred}}\in{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}}).

From Corollary
3.1.5
we obtain:

Corollary 3.1.7
.

The object
{\mathcal{A}}_{G,\operatorname{irred}}\in{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}})
is self-dual. In particular, it
belongs to
{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}})^{\operatorname{perf}}
, i.e., it is compact.

Eventually we will prove an even more precise version of the second part of the above corollary (see Sect.
4.4
):

Main Theorem 3.1.8
.

The object
{\mathcal{A}}_{G,\operatorname{irred}}\in{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}})
is a classical vector bundle, which is equipped
with a naturally defined flat connection
6
6

6

Note that Corollary
4.2.5
and
Proposition
4.2.8
imply that the resulting local system on
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}}
has a finite monodromy
.

Remark 3.1.9
.

Note that it makes sense to talk about classical vector bundles on
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}}
, since,
under the assumption that
{\check{G}}
is semi-simple, the stack
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}}
is classical and
smooth.

3.1.10.

The rest of this section is devoted to the proof of Theorem
3.1.2
.

3.2.
Critical localization

In this subsection, we will show that the right adjoint of the critical localization functor (functor
\operatorname{Loc}_{G}
below)
is
essentially
isomorphic to its dual, once we restrict to the cuspidal automorphic category.

3.2.1.

Let
\operatorname{Loc}_{G}
be the functor

\operatorname{KL}(G)_{{\operatorname{crit}},{\operatorname{Ran}}}\to\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})

of
[
GLC2
, Sect. 14.1.4]
.

3.2.2.

Denote by
\operatorname{Loc}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}
the composite functor

\operatorname{KL}(G)_{{\operatorname{crit}},{\operatorname{Ran}}}\overset{\operatorname{Loc}_{G}}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\overset{{\mathbf{e}}^{L}}{\to}\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{cusp}}.

We have the following counterpart of Proposition
1.1.4
:

Proposition 3.2.3
.

The functor
\operatorname{Loc}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}
is Verdier quotient.

Proof.

Let
U_{0}
be as in Sect.
2.2.3
. Denote

\operatorname{Loc}_{G,U_{0}}:=j_{0}^{*}\circ\operatorname{Loc}_{G},\quad\operatorname{KL}(G)_{{\operatorname{crit}},{\operatorname{Ran}}}\to\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(U_{0}),

so that

(3.1)

\operatorname{Loc}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}\simeq{\mathbf{e}}_{U_{0}}^{L}\circ\operatorname{Loc}_{G,U_{0}}

where
{\mathbf{e}}^{L}_{U_{0}}
is the left adjoint of the functor
{\mathbf{e}}_{U_{0}}
of (
2.7
).

It is known that for any quasi-compact
U
, the corresponding functor
\operatorname{Loc}_{G,U}
is a Verdier quotient
(see
[
GLC2
, Theorem 13.4.2]
). Now, the assertion follows from (
3.1
), since
{\mathbf{e}}_{U_{0}}^{L}
is also a Verdier quotient.

∎

3.2.4.

Recall (see
[
GLC2
, Sect. 2.2.4]
) that the category
\operatorname{KL}(G)_{{\operatorname{crit}},{\operatorname{Ran}}}
is also canonically self-dual.
Thus, using the self-duality of
\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{cusp}}
given by (
2.6
),
we can regard the dual of
\operatorname{Loc}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}
as a functor

(3.2)

\operatorname{Loc}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}^{\vee}:\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{cusp}}\to\operatorname{KL}(G)_{{\operatorname{crit}},{\operatorname{Ran}}}.

The following assertion is a counterpart of Lemma
2.4.5
:

Proposition 3.2.5
.

We have a canonical identification between
\operatorname{Loc}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}^{\vee}
and

\operatorname{Loc}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}^{R}\otimes\operatorname{det}(\Gamma(X,{\mathcal{O}}_{X})\otimes{\mathfrak{g}})[\delta_{G}],

where
\delta_{G}=\dim(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
.

Proof.

Let
\operatorname{Loc}_{G,U_{0}}
be as in the proof of Proposition
3.2.3
. It follows formally that we have the identifications

\operatorname{Loc}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}^{\vee}\simeq\operatorname{Loc}_{G,U_{0}}^{\vee}\circ{\mathbf{e}}_{U_{0}}\text{ and }\operatorname{Loc}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}^{R}\simeq\operatorname{Loc}_{G,U_{0}}^{R}\circ{\mathbf{e}}_{U_{0}}.

Thus, in order to prove Proposition
3.2.5
, it suffices to show that for a quasi-compact
U\subset\operatorname{Bun}_{G}
,
with respect to the Verdier self-duality of
\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(U)
, we have

(3.3)

\operatorname{Loc}_{G,U}^{\vee}\simeq\operatorname{Loc}_{G,U}^{R}\otimes\operatorname{det}(\Gamma(X,{\mathcal{O}}_{X})\otimes{\mathfrak{g}})[\delta_{G}]

as functors

\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(U)\to\operatorname{KL}(G)_{{\operatorname{crit}},{\operatorname{Ran}}}.

Denote by
\operatorname{Loc}_{G,{\operatorname{crit}}}
the composition of
\operatorname{Loc}_{G}
with the identification

\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\simeq\operatorname{D-mod}_{\operatorname{crit}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})

of
[
GLC2
, Equation (10.2)]
, and similarly for
\operatorname{Bun}_{G}
replaced by
U
.

In terms of the self-duality of
\operatorname{D-mod}_{\operatorname{crit}}(U)
specified in
[
GLC2
, Sect. 10.5.2]
, we can reformulate
(
3.3
) as

(3.4)

\operatorname{Loc}_{G,{\operatorname{crit}},U}^{\vee}\simeq\operatorname{Loc}_{G,{\operatorname{crit}},U}^{R}.

Note that by the definition of the functor
\operatorname{Loc}_{G,{\operatorname{crit}}}
in
[
GLC2
, Sect. 11.3.3-11.3.4]
,
the functor
\operatorname{Loc}_{G,{\operatorname{crit}},U}^{R}
is the functor
\Gamma_{G,{\operatorname{crit}}}\circ j_{\operatorname{co},*}
, where

\Gamma_{G,{\operatorname{crit}}}:\operatorname{D-mod}_{\operatorname{crit}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}\to\operatorname{KL}(G)_{{\operatorname{crit}},{\operatorname{Ran}}}

is the functor of
[
GLC2
, Sect. 10.2.5]
.

We have:

\operatorname{Loc}_{G,{\operatorname{crit}},U}^{\vee}\simeq\operatorname{Loc}_{G,{\operatorname{crit}}}^{\vee}\circ(j_{*})^{\vee}=\operatorname{Loc}_{G,{\operatorname{crit}}}^{\vee}\circ j_{\operatorname{co},*}.

Hence, in order to prove (
3.4
) it remains to identify

\operatorname{Loc}_{G,{\operatorname{crit}}}^{\vee}\simeq\Gamma_{G,{\operatorname{crit}}}.

However, the latter is the statement of
[
GLC2
, Proposition 10.5.7(b)]
.

∎

3.3.
The spectral Poincaré functor

In this section we will show that the right adjoint of the spectral Poincaré functor (functor
\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{spec}}_{{\check{G}},*}
below)
is
essentially
isomorphic to its dual, once we restrict to the locus of irreducible local systems.

NB: it is in this subsection that the assumption that
G
(rather
{\check{G}}
) is semi-simple is
used
7
7

7

In fact, it is used twice, and one can show that the two usages cancel each other out. We just chose
not to go through this exercise.
.

3.3.1.

Let
