\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{spec}}_{{\check{G}},*}:{\operatorname{IndCoh}}^{*}({\operatorname{Op}}^{\operatorname{mon-free}}_{\check{G}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}\to{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}})

be the functor of
[
GLC2
, Sect. 17.4.2]
.

Denote by
\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{spec}}_{{\check{G}},*,\operatorname{irred}}
the composite functor

{\operatorname{IndCoh}}^{*}({\operatorname{Op}}^{\operatorname{mon-free}}_{\check{G}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}\overset{\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{spec}}_{{\check{G}},*}}{\longrightarrow}{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}})\overset{\jmath^{*}}{\to}{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}}).

3.3.2.

Recall now (see
[
GLC2
, Sect. 3.2]
) that in addition to
{\operatorname{IndCoh}}^{*}({\operatorname{Op}}^{\operatorname{mon-free}}_{\check{G}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}
, we can consider the category

{\operatorname{IndCoh}}^{!}({\operatorname{Op}}^{\operatorname{mon-free}}_{\check{G}})_{\operatorname{Ran}},

and we have a canonical identification

(3.5)

({\operatorname{IndCoh}}^{*}({\operatorname{Op}}^{\operatorname{mon-free}}_{\check{G}})_{\operatorname{Ran}})^{\vee}\overset{{\mathbf{D}}^{\operatorname{Serre}}}{\simeq}{\operatorname{IndCoh}}^{!}({\operatorname{Op}}^{\operatorname{mon-free}}_{\check{G}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}.

.

In addition, we have an identification

\Theta_{{\operatorname{Op}}^{\operatorname{mon-free}}({\check{G}})}:{\operatorname{IndCoh}}^{!}({\operatorname{Op}}^{\operatorname{mon-free}}_{\check{G}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}\to{\operatorname{IndCoh}}^{*}({\operatorname{Op}}^{\operatorname{mon-free}}_{\check{G}})_{\operatorname{Ran}},

see
[
GLC2
, Equation (3.21)]
.

Composing we obtain a datum of self-duality:

(3.6)

{\operatorname{IndCoh}}^{*}({\operatorname{Op}}^{\operatorname{mon-free}}_{\check{G}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\vee}\overset{\Theta_{{\operatorname{Op}}^{\operatorname{mon-free}}({\check{G}})}\circ{\mathbf{D}}^{\operatorname{Serre}}}{\simeq}{\operatorname{IndCoh}}^{*}({\operatorname{Op}}^{\operatorname{mon-free}}_{\check{G}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}.

3.3.3.

We are going to prove:

Proposition 3.3.4
.

With respect to the self-dualities (
3.6
) and (
2.13
), we have a canonical
identification between the functor
dual
to
\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{spec}}_{{\check{G}},*,\operatorname{irred}}
and

(\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{spec}}_{{\check{G}},*,\operatorname{irred}})^{R}\otimes{\mathfrak{l}}_{\operatorname{Kost}}[-\delta_{G}],

where
{\mathfrak{l}}_{\operatorname{Kost}}
is the line of
[
GLC2
, Sect. 17.2.2]
.

The rest of this subsection is devoted to the proof of Proposition
3.3.4
.

3.3.5.

Recall (see
[
GLC2
, Sect. 17.4.1]
)
that in addition to the functor
\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{spec}}_{{\check{G}},*}
, there exists a functor

\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{spec}}_{{\check{G}},!}:{\operatorname{IndCoh}}^{!}({\operatorname{Op}}^{\operatorname{mon-free}}_{\check{G}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}\to{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}}).

Denote

\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{spec}}_{{\check{G}},!,\operatorname{irred}}:=\jmath^{*}\circ\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{spec}}_{{\check{G}},!}.

According to
[
GLC2
, Theorem 17.4.7]
, we have:

\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{spec}}_{{\check{G}},!}\otimes{\mathfrak{l}}_{\operatorname{Kost}}[-\delta_{G}]\simeq\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{spec}}_{{\check{G}},*}\circ\Theta_{{\operatorname{Op}}^{\operatorname{mon-free}}({\check{G}})}.

Hence, the assertion of the proposition can be reformulated as an isomorphism

(3.7)

(\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{spec}}_{{\check{G}},!,\operatorname{irred}})^{\vee}\simeq(\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{spec}}_{{\check{G}},*,\operatorname{irred}})^{R}[2\delta_{G}],

as functors

{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}})\rightrightarrows{\operatorname{IndCoh}}^{*}({\operatorname{Op}}^{\operatorname{mon-free}}_{\check{G}})_{\operatorname{Ran}},

where we regard
(\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{spec}}_{{\check{G}},!,\operatorname{irred}})^{\vee}
as a functor

{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}})\overset{\text{\eqref{e:naive self-dual QCoh irred}}}{\longrightarrow}{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}})^{\vee}\to{\operatorname{IndCoh}}^{!}({\operatorname{Op}}^{\operatorname{mon-free}}_{\check{G}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\vee}\simeq{\operatorname{IndCoh}}^{*}({\operatorname{Op}}^{\operatorname{mon-free}}_{\check{G}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}.

To simplify the notation, we will prove a variant of (
3.7
), where instead of the entire
{\operatorname{Ran}}
we worked at a fixed
point
\underline{x}\in{\operatorname{Ran}}
. I.e., we will prove

(3.8)

(\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{spec}}_{{\check{G}},!,\underline{x},\operatorname{irred}})^{\vee}\simeq(\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{spec}}_{{\check{G}},*,\underline{x},\operatorname{irred}})^{R}[2\delta_{G}]

as functors

{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}})\rightrightarrows{\operatorname{IndCoh}}^{*}({\operatorname{Op}}^{\operatorname{mon-free}}_{{\check{G}},\underline{x}}),

3.3.6.

Recall that

\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{spec}}_{{\check{G}},*,\underline{x}}\text{ and }\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{spec}}_{{\check{G}},!,\underline{x}}

are given by

(\pi_{\underline{x}})^{{\operatorname{IndCoh}}}_{*}\circ(s_{\underline{x}})^{{\operatorname{IndCoh}},*}\text{ and }(\pi_{\underline{x}})^{{\operatorname{IndCoh}}}_{*}\circ(s_{\underline{x}})^{!},

respectively, for the morphisms

{\operatorname{Op}}^{\operatorname{mon-free}}_{{\check{G}},\underline{x}}\overset{s_{\underline{x}}}{\leftarrow}{\operatorname{Op}}^{\operatorname{mon-free}}_{\check{G}}(X-\underline{x})\overset{\pi_{\underline{x}}}{\to}\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}},

see
[
GLC2
, Sects. 17.4.1 and 17.4.2]
.

Let

\operatorname{coeff}^{\operatorname{spec}}_{{\check{G}},\underline{x}}:{\operatorname{IndCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}})\to{\operatorname{IndCoh}}^{*}({\operatorname{Op}}^{\operatorname{mon-free}}_{{\check{G}},\underline{x}})

denote the functor

(s_{\underline{x}})^{{\operatorname{IndCoh}}}_{*}\circ(\pi_{\underline{x}})^{!}.

For future use, we also introduce the notation for the Ran version of this functor

\operatorname{coeff}^{\operatorname{spec}}_{\check{G}}:{\operatorname{IndCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}})\to{\operatorname{IndCoh}}^{*}({\operatorname{Op}}^{\operatorname{mon-free}}_{\check{G}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}.

3.3.7.

Let

(3.9)

{\operatorname{IndCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}})^{\vee}\overset{{\mathbf{D}}^{\operatorname{Serre}}}{\to}{\operatorname{IndCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}})

be the identification, given by
Serre duality
.

Note that with respect to identifications (
3.9
) and (
3.5
),
we have

(3.10)

(\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{spec}}_{{\check{G}},!,\underline{x}})^{\vee}\simeq\operatorname{coeff}^{\operatorname{spec}}_{{\check{G}},\underline{x}}.

3.3.8.

Due to the assumption that
G
(and hence
{\check{G}}
) is semi-simple, the stack
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}}
is smooth, so the natural embedding

{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}})\hookrightarrow{\operatorname{IndCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}})

is an equivalence.

In particular, the identification (
3.9
) induces an identification

(3.11)

{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}})^{\vee}\overset{{\mathbf{D}}^{\operatorname{Serre}}}{\to}{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}}).

From (
3.10
) we obtain

(\jmath^{*}\circ\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{spec}}_{{\check{G}},!,\underline{x}})^{\vee}\simeq\operatorname{coeff}^{\operatorname{spec}}_{{\check{G}},\underline{x}}\circ\jmath_{*}=:\operatorname{coeff}^{\operatorname{spec}}_{{\check{G}},\underline{x},\operatorname{irred}},

as functors

{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}})\rightrightarrows{\operatorname{IndCoh}}^{*}({\operatorname{Op}}^{\operatorname{mon-free}}_{{\check{G}},\underline{x}}),

where we use (
3.11
) to identify
{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}})
with its own dual.

3.3.9.

Note now that since
{\check{G}}
is semi-simple, the Killing form on
{\check{{\mathfrak{g}}}}
defines a canonical symplectic structure on

\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}}
. Hence,

{\mathbf{D}}^{\operatorname{Serre}}\simeq{\mathbf{D}}^{\operatorname{naive}}[\dim(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}}^{\operatorname{irred}}(X))]={\mathbf{D}}^{\operatorname{naive}}[2\delta_{G}].

Hence, (
3.8
) becomes equivalent to an isomorphism

(3.12)

\operatorname{coeff}^{\operatorname{spec}}_{{\check{G}},\underline{x},\operatorname{irred}}\simeq(\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{spec}}_{{\check{G}},*,\underline{x},\operatorname{irred}})^{R}.

3.3.10.

Thus, we have to establish an adjunction between

\jmath^{*}\circ(\pi_{\underline{x}})^{{\operatorname{IndCoh}}}_{*}\circ(s_{\underline{x}})^{*}\text{ and }(s_{\underline{x}})^{{\operatorname{IndCoh}}}_{*}\circ(\pi_{\underline{x}})^{!}\circ\jmath_{*}.

Since the functors
((s_{\underline{x}})^{{\operatorname{IndCoh}},*},(s_{\underline{x}})^{{\operatorname{IndCoh}}}_{*})
form an adjoint pair, it suffices to establish an adjunction between

\jmath^{*}\circ(\pi_{\underline{x}})^{{\operatorname{IndCoh}}}_{*}\text{ and }(\pi_{\underline{x}})^{!}\circ\jmath_{*}.

3.3.11.

Set

{\operatorname{Op}}^{\operatorname{mon-free,irred}}_{\check{G}}(X-\underline{x}):={\operatorname{Op}}^{\operatorname{mon-free}}_{\check{G}}(X-\underline{x})\underset{\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}}}{\times}\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}}.

Let
\pi^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\underline{x}}
denote the resulting morphism

{\operatorname{Op}}^{\operatorname{mon-free,irred}}_{\check{G}}(X-\underline{x})\to\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}}.

By base change, the required adjunction is equivalent to an adjunction between

{\operatorname{IndCoh}}({\operatorname{Op}}^{\operatorname{mon-free,irred}}_{\check{G}}(X-\underline{x}))\overset{(\pi^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\underline{x}})^{{\operatorname{IndCoh}}}_{*}}{\longrightarrow}{\operatorname{IndCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}})

and

{\operatorname{IndCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}})\overset{(\pi^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\underline{x}})^{!}}{\longrightarrow}{\operatorname{IndCoh}}({\operatorname{Op}}^{\operatorname{mon-free,irred}}_{\check{G}}(X-\underline{x})).

However, this follows from the fact that, under the assumption that
{\check{G}}
is semi-simple, the morphism
\pi^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\underline{x}}

is ind-proper. Indeed, the generic non-degeneracy condition for opers
is automatic, once the underlying local system is irreducible.

∎[Proposition
3.3.4
]

3.4.
Proof of Theorem
3.1.2

This proof will amount to comparing the commutative diagrams obtained from the diagram expressing
the compatibility of
{\mathbb{L}}_{G}
with critical localization (diagram (
3.14
) below)
by passage to right adjoint and dual functors, respectively.

3.4.1.

By Proposition
3.2.3
, in order to construct an isomorphism

({\mathbb{L}}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}})^{R}\simeq\Phi_{G,\operatorname{cusp}},

it suffices to establish an isomorphism

(3.13)

(\operatorname{Loc}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}})^{\vee}\circ({\mathbb{L}}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}})^{R}\simeq(\operatorname{Loc}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}})^{\vee}\circ\Phi_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}.

3.4.2.

Recall that according to
[
GLC2
, Theorem 18.5.2]
, we have the following commutative diagram:

(3.14)

\begin{CD}\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})@>{{\mathbb{L}}_{G}}>{}>{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}})\\
@A{\operatorname{Loc}_{G}\otimes{\mathfrak{l}}}A{}A@A{}A{\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{spec}}_{{\check{G}},*}}A\\
\operatorname{KL}(G)_{{\operatorname{crit}},{\operatorname{Ran}}}@>{\operatorname{FLE}_{G,{\operatorname{crit}}}}>{}>{\operatorname{IndCoh}}^{*}({\operatorname{Op}}^{\operatorname{mon-free}}_{\check{G}})_{\operatorname{Ran}},\end{CD}

where :

•

\operatorname{FLE}_{G,{\operatorname{crit}}}
is the
critical FLE functor
of
[
GLC2
, Equation (6.7)]
;

•

{\mathfrak{l}}
is the comologically graded line

{\mathfrak{l}}^{\otimes\frac{1}{2}}_{G,N_{\rho(\omega_{X})}}\otimes{\mathfrak{l}}^{\otimes-1}_{N_{\rho(\omega_{X})}}[-\delta_{N_{\rho(\omega_{X})}}],

where:

–

{\mathfrak{l}}^{\otimes\frac{1}{2}}_{G,N_{\rho(\omega_{X})}}
is the (non-graded) line of
[
GLC2
, Equation (9.7)]
;

–

{\mathfrak{l}}_{N_{\rho(\omega_{X})}}
is the (non-graded) line of
[
GLC2
, Equation (14.2)]
;

–

\delta_{N_{\rho(\omega_{X})}}=\operatorname{dim}(\operatorname{Bun}_{N_{\rho(\omega_{X})}})
.

3.4.3.

Concatenating diagrams (
3.14
) and (
1.9
), we obtain a commutative diagram

(3.15)

\begin{CD}\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{cusp}}@>{{\mathbb{L}}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}}>{}>{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}})\\
@A{\operatorname{Loc}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}\otimes{\mathfrak{l}}}A{}A@A{}A{\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{spec}}_{{\check{G}},*,\operatorname{irred}}}A\\
\operatorname{KL}(G)_{{\operatorname{crit}},{\operatorname{Ran}}}@>{\operatorname{FLE}_{G,{\operatorname{crit}}}}>{}>{\operatorname{IndCoh}}^{*}({\operatorname{Op}}^{\operatorname{mon-free}}_{\check{G}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}.\end{CD}

Passing to the right adjoints in (
3.15
) we obtain a diagram

(3.16)

\begin{CD}\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{cusp}}@<{({\mathbb{L}}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}})^{R}}<{}<{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}})\\
@V{\operatorname{Loc}^{R}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}}V{}V@V{}V{(\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{spec}}_{{\check{G}},*,\operatorname{irred}})^{R}\otimes{\mathfrak{l}}}V\\
\operatorname{KL}(G)_{{\operatorname{crit}},{\operatorname{Ran}}}@<{\operatorname{FLE}_{G,{\operatorname{crit}}}^{-1}}<{}<{\operatorname{IndCoh}}^{*}({\operatorname{Op}}^{\operatorname{mon-free}}_{\check{G}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}.\end{CD}

We will establish (
3.13
) by showing that the diagram

(3.17)

\begin{CD}\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{cusp}}@<{\Phi_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}}<{}<{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}})\\
@V{\operatorname{Loc}^{R}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}}V{}V@V{}V{(\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{spec}}_{{\check{G}},*,\operatorname{irred}})^{R}\otimes{\mathfrak{l}}}V\\
\operatorname{KL}(G)_{{\operatorname{crit}},{\operatorname{Ran}}}@<{\operatorname{FLE}_{G,{\operatorname{crit}}}^{-1}}<{}<{\operatorname{IndCoh}}^{*}({\operatorname{Op}}^{\operatorname{mon-free}}_{\check{G}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}\end{CD}

commutes as well.

3.4.4.

Consider the diagram obtained by passing to the duals in (
3.15
):

(3.18)

\begin{CD}\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{cusp}}@<{({\mathbb{L}}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}})^{\vee}}<{}<{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}})\\
@V{\operatorname{Loc}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}^{\vee}\otimes{\mathfrak{l}}}V{}V@V{}V{(\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{spec}}_{{\check{G}},*,\operatorname{irred}})^{\vee}}V\\
\operatorname{KL}(G)_{{\operatorname{crit}},{\operatorname{Ran}}}@<{\operatorname{FLE}_{G,{\operatorname{crit}}}^{\vee}}<{}<{\operatorname{IndCoh}}^{!}({\operatorname{Op}}^{\operatorname{mon-free}}_{\check{G}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}\end{CD}

Recall now that according to
[
GLC2
, Theorem 8.1.4]
, we have a canonical identification

\operatorname{FLE}_{G,{\operatorname{crit}}}^{\vee}\simeq\tau_{G}\circ\operatorname{FLE}_{G,{\operatorname{crit}}}^{-1}\circ\Theta_{{\operatorname{Op}}^{\operatorname{mon-free}}({\check{G}})}

as functors

{\operatorname{IndCoh}}^{!}({\operatorname{Op}}^{\operatorname{mon-free}}_{\check{G}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}\to\operatorname{KL}(G)_{{\operatorname{crit}},{\operatorname{Ran}}}.

Combining with Proposition
3.3.4
, we can rewrite (
3.18
) as

(3.19)

\begin{CD}\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{cusp}}@<{\tau_{G}\circ({\mathbb{L}}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}})^{\vee}}<{}<{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}})\\
@V{\operatorname{Loc}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}^{\vee}\otimes{\mathfrak{l}}}V{}V@V{}V{(\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{spec}}_{{\check{G}},*,\operatorname{irred}})^{R}\otimes{\mathfrak{l}}_{\operatorname{Kost}}^{\otimes-1}[\delta_{G}]}V\\
\operatorname{KL}(G)_{{\operatorname{crit}},{\operatorname{Ran}}}@<{\operatorname{FLE}_{G,{\operatorname{crit}}}^{-1}}<{}<{\operatorname{IndCoh}}^{*}({\operatorname{Op}}^{\operatorname{mon-free}}_{\check{G}})_{\operatorname{Ran}},\end{CD}

and further as

(3.20)

\begin{CD}\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{cusp}}@<{\Phi_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}}<{}<{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}})\\
@V{\operatorname{Loc}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}^{\vee}[2\delta_{N_{\rho(\omega_{X})}}]}V{}V@V{}V{(\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{spec}}_{{\check{G}},*,\operatorname{irred}})^{R}\otimes{\mathfrak{l}}^{\otimes-1}\otimes{\mathfrak{l}}_{\operatorname{Kost}}^{\otimes-1}[\delta_{G}]}V\\
\operatorname{KL}(G)_{{\operatorname{crit}},{\operatorname{Ran}}}@<{\operatorname{FLE}_{G,{\operatorname{crit}}}^{-1}}<{}<{\operatorname{IndCoh}}^{*}({\operatorname{Op}}^{\operatorname{mon-free}}_{\check{G}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}.\end{CD}

Taking into account Proposition
3.2.5
, we can further rewrite (
3.20
) as

(3.21)

\begin{CD}\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{cusp}}@<{\Phi_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}}<{}<{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Nilp}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}})\\
@V{\operatorname{Loc}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}^{R}[2\delta_{N_{\rho(\omega_{X})}}]}V{}V@V{}V{(\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{spec}}_{{\check{G}},*,\operatorname{irred}})^{R}\otimes{\mathfrak{l}}^{\otimes-1}\otimes{\mathfrak{l}}_{\operatorname{Kost}}^{\otimes-1}\otimes\operatorname{det}(\Gamma(X,{\mathcal{O}}_{X})\otimes{\mathfrak{g}})^{\otimes-1}}V\\
\operatorname{KL}(G)_{{\operatorname{crit}},{\operatorname{Ran}}}@<{\operatorname{FLE}_{G,{\operatorname{crit}}}^{-1}}<{}<{\operatorname{IndCoh}}^{*}({\operatorname{Op}}^{\operatorname{mon-free}}_{\check{G}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}.\end{CD}

3.4.5.

Comparing (
3.21
) with the desired diagram (
3.17
), we
conclude that it establish the isomorphism

{\mathfrak{l}}_{\operatorname{Kost}}\otimes\operatorname{det}(\Gamma(X,{\mathcal{O}}_{X})\otimes{\mathfrak{g}})^{\otimes-1}\simeq({\mathfrak{l}}^{\otimes\frac{1}{2}}_{G,N_{\rho(\omega_{X})}})^{\otimes 2}\otimes{\mathfrak{l}}_{N_{\rho(\omega_{X})}}^{\otimes-2}.

However, the latter is given by
[
GLC3
, Proposition 15.1.10]
.

∎[Theorem
3.1.2
]

3.5.
An addendum: ambidexterity for eigensheaves

The contents of this subsection will not be used elsewhere in the paper. Here we will explain another
approach to ambidexterity, albeit so far working only for Hecke eigensheaves (or more generally
D-modules with nilpotent singular support, see Remark
3.5.9
).

3.5.1.

Fix a point
\sigma\in\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}}
, and let

\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\sigma}:=\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\underset{{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}})}{\otimes}\operatorname{Vect}

be the corresponding category of Hecke eigensheaves, where

{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}})\to\operatorname{Vect}

is the functor of *-fiber of
\sigma
, to be denoted
(i_{\sigma})^{*}
.

Note that since
\sigma
was assumed irreducible, the forgetful functor

(3.22)

\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\sigma}\overset{{\mathbf{oblv}}_{\sigma}}{\to}\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})

lands in
\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{cusp}}
, see Corollary
1.4.5
.

3.5.2.

The functor
{\mathbb{L}}_{G}
induces a functor

{\mathbb{L}}_{G,\sigma}:\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\sigma}\to\operatorname{Vect}.

According to Theorem
3.1.4
, the left and rights adjoints of
{\mathbb{L}}_{G,\sigma}
are (canonically) isomorphic.
In this subsection we will exhibit another way of constructing such an isomorphism
8
8

8

However, it is is not
obvious that the isomorphism we will construct in this subsection is the same as one from Theorem
3.1.4
.
.

3.5.3.

Note that by construction, the functor
{\mathbb{L}}_{G,\sigma}
is isomorphic to the composition

\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\sigma}\overset{{\mathbf{oblv}}_{\sigma}}{\to}\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\overset{\operatorname{coeff}_{G}^{\operatorname{Vac,glob}}}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Vect},

where
\operatorname{coeff}_{G}^{\operatorname{Vac,glob}}
is as in
[
GLC2
, Sect. 9.6.3]
.

The left adjoint of
{\mathbb{L}}_{G,\sigma}
, denoted
{\mathbb{L}}^{L}_{G,\sigma}
sends the generator
k\in\operatorname{Vect}
to
the object

(i_{\sigma})^{*}(\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{Vac,glob}}_{G,!}),

where:

•

(i_{\sigma})^{*}
denotes the functor

\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\simeq\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\underset{{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}})}{\otimes}{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}})\overset{\operatorname{id}\otimes(i_{\sigma})^{*}}{\longrightarrow}\\
\to\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\underset{{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}})}{\otimes}\operatorname{Vect}=\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\sigma},

left adjoint to the forgetful functor
{\mathbf{oblv}}_{\sigma}
;

•

\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{Vac,glob}}_{G,!}\in\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
is the object from
[
GLC1
, Sect. 1.3]
.

3.5.4.

Thus, we wish to construct a canonical isomorphism

(3.23)

{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\sigma}}({\mathcal{F}},{\mathbb{L}}^{L}_{G,\sigma}({\mathsf{V}}))\simeq{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{\operatorname{Vect}}(\operatorname{coeff}_{G}^{\operatorname{Vac,glob}}\circ{\mathbf{oblv}}_{\sigma}({\mathcal{F}}),{\mathsf{V}})

for
{\mathcal{F}}\in\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\sigma}
and
{\mathsf{V}}\in\operatorname{Vect}
.

We will rewrite both sides of (
3.23
) and show that they are canonically isomorphic.

3.5.5.

Using Theorem
2.3.4
, we rewrite
{\mathbb{L}}^{L}_{G,\sigma}({\mathsf{V}})
as

(3.24)

(i_{\sigma})^{*}(\operatorname{Ps-Id}^{\operatorname{nv}}(\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{Vac,glob}}_{G,*}))\otimes{\mathsf{V}},

where

\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{Vac,glob}}_{G,*}:={\mathbb{D}}^{\operatorname{Verdier}}(\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{Vac,glob}}_{G,!})[-2\delta_{N_{\rho(\omega_{X})}}]\in\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}.

Since
\sigma
is a smooth point of
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}}
, we have

(i_{\sigma})^{*}\simeq(i_{\sigma})^{!}[\dim(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}})]\otimes\operatorname{det}(T^{*}_{\sigma}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}})),

where
i_{\sigma}:{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}}^{\operatorname{irred}})\to\operatorname{Vect}
is the functor of !-pullback, which is defined
for maps of finite Tor-dimension, and is the the
right
adjoint of
(i_{\sigma})_{*}
, since the map

i_{\sigma}:\operatorname{pt}\to\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}}
is proper.

In particular, the functor

\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\simeq\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\underset{{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}})}{\otimes}{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}})\overset{\operatorname{id}\otimes(i_{\sigma})^{!}}{\longrightarrow}\\
\to\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\underset{{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}})}{\otimes}\operatorname{Vect}=\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\sigma}

is the right adjoint of
{\mathbf{oblv}}_{\sigma}
.

Note also that using the symplectic structure on
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}}
, we can trivialize the line
\operatorname{det}(T^{*}_{\sigma}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}}))
,
and we note that
\dim(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}})=2\dim(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
.

Combining, we obtain that the left-hand side in (
3.23
) identifies with

(3.25)

{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})}({\mathbf{oblv}}_{\sigma}({\mathcal{F}}),\operatorname{Ps-Id}^{\operatorname{nv}}(\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{Vac,glob}}_{G,*})\otimes{\mathsf{V}})[2\dim(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})].

3.5.6.

Denote

{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}:={\mathbf{oblv}}_{\sigma}({\mathcal{F}}).

We rewrite (
3.25
) using Verdier duality as

(3.26)

{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{\operatorname{Vect}}(\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{c}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G},{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}\overset{*}{\otimes}\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{Vac,glob}}_{G,!}),{\mathsf{V}})[2\dim(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})+2\delta_{N_{\rho(\omega_{X})}}].

And we rewrite the right-hand side of (
3.23
) as

(3.27)

{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{\operatorname{Vect}}(\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G},{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}\overset{!}{\otimes}\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{Vac,glob}}_{G,*}),{\mathsf{V}})[2\delta_{N_{\rho(\omega_{X})}}].

Hence, in order to establish (
3.23
), we need to construct an isomorphism

(3.28)

\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{c}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G},{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}\overset{*}{\otimes}\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{Vac,glob}}_{G,!})[-2\dim(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})]\simeq\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G},{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}\overset{!}{\otimes}\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{Vac,glob}}_{G,*}).

3.5.7.

By the main theorem of
[
Lin
]
, we have

\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{Vac,glob}}_{G,!}[-2\dim(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})]\simeq\operatorname{Mir}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}(\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{Vac,glob}}_{G,*}).

Now (
3.28
) follows from the fact that
{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}
has nilpotent singular support (see
[
AGKRRV1
, Corollary 14.4.10]
)
combined with the next general assertion from
[
AGKRRV2
, Theorem 3.4.2]
:

Theorem 3.5.8
.

For any
{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}\in\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
and any
{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime\prime}\in\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{co}}
,
there is a canonical isomorphism

\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{c}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G},{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}\overset{*}{\otimes}\operatorname{Mir}_{\operatorname{Bun}_{G}}({\mathcal{F}}^{\prime\prime}))\simeq\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G},{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}\overset{!}{\otimes}{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime\prime}).

Remark 3.5.9
.

The above argument can be generalized so that it proves ambidexterity for the functor induced by
{\mathbb{L}}_{G}

\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{cusp}}\to{\operatorname{IndCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred,restr}}_{\check{G}}),

where

\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{cusp}}:=\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2},{\operatorname{Nilp}}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})\cap\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{cusp}}

and

\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred,restr}}_{\check{G}}:=\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\check{G}}\cap\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}}.

Note that
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred,restr}}_{\check{G}}
is a disjoint union of formal schemes, each of which is isomorphic to
the formal completion of a point in a smooth symplectic scheme of dimension
2\dim(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})
.

4.
The expression for
{\mathcal{A}}_{G,\operatorname{irred}}
via opers

In this section we will prove that the object
{\mathcal{A}}_{G,\operatorname{irred}}\in{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}})
can be expressed
via opers.

This will lead to a number of structural results concerning
{\mathcal{A}}_{G,\operatorname{irred}}
, as well as the space of generic oper
structures on irreducible local systems.

Furthermore, given the recent result of
[
BKS
]
, we will deduce GLC for classical groups.

4.1.
Statement of the result

4.1.1.

Consider the space
{\operatorname{Op}}^{\operatorname{mon-free}}_{\check{G}}(X^{\operatorname{gen}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}
fibered over
{\operatorname{Ran}}
, whose fiber over

\underline{x}\in{\operatorname{Ran}}
is

{\operatorname{Op}}^{\operatorname{mon-free}}_{\check{G}}(X-\underline{x}).

Let
\pi_{\operatorname{Ran}}
denote the resulting map

{\operatorname{Op}}^{\operatorname{mon-free}}_{\check{G}}(X^{\operatorname{gen}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}\to\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}}.

Set

{\operatorname{Op}}^{\operatorname{mon-free,irred}}_{\check{G}}(X^{\operatorname{gen}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}:={\operatorname{Op}}^{\operatorname{mon-free}}_{\check{G}}(X^{\operatorname{gen}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}\underset{\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}}}{\times}\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}}.

Let
\pi^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}
denote the resulting map

{\operatorname{Op}}^{\operatorname{mon-free,irred}}_{\check{G}}(X^{\operatorname{gen}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}\to\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}}.

Note that the morphism
\pi^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}
is pseudo-proper, i.e., a (not necessarily filtered) colimit of proper maps,
see Sect.
3.3.11
.

4.1.2.

Consider the object
9
9

9

The superscript “Op” in the notation below refers to opers and
not
to the opposite algebra structure.

{\mathcal{B}}^{{\operatorname{Op}}}_{G,\operatorname{irred}}:={\mathbf{oblv}}^{l}\left((\pi^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\operatorname{Ran}})_{!}(\omega_{{\operatorname{Op}}^{\operatorname{mon-free,irred}}_{\check{G}}(X^{\operatorname{gen}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}})\right)\in{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}}),

where

{\mathbf{oblv}}^{l}:\operatorname{D-mod}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}})\to{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}})

is the “left” forgetful functor, see
[
GaRo1
, Equation (5.3)]
.

By construction,
{\mathcal{B}}^{{\operatorname{Op}}}_{G,\operatorname{irred}}
is a co-commutative coalgebra in
{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}})
.

4.1.3.

Denote by

{\mathbb{L}}^{R}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}:{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}})\to\operatorname{D-mod}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G})_{\operatorname{cusp}}

the functor
right adjoint
to
{\mathbb{L}}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}
.

Since the monoidal category
{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}})
is rigid and the functor
{\mathbb{L}}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}
is

{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}})
-linear, the functor
{\mathbb{L}}^{R}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}
is also naturally

{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}})
-linear.

Hence, the comonad

{\mathbb{L}}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}\circ{\mathbb{L}}^{R}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}

acting on
{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}})
is given by tensoring by a co-associative coalgebra object,
to be denoted
{\mathcal{B}}_{G,\operatorname{irred}}
.

4.1.4.

The main result of this section reads:

Theorem 4.1.5
.

There exists a canonical isomorphism between

{\mathcal{B}}_{G,\operatorname{irred}}\simeq{\mathcal{B}}^{{\operatorname{Op}}}_{G,\operatorname{irred}}

as plain objects of
{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}})
.

Remark 4.1.6
.

One can show that the isomorphism of Theorem
4.1.5
respects the co-associative coalgebra
structures on the two sides. However, we will neither prove
10
10

10

See, however, Remark
4.6.7
.
nor use this.

4.2.
Combining with ambidexterity

Prior to proving Theorem
4.1.5
we will derive some consequences.

4.2.1.

Note that
a priori
, the comonad

{\mathbb{L}}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}\circ{\mathbb{L}}^{R}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}

is the right adjoint
of the monad

{\mathbb{L}}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}\circ{\mathbb{L}}^{L}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}.

Hence, the
coalgebra

{\mathcal{B}}_{G,\operatorname{irred}}
identifies a priori with the monoidal dual of the
algebra

{\mathcal{A}}_{G,\operatorname{irred}}
.

4.2.2.

Combining with Corollary
3.1.7
we obtain:

Corollary 4.2.3
.

There is a canonical isomorphism

{\mathcal{A}}_{G,\operatorname{irred}}\simeq{\mathcal{B}}_{G,\operatorname{irred}}

as objects of
{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}})
.

4.2.4.

Combing further with Theorem
4.1.5
, we obtain:

Corollary 4.2.5
.

There is a canonical isomorphism

{\mathcal{A}}_{G,\operatorname{irred}}\simeq{\mathcal{B}}^{{\operatorname{Op}}}_{G,\operatorname{irred}}

as objects of
{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}})
.

And as a result:

Corollary 4.2.6
.

The object
{\mathcal{B}}^{{\operatorname{Op}}}_{G,\operatorname{irred}}\in{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}})
is compact.

4.2.7.

Consider the object

(4.1)

(\pi^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\operatorname{Ran}})_{!}(\omega_{{\operatorname{Op}}^{\operatorname{mon-free,irred}}_{\check{G}}(X^{\operatorname{gen}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}})\in\operatorname{D-mod}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}}).

Assuming Theorem
3.1.8
for a moment, we obtain that the object (
4.1
) has the form

\omega_{\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}}}\otimes\underline{{\mathcal{B}}}^{{\operatorname{Op}}}_{G,\operatorname{irred}},

where

\underline{{\mathcal{B}}}^{{\operatorname{Op}}}_{G,\operatorname{irred}}
is a
classical local system of finite rank
on
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}}
.

We will prove the following assertion, which would be needed for the final step in the proof of GLC:

Proposition 4.2.8
.

The local system
\underline{{\mathcal{B}}}^{{\operatorname{Op}}}_{G,\operatorname{irred}}
has a finite monodromy, i.e., it trivializes over a finite étale
cover of
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}}
.

4.3.
Proof of Proposition
4.2.8

We will deduce Proposition
4.2.8
from Theorem
4.1.5
.

4.3.1.

Denote

{\mathcal{F}}:=(\pi^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\operatorname{Ran}})_{!}(\omega_{{\operatorname{Op}}^{\operatorname{mon-free,irred}}_{\check{G}}(X^{\operatorname{gen}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}})[-n],

where
11
11

11

The cohomological shift is introduced for the sake of perverse normalization.

n=\dim(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}})
.

Since the map
\pi^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}
is pseudo-proper, this object can be written as

\underset{i\in I}{\operatorname{colim}}\,{\mathcal{F}}_{i}\,\quad{\mathcal{F}}_{i}\in\operatorname{D-mod}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}}^{\operatorname{irred}}),

over some diagram
I
, where each
{\mathcal{F}}_{i}
is of the form

(f_{i})_{*,{\operatorname{dR}}}(\omega_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{i}})[-n],

where
f_{i}:{\mathcal{Y}}_{i}\to\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}}^{\operatorname{irred}}
is a proper map of algebraic stacks.

For each index
i
, consider the Stein factorization of the map
f_{i}

{\mathcal{Y}}_{i}\to{\mathcal{Y}}^{0}_{i}\overset{f^{0}_{i}}{\to}\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}}^{\operatorname{irred}},

so that
f^{0}_{i}
is a finite map.

Denote

{\mathcal{F}}_{i}^{0}:=(f^{0}_{i})_{*,{\operatorname{dR}}}(\omega_{{\mathcal{Y}}^{0}_{i}})[-n].

4.3.2.

Let
\eta
be the generic point of a connected component of
\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}}^{\operatorname{irred}}
. It is enough to show that

{\mathcal{F}}|_{\eta}
has a finite monodromy.

We have

{\mathcal{F}}_{i}|_{\eta}\in\operatorname{D-mod}(\eta)^{\leq 0},\,\,{\mathcal{F}}_{i}^{0}|_{\eta}\in\operatorname{D-mod}(\eta)^{\heartsuit}

and the map

{\mathcal{F}}_{i}\to{\mathcal{F}}_{i}^{0}

induces an isomorphism

H^{0}({\mathcal{F}}_{i}|_{\eta})\to{\mathcal{F}}_{i}^{0}|_{\eta}.

By Theorem
3.1.8
combined with Corollary
4.2.5
, the object

{\mathcal{F}}|_{\eta}
is concentrated in cohomological degree
0
. Hence, we obtain that

{\mathcal{F}}|_{\eta}\simeq\underset{i\in I}{\operatorname{colim}}\,{\mathcal{F}}^{0}_{i}|_{\eta}.

Since
{\mathcal{F}}|_{\eta}
is finite-dimensional, we obtain that
I
contains a finite sub-diagram
I^{f}\subset I
such that the map

\underset{i\in I^{f}}{\oplus}\,{\mathcal{F}}^{0}_{i}|_{\eta}\to{\mathcal{F}}|_{\eta}

is surjective.

Since each
{\mathcal{F}}^{0}_{i}|_{\eta}
has a finite monodromy, we obtain that so does
{\mathcal{F}}|_{\eta}
.

∎[Proposition
4.2.8
]

4.4.
Proof of Theorem
3.1.8

In this subsection we will continue to assume Theorem
4.1.5
, and deduce
Theorem
3.1.8
.

4.4.1.

Since
{\mathcal{A}}_{G,\operatorname{irred}}
is perfect, in order to prove that it is a classical vector bundle,
it suffices to show that the *-fibers of

{\mathcal{A}}_{G,\operatorname{irred}}
at
k
-points of
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}}
are concentrated in cohomological
degree
0
.

By the self-duality assertion in Corollary
3.1.7
, it suffices to show that the *-fibers of

{\mathcal{A}}_{G,\operatorname{irred}}
are concentrated in
non-positive
cohomological degrees.

4.4.2.

By Corollary
4.2.5
, it suffices to show that the *-fibers of

{\mathcal{B}}^{{\operatorname{Op}}}_{G,\operatorname{irred}}
are concentrated in
non-positive
cohomological degrees.

However, the *-fiber of
{\mathcal{B}}^{{\operatorname{Op}}}_{G,\operatorname{irred}}
at a
k
-point
\sigma\in\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}}

is isomorphic to
\operatorname{C}_{\cdot}({\operatorname{Op}}^{\operatorname{gen}}_{{\check{G}},\sigma})
,
where

{\operatorname{Op}}^{\operatorname{gen}}_{{\check{G}},\sigma}:=\{\sigma\}\underset{\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}}}{\times}{\operatorname{Op}}^{\operatorname{mon-free,irred}}_{\check{G}}(X^{\operatorname{gen}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}.

It is automatically concentrated in
non-positive
cohomological degrees, being the
homology
of a prestack.

4.4.3.

The D-module structure on
{\mathcal{A}}_{G,\operatorname{irred}}
comes from the isomorphism of Corollary
4.2.5
.

∎[Theorem
3.1.8
]

4.5.
Contractibility of opers

In this subsection we will continue to assume Theorem
4.1.5
. We will show that the
validity of GLC is equivalent to the contractibility (and, in fact, just connectedness) of the space
of generic oper structures on irreducible local systems.

4.5.1.

Note that in the course of the proof of Theorem
3.1.8
above we have established:

Corollary 4.5.2
.

The homology of the fibers
of the map
\pi^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}
is acyclic off degree
0
.

This can be equivalently reformulated as follows:

Corollary 4.5.3
.

The connected components of the fibers of the map
\pi^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}
are homologically contractible.

4.5.4.

Applying Corollary
1.6.5
, we obtain:

Corollary 4.5.5
.

The following assertions are equivalent:

(i) The functor
{\mathbb{L}}_{G}
is equivalence.

(ii) The fibers of the map
\pi^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}
are connected.

(iii) The fibers of the map
\pi^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}
are
homologically contractible
.

4.5.6.

In particular, we obtain that GLC is equivalent to the following conjecture:

Conjecture 4.5.7
.

The space of
generic oper structures
on a given irreducible local system is homologically contractible.

Remark 4.5.8
.

Note that the “bottom” layer of Conjecture
4.5.7
says that the
space of generic oper structures on a given irreducible local system is
non-empty
.
This statement is actually a theorem, thanks to
[
Ari
]
.

Remark 4.5.9
.

The assertion of Conjecture
4.5.7
is easy for
G=GL_{n}
. In particular, in this way we obtain another proof
of GLC in this case (i.e., one that is logically different from that in Sect.
1.7
)
12
12

12

The difference between
the two arguments is that one uses a fully faithfulness assertion on the automorphic side, another on the spectral side.
.

4.5.10.

Recall now that thanks to
[
BKS
]
, Conjecture
4.5.7
is actually a theorem whenever
G
is a
classical group
13
13

13

Here by a classical group we mean a reductive group whose root datum is of
type A, B, C or D.
. Hence, we obtain:

Main Theorem 4.5.11
.

The geometric Langlands conjecture holds when
G
is a classical group.

Remark 4.5.12
.

Formally speaking, the main theorem of
[
BKS
]
establishes Conjecture
4.5.7
for a slightly different notion
of oper, namely, for
{\check{{\mathfrak{g}}}}
-opers, rather than
{\check{G}}
-opers (and it is the latter that appears in Conjecture
4.5.7
).
In other words,
[
BKS
]
implies Conjecture
4.5.7
not for
{\check{G}}
itself but rather for its adjoint quotient.

However, as we shall see in the sequel to this paper, the statement of GLC for a given pair
(G,{\check{G}})
formally follows
from the case when
{\check{G}}
is replaced by its adjoint quotient (resp.,
G
is replaced by the simply-connected cover
of its derived group).

4.6.
Proof of Theorem
4.1.5

As we shall presently see, the proof of the theorem follows almost immediately from diagram (
3.15
),
once we combine the following pieces of information:

•

The functor
\operatorname{FLE}_{G,{\operatorname{crit}}}
is an equivalence;

•

The functor
{\mathbb{L}}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}
is a Verdier quotient.

4.6.1.

Consider the adjoint pair

(4.2)

\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{spec}}_{{\check{G}},*,\operatorname{irred}}:{\operatorname{IndCoh}}^{*}({\operatorname{Op}}^{\operatorname{mon-free}}_{\check{G}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}\rightleftarrows{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}}):\operatorname{coeff}^{\operatorname{spec}}_{{\check{G}},\operatorname{irred}},

see (
3.12
), where
\operatorname{coeff}^{\operatorname{spec}}_{{\check{G}},\operatorname{irred}}
is the version of the functor

\operatorname{coeff}^{\operatorname{spec}}_{{\check{G}},\underline{x},\operatorname{irred}}
when
\underline{x}
varies in families along
{\operatorname{Ran}}
.

4.6.2.

We will deduce Theorem
4.1.5
from the following assertion, which takes place
purely on the spectral side.

Theorem 4.6.3
.

The comonad on
{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}})
corresponding to (
4.2
)
is given by tensor product with
{\mathcal{B}}^{{\operatorname{Op}}}_{G,\operatorname{irred}}
.

This theorem will be proved in Sect.
5
. Let us assume it, and proceed with the proof
of Theorem
4.1.5
.

4.6.4.

Since we only want to identify

(4.3)

{\mathcal{B}}_{G,\operatorname{irred}}\simeq{\mathcal{B}}^{\operatorname{Op}}_{G,\operatorname{irred}}

as objects of
{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}})

(and not as co-algebras), it suffices to construct an isomorphism of comonads

(4.4)

\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{spec}}_{{\check{G}},*,\operatorname{irred}}\circ(\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{spec}}_{{\check{G}},*,\operatorname{irred}})^{R}\simeq{\mathbb{L}}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}\circ({\mathbb{L}}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}})^{R}

acting on
{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}})
. Indeed, each side of (
4.3
) is obtained by applying
the corresponding side of (
4.4
) to
{\mathcal{O}}_{\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}}}
.

4.6.5.

Since
\operatorname{FLE}_{G,{\operatorname{crit}}}
is an equivalence, the left-hand side in (
4.4
)
identifies with

\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{spec}}_{{\check{G}},*,\operatorname{irred}}\circ\operatorname{FLE}_{G,{\operatorname{crit}}}\circ\operatorname{FLE}_{G,{\operatorname{crit}}}^{R}\circ(\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{spec}}_{{\check{G}},*,\operatorname{irred}})^{R}.

Since
\operatorname{Loc}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}
is a Verdier quotient, the right-hand side in (
4.4
) identifies with

{\mathbb{L}}_{G,\operatorname{irred}}\circ(\operatorname{Loc}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}\otimes{\mathfrak{l}})\circ((\operatorname{Loc}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}\otimes{\mathfrak{l}})^{R}\circ({\mathbb{L}}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}})^{R}.

4.6.6.

Hence, it suffices to establish an isomorphism between the comonads

\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{spec}}_{{\check{G}},*,\operatorname{irred}}\circ\operatorname{FLE}_{G,{\operatorname{crit}}}\circ\operatorname{FLE}_{G,{\operatorname{crit}}}^{R}\circ(\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{spec}}_{{\check{G}},*,\operatorname{irred}})^{R}\simeq\\
\simeq{\mathbb{L}}_{G,\operatorname{irred}}\circ(\operatorname{Loc}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}\otimes{\mathfrak{l}})\circ((\operatorname{Loc}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}\otimes{\mathfrak{l}})^{R}\circ({\mathbb{L}}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}})^{R},

which is the same as

(\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{spec}}_{{\check{G}},*,\operatorname{irred}}\circ\operatorname{FLE}_{G,{\operatorname{crit}}})\circ(\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{spec}}_{{\check{G}},*,\operatorname{irred}}\circ\operatorname{FLE}_{G,{\operatorname{crit}}})^{R}\simeq\\
\simeq({\mathbb{L}}_{G,\operatorname{irred}}\circ(\operatorname{Loc}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}\otimes{\mathfrak{l}}))\circ({\mathbb{L}}_{G,\operatorname{irred}}\circ(\operatorname{Loc}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}\otimes{\mathfrak{l}}))^{R}.

However, this follows formally from the commutativity of (
3.15
).

∎[Theorem
4.1.5
]

Remark 4.6.7
.

Note that Theorem
4.1.5
only says that
{\mathcal{B}}_{G,\operatorname{irred}}
and
{\mathcal{B}}^{{\operatorname{Op}}}_{G,\operatorname{irred}}
are isomorphic
as objects of
{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}})
, but not as co-associative co-algebras. One can upgrade the proof
given above to an isomorphism of coalgebras along the following lines:

It follows from the construction that both comonads in (
4.4
) are linear with respect to
the action of
{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{G}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}
on
{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}})
via the functor
\jmath^{*}\circ\operatorname{Loc}^{\operatorname{spec}}_{\check{G}}
, and the
isomorphism between them constructed above respects this structure.

It also follows from the construction that the
{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}})
-linear structure on

{\mathbb{L}}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}}\circ({\mathbb{L}}_{G,\operatorname{cusp}})^{R}
agrees with the above
{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{G}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}
-linear structure.

The comonad given by tensor product with
{\mathcal{B}}^{{\operatorname{Op}}}_{G,\operatorname{irred}}
has a tautological linear structure with respect to

{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}})
, and hence also with respect to
{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{G}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}
. It follows from the proof
of Theorem
4.6.3
given in the next section that the above
{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{G}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}
-linear structure on
-\otimes{\mathcal{B}}^{{\operatorname{Op}}}_{G,\operatorname{irred}}

agrees with the
{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{G}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}
-linear structure on
\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{spec}}_{{\check{G}},*,\operatorname{irred}}\circ(\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{spec}}_{{\check{G}},*,\operatorname{irred}})^{R}
.

Thus, we obtain that the two comonads

-\otimes{\mathcal{B}}_{G,\operatorname{irred}}\text{ and }-\otimes{\mathcal{B}}^{{\operatorname{Op}}}_{G,\operatorname{irred}}

are identified as
{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{G}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}
-linear comonads. Since the functor
\jmath^{*}\circ\operatorname{Loc}^{\operatorname{spec}}_{\check{G}}
is a Verdier quotient,
this implies that the above identification is automatically
{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}})
-linear. The latter is equivalent to the
identification of
{\mathcal{B}}_{G,\operatorname{irred}}
and
{\mathcal{B}}^{{\operatorname{Op}}}_{G,\operatorname{irred}}
as co-associative coalgebras in
{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}})
.

5.
Proof of Theorem
4.6.3

The rest of the paper is devoted to the proof of Theorem
4.6.3
. In particular,
it takes place purely on the spectral side.

We will break up Theorem
4.6.3
into two assertions: Propositions
5.1.2

and
5.1.5
. The former can be informally phrased as “the Ran integral equates the quasi-coherent
and de Rham direct images”. The latter can be informally phrased as “the Ran integral erases the
difference between the local and the global”.

It turns out that both these assertions are quite general, i.e., have nothing to do with the specifics of
opers or local systems.

5.1.
Strategy of the proof

5.1.1.

Consider the tautological natural transformation

(5.1)

(\pi_{\operatorname{Ran}})^{{\operatorname{IndCoh}}}_{*}\circ{\mathbf{oblv}}_{{\operatorname{Op}}^{\operatorname{mon-free}}_{\check{G}}(X^{\operatorname{gen}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}}^{r}\to{\mathbf{oblv}}^{r}_{\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}}}\circ(\pi_{\operatorname{Ran}})_{*,{\operatorname{dR}}},

as functors

\operatorname{D-mod}({\operatorname{Op}}^{\operatorname{mon-free}}_{\check{G}}(X^{\operatorname{gen}})_{\operatorname{Ran}})\rightrightarrows{\operatorname{IndCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}}).

We will prove

Proposition 5.1.2
.

The natural transformation (
5.1
) is an isomorphism when evaluated on objects
in the essential image of the functor

\pi^{!}_{\operatorname{Ran}}:\operatorname{D-mod}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}})\to\operatorname{D-mod}({\operatorname{Op}}^{\operatorname{mon-free}}_{\check{G}}(X^{\operatorname{gen}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}).

Remark 5.1.3
.

For the validity of Proposition
5.1.2
, it is essential that we work with the entire
{\operatorname{Ran}}

and not a fixed
\underline{x}\in{\operatorname{Ran}}
.

5.1.4.

Let
(s_{\operatorname{Ran}})^{{\operatorname{IndCoh}}}_{*}
denote the functor

{\operatorname{IndCoh}}({\operatorname{Op}}^{\operatorname{mon-free}}_{\check{G}}(X^{\operatorname{gen}})_{\operatorname{Ran}})\to{\operatorname{IndCoh}}^{*}({\operatorname{Op}}^{\operatorname{mon-free}}_{\check{G}})_{\operatorname{Ran}},

and let
(s_{\operatorname{Ran}})^{{\operatorname{IndCoh}},*}
denote its left adjoint.

The counit of the
((s_{\operatorname{Ran}})^{{\operatorname{IndCoh}},*},(s_{\operatorname{Ran}})^{{\operatorname{IndCoh}}}_{*})
-adjunction defines a natural transformation

(5.2)

(\pi_{\operatorname{Ran}})^{{\operatorname{IndCoh}}}_{*}\circ(s_{\operatorname{Ran}})^{{\operatorname{IndCoh}},*}\circ(s_{\operatorname{Ran}})^{{\operatorname{IndCoh}}}_{*}\to(\pi_{\operatorname{Ran}})^{{\operatorname{IndCoh}}}_{*}.

We will prove:

Proposition 5.1.5
.

The natural transformation (
5.2
), composed with the coarsening functor

\Psi_{\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}}}:{\operatorname{IndCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}})\to{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}}),

is an isomorphism, when evaluated on objects
in the essential image of the functor

\pi^{!}_{\operatorname{Ran}}:{\operatorname{IndCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}})\to{\operatorname{IndCoh}}({\operatorname{Op}}^{\operatorname{mon-free}}_{\check{G}}(X^{\operatorname{gen}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}).

5.1.6.

We claim that the combination of Propositions
5.1.2
and
5.1.5
implies
the assertion of Theorem
4.6.3
.

Recall that the morphism
\pi^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}
is pseudo-proper, so we can identify

(\pi^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\operatorname{Ran}})^{!}\simeq((\pi^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\operatorname{Ran}})_{*})^{R}
. Hence, the comonad

\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{spec}}_{{\check{G}},*,\operatorname{irred}}\circ(\operatorname{Poinc}^{\operatorname{spec}}_{{\check{G}},*,\operatorname{irred}})^{R}

identifies with

\jmath^{*}\circ(\pi_{\operatorname{Ran}})^{{\operatorname{IndCoh}}}_{*}\circ(s_{\operatorname{Ran}})^{{\operatorname{IndCoh}},*}\circ(s_{\operatorname{Ran}})^{{\operatorname{IndCoh}}}_{*}\circ(\pi_{\operatorname{Ran}})^{!}\circ\jmath_{*}.

According to Proposition
5.1.5
, this comonad maps isomorphically to the comonad

\jmath^{*}\circ(\pi_{\operatorname{Ran}})^{{\operatorname{IndCoh}}}_{*}\circ(\pi_{\operatorname{Ran}})^{!}\circ\jmath_{*}\simeq(\pi^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\operatorname{Ran}})^{{\operatorname{IndCoh}}}_{*}\circ(\pi^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\operatorname{Ran}})^{!}.

In particular, we obtain that this comonad is obtained by the !-tensor product with the
coalgebra object

(5.3)

(\pi^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\operatorname{Ran}})^{{\operatorname{IndCoh}}}_{*}\circ(\pi^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\operatorname{Ran}})^{!}(\omega_{\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}}})\simeq(\pi^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\operatorname{Ran}})^{{\operatorname{IndCoh}}}_{*}(\omega_{{\operatorname{Op}}^{\operatorname{mon-free,irred}}_{\check{G}}(X^{\operatorname{gen}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}}).

5.1.7.

Restricting along the horizontal arrows in the Cartesian diagram

\begin{CD}{\operatorname{Op}}^{\operatorname{mon-free,irred}}_{\check{G}}(X^{\operatorname{gen}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}@>{\jmath}>{}>{\operatorname{Op}}^{\operatorname{mon-free}}_{\check{G}}(X^{\operatorname{gen}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}\\
@V{\pi^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}}V{}V@V{}V{\pi_{\operatorname{Ran}}}V\\
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}}@>{\jmath}>{}>\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}},\end{CD}

from Proposition
5.1.2
we obtain that the natural transformation

(5.4)

(\pi^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\operatorname{Ran}})^{{\operatorname{IndCoh}}}_{*}\circ{\mathbf{oblv}}_{{\operatorname{Op}}^{\operatorname{mon-free,irred}}_{\check{G}}(X^{\operatorname{gen}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}}^{r}\to{\mathbf{oblv}}^{r}_{\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}}}\circ(\pi^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\operatorname{Ran}})_{*,{\operatorname{dR}}}

is an isomorphism, when evaluated on objects lying in the essential image of

(\pi^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\operatorname{Ran}})^{!}:\operatorname{D-mod}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}})\to\operatorname{D-mod}({\operatorname{Op}}^{\operatorname{mon-free,irred}}_{\check{G}}(X^{\operatorname{gen}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}).

Hence, we obtain that the coalgebra (
5.3
) maps isomorphically to

{\mathbf{oblv}}^{r}_{\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}}}\left((\pi^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\operatorname{Ran}})_{*,{\operatorname{dR}}}\circ(\pi^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\operatorname{Ran}})^{!}(\omega_{\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}}})\right)\simeq{\mathbf{oblv}}^{r}_{\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}}}\circ(\pi^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\operatorname{Ran}})_{*,{\operatorname{dR}}}(\omega_{{\operatorname{Op}}^{\operatorname{mon-free,irred}}_{\check{G}}(X^{\operatorname{gen}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}}).

Finally, we note that

{\mathbf{oblv}}^{r}_{\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}}}(-)\simeq{\mathbf{oblv}}^{l}_{\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}}}(-)\otimes\omega_{\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{irred}}_{\check{G}}}.

∎[Theorem
4.6.3
]

5.2.
Framework for the proof of Proposition
5.1.2

In this subsection we will explain a general framework for the proof of Proposition
5.1.2
: it has to do with
a morphism between D-prestacks over
X
.

5.2.1.

Consider the prestack

({\operatorname{Op}}^{\operatorname{mon-free}}_{\check{G}}(X^{\operatorname{gen}})_{\operatorname{Ran}})_{{\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{rel}}}:=({\operatorname{Op}}^{\operatorname{mon-free}}_{\check{G}}(X^{\operatorname{gen}})_{\operatorname{Ran}})_{\operatorname{dR}}\underset{(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}})_{\operatorname{dR}}}{\times}\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}},

so that

{\operatorname{IndCoh}}(({\operatorname{Op}}^{\operatorname{mon-free}}_{\check{G}}(X^{\operatorname{gen}})_{\operatorname{Ran}})_{{\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{rel}}}),

is the category of
relative
D-modules on
{\operatorname{Op}}^{\operatorname{mon-free}}_{\check{G}}(X^{\operatorname{gen}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}
with respect to the projection

\pi_{\operatorname{Ran}}
.

Denote by

{\mathbf{ind}}^{\operatorname{rel}}:{\operatorname{IndCoh}}({\operatorname{Op}}^{\operatorname{mon-free}}_{\check{G}}(X^{\operatorname{gen}})_{\operatorname{Ran}})\rightleftarrows{\operatorname{IndCoh}}(({\operatorname{Op}}^{\operatorname{mon-free}}_{\check{G}}(X^{\operatorname{gen}})_{\operatorname{Ran}})_{{\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{rel}}}):{\mathbf{oblv}}^{\operatorname{rel}}

the resulting pair of adjoint functors.

Consider also the functors

(5.5)

(\pi_{\operatorname{Ran}})^{{\operatorname{IndCoh}}}_{*,{\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{rel}}}:{\operatorname{IndCoh}}(({\operatorname{Op}}^{\operatorname{mon-free}}_{\check{G}}(X^{\operatorname{gen}})_{\operatorname{Ran}})_{{\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{rel}}})\to{\operatorname{IndCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}})

and

(5.6)

(\pi_{\operatorname{Ran}})^{!}_{{\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{rel}}}:{\operatorname{IndCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}})\to{\operatorname{IndCoh}}(({\operatorname{Op}}^{\operatorname{mon-free}}_{\check{G}}(X^{\operatorname{gen}})_{\operatorname{Ran}})_{{\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{rel}}}).

5.2.2.

As in Sect.
5.1.1
we have a natural transformation

(5.7)

(\pi_{\operatorname{Ran}})^{{\operatorname{IndCoh}}}_{*}\circ{\mathbf{oblv}}^{\operatorname{rel}}\to(\pi_{\operatorname{Ran}})_{*,{\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{rel}}},

as functors

{\operatorname{IndCoh}}(({\operatorname{Op}}^{\operatorname{mon-free}}_{\check{G}}(X^{\operatorname{gen}})_{\operatorname{Ran}})_{{\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{rel}}})\rightrightarrows{\operatorname{IndCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}}).

The assertion of Proposition
5.1.2
follows immediately from the one:

Proposition 5.2.3
.

The natural transformation (
5.7
) is an isomorphism when evaluated on objects
in the essential image of the functor (
5.6
).

In its turn, Proposition
5.2.3
follows from the next assertion:

Proposition 5.2.4
.

The counit of the adjunction

{\mathbf{ind}}^{\operatorname{rel}}\circ{\mathbf{oblv}}^{\operatorname{rel}}\to\operatorname{Id}

is an isomorphism, when evaluated on objects in the essential image of the functor (
5.6
).

5.3.
An abstract version of Proposition
5.2.4

In this subsection we will show that Proposition
5.2.4
is a particular case of a general assertion that has
to do with a morphism
f:{\mathcal{Z}}\to{\mathcal{Y}}
of D-prestacks over
X
.

5.3.1.

Consider the prestack
\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X,{\mathcal{Y}})
of horizontal sections of
{\mathcal{Y}}
, as well as

\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}\text{ and }\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}

that associate to a point
\underline{x}\in{\operatorname{Ran}}
the spaces of horizontal sections of
{\mathcal{Y}}
and
{\mathcal{Z}}

over
X-\underline{x}
, respectively.

Note that we have a tautological map

\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X,{\mathcal{Y}})\times{\operatorname{Ran}}\to\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}

(and similarly for
{\mathcal{Z}}
).

Denote

\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}:=\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}\underset{\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}}{\times}(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X,{\mathcal{Y}})\times{\operatorname{Ran}}).

Denote by
\pi_{\operatorname{Ran}}
the natural projection

\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}\to\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X,{\mathcal{Y}})

and by
\pi_{{\operatorname{Ran}},{\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{rel}}}
the map

\left(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}\right)_{{\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{rel}}}\to\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X,{\mathcal{Y}})

5.3.2.

We will impose the following finiteness conditions on
{\mathcal{Y}}
and
{\mathcal{Z}}
:

•

{\mathcal{Y}}
is
sectionally laft
in the sense of
[
Ro
, Sect. 3.1.3(ii)]
, i.e.,

–

The prestack
\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X,{\mathcal{Y}})
is locally almost of finite type;

–

The condition of
[
Ro
, Sect. 3.1.3(ii)]
is satisfied for points of
\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X,{\mathcal{Y}})
;

•

{\mathcal{Z}}
is
meromorphically sectionally laft
relative to
{\mathcal{Y}}
, i.e.,

–

The prestack
\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}
is locally almost of finite type;

–

The condition of
[
Ro
, Sect. 3.1.3(ii)]
is satisfied for points of
\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}
.

5.3.3.

Denote

\left(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}\right)_{{\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{rel}}}:=\left(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}\right)_{\operatorname{dR}}\underset{\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X,{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{dR}}}{\times}\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X,{\mathcal{Y}}).

Let

(5.8)

{\mathbf{ind}}^{\operatorname{rel}}:{\operatorname{IndCoh}}(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{Ran}})\rightleftarrows{\operatorname{IndCoh}}\left(\left(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}\right)_{{\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{rel}}}\right):{\mathbf{oblv}}^{\operatorname{rel}}

the resulting pair of adjoint functors.

We have:

Proposition 5.3.4
.

The counit of the adjunction

{\mathbf{ind}}^{\operatorname{rel}}\circ{\mathbf{oblv}}^{\operatorname{rel}}\to\operatorname{Id}

is an isomorphism, when evaluated on objects in the essential image of the pullback functor

(\pi_{{\operatorname{Ran}},{\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{rel}}})^{!}:{\operatorname{IndCoh}}(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X,{\mathcal{Y}}))\to{\operatorname{IndCoh}}\left(\left(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}\right)_{{\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{rel}}}\right).

The proof will be given in Sect.
A
.

5.3.5.

Note that Proposition
5.2.4
is indeed a particular case of Proposition
5.3.4
: we take
{\mathcal{Y}}
to be the constant D-stack
with fiber
\operatorname{pt}/{\check{G}}
and
{\mathcal{Z}}:={\operatorname{Op}}_{\check{G}}
.

∎[Proposition
5.2.4
]

5.4.
A digression: the category
{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}

In order to formulate (an abstract version of) Proposition
5.1.5
, it will be convenient to introduce
a general construction of a certain variant of the category of quasi-coherent sheaves on a prestack,
denoted
{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}(-)
(see
[
GLC2
, Sect. A.1]
for a more detailed discussion).

5.4.1.

Let
{\mathcal{W}}
be a prestack. We define the category
{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}({\mathcal{W}})
by

{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}({\mathcal{W}}):=\underset{S\to{\mathcal{W}},\,S\in\operatorname{Sch}^{\operatorname{aff}}}{\operatorname{colim}}\,{\operatorname{QCoh}}(S),

where the colimit is taken with respect to the
direct image functors
14
14

14

The reason for the notation
“
{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}
” is that it is a version of the
{\operatorname{QCoh}}
category, i.e., we take
the colimit with respect to the *-direct image maps, instead of the limit with respect to the *-pullback maps.
.

5.4.2.

If
{\mathcal{W}}
has an affine diagonal, we have a functor

\Omega_{\mathcal{W}}:{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}({\mathcal{W}})\to{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathcal{W}}).

Namely, it corresponds to the compatible family of direct image functors

{\operatorname{QCoh}}(S)\to{\operatorname{QCoh}}(W),\quad S\in\operatorname{Sch}^{\operatorname{aff}}_{/{\mathcal{W}}},

which are well-defined since the morphisms
S\to{\mathcal{W}}
are affine.

5.4.3.
Example

Suppose that
{\mathcal{W}}
is a
scheme
. Then it is easy to see that in this case the functor
\Omega_{\mathcal{W}}

is an equivalence.

In fact, according to
[
Ga4
, Proposition 6.2.7 and Theorem 2.2.6]
, the same is true when
{\mathcal{W}}
is an eventually coconnective
quasi-compact algebraic stack of finite type with an affine diagonal.

Note that
{\mathcal{W}}=\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}}
is an example of such an algebraic stack.

Remark 5.4.4
.

We do not know whether
{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}({\mathcal{W}})
is dualizable. However,

{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}({\mathcal{W}})
is tautologically the pre-dual of
\operatorname{QCoh}({\mathcal{W}})
, i.e.,

\operatorname{QCoh}({\mathcal{W}})\simeq\operatorname{Funct}({\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}({\mathcal{W}}),\operatorname{Vect}),

where
\operatorname{Funct}(-,-)
is the category of colimit-preserving functors.

In particular, if
{\mathcal{W}}
is such that
{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}({\mathcal{W}})
is dualizable, then so is
\operatorname{QCoh}({\mathcal{W}})
.

5.4.5.
Example

Let
{\mathcal{W}}
be an ind-scheme, written as

{\mathcal{W}}=\underset{i}{\operatorname{colim}}\,W^{i},\quad W^{i}\in\operatorname{Sch},

where the transition maps
W^{i}\to W^{j}
are closed embeddings.

In this case,

{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}({\mathcal{W}})\simeq\underset{i}{\operatorname{colim}}\,{\operatorname{QCoh}}(W^{i})

where the colimit is taken with respect to the direct image functors.

Note that if
{\mathcal{W}}
is of ind-finite type, we
have a naturally defined functor

(5.9)

\Psi_{\mathcal{W}}:{\operatorname{IndCoh}}({\mathcal{W}})\to{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}({\mathcal{W}}).

Indeed, we can write

{\operatorname{IndCoh}}({\mathcal{W}})\simeq\underset{i}{\operatorname{colim}}\,{\operatorname{IndCoh}}(W^{i})

(under direct image functors) and (
5.9
) is given by the compatible family of functors

\Psi_{W^{i}}:{\operatorname{IndCoh}}(W^{i})\to{\operatorname{QCoh}}(W^{i}).

One can show that (
5.9
)
is an equivalence if
{\mathcal{W}}
is formally smooth.

5.4.6.

The assignment

{\mathcal{W}}\rightsquigarrow{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}({\mathcal{W}})

has the following functoriality properties for maps
f:{\mathcal{W}}_{1}\to{\mathcal{W}}_{2}
:

•

We have the direct image functor

f_{*}:{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}({\mathcal{W}}_{1})\to{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}({\mathcal{W}}_{2}).

•

If
f
is
schematic
, we also have the pullback functor

f^{*}:{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}({\mathcal{W}}_{2})\to{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}({\mathcal{W}}_{1}),

which is a left adjoint of
f_{*}
.

•

For a pullback square

\begin{CD}{\mathcal{W}}_{1}@>{f}>{}>{\mathcal{W}}_{2}\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
S_{1}@>{f}>{}>S_{2},\end{CD}

where
S_{1}
and
S_{2}
are affine schemes, the functor

{\operatorname{QCoh}}(S_{1})\underset{{\operatorname{QCoh}}(S_{2})}{\otimes}{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}({\mathcal{W}}_{2})\to{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}({\mathcal{W}}_{1}),

defined by
f^{*}
, is an equivalence;

•

If
f
is schematic
and of finite Tor dimension
, we also have the !-pullback functor

f^{!}:{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}({\mathcal{W}}_{2})\to{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}({\mathcal{W}}_{1}).

Note that if
f
is also proper, then the functors
(f_{*},f^{!})
are mutually adjoint.

5.4.7.

Let
{\mathcal{W}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}
be a prestack over
{\operatorname{Ran}}
. Set

(5.10)

{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}({\mathcal{W}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}:=\underset{S\in\operatorname{Sch}^{\operatorname{aff}}_{\operatorname{aft}},\,S\to{\operatorname{Ran}}}{\operatorname{lim}}\,{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}({\mathcal{W}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}\underset{{\operatorname{Ran}}}{\times}S)\underset{{\operatorname{QCoh}}(S)}{\otimes}{\operatorname{IndCoh}}(S),

where the limit is formed using the *-pullback functors along the
{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}({\mathcal{W}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}\underset{{\operatorname{Ran}}}{\times}S)
-factors and
!-pullback functors along the
{\operatorname{IndCoh}}(S)
-factors.

Thus, an object
{\mathcal{F}}\in{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}({\mathcal{W}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}
gives rise to an object

{\mathcal{F}}_{S,\underline{x}}\in{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}({\mathcal{W}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}\underset{{\operatorname{Ran}}}{\times}S)\underset{{\operatorname{QCoh}}(S)}{\otimes}{\operatorname{IndCoh}}(S)

for every
\underline{x}\in{\operatorname{Ran}}(S)
.

In the case when
{\mathcal{W}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}\to{\operatorname{Ran}}
is schematic, so that for every
(S,\underline{x})
as above we have

{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}({\mathcal{W}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}\underset{{\operatorname{Ran}}}{\times}S)\simeq{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathcal{W}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}\underset{{\operatorname{Ran}}}{\times}S),

we will simply write

{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathcal{W}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}
instead of
{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}({\mathcal{W}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}
.

Remark 5.4.8
.

The assignment

S\rightsquigarrow{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}({\mathcal{W}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}\underset{{\operatorname{Ran}}}{\times}S)

naturally forms a sheaf of categories over
{\operatorname{Ran}}
, to be denoted

\underline{{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}}({\mathcal{W}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}.

The above definition of
{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}({\mathcal{W}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}
is a particular case of the following construction:
for any sheaf of categories
\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}
over
{\operatorname{Ran}}
, we can assign the category

{\mathbf{C}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}:=\underset{S\in\operatorname{Sch}^{\operatorname{aff}}_{\operatorname{aft}},\,S\to{\operatorname{Ran}}}{\operatorname{lim}}\,{\mathbf{C}}(S)\underset{{\operatorname{QCoh}}(S)}{\otimes}{\operatorname{IndCoh}}(S).

Note that since
{\operatorname{Ran}}
is 1-affine, we have

{\mathbf{C}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}\simeq\Gamma({\operatorname{Ran}},\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}_{\operatorname{Ran}})\underset{{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\operatorname{Ran}})}{\otimes}{\operatorname{IndCoh}}({\operatorname{Ran}}).

In particular, since the functor

{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\operatorname{Ran}})\to{\operatorname{IndCoh}}({\operatorname{Ran}}),\quad{\mathcal{F}}\mapsto{\mathcal{F}}\otimes\omega_{\operatorname{Ran}}

is an equivalence, we have an equivalence

{\mathbf{C}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}\simeq\Gamma({\operatorname{Ran}},\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}).

5.4.9.

Let
p_{{\mathcal{W}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}}
denote the projection

{\mathcal{W}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}\to{\operatorname{Ran}}.

Note that we have a well-defined functor

(p_{{\mathcal{W}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}})_{*}:{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}({\mathcal{W}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}\to{\operatorname{IndCoh}}({\operatorname{Ran}})\simeq\operatorname{D-mod}({\operatorname{Ran}}).

Let us denote by

\Gamma^{{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}}({\mathcal{W}}_{\operatorname{Ran}},-):{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}({\mathcal{W}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}\to\operatorname{Vect}

the functor equal to the composition

{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}({\mathcal{W}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}\overset{(p_{{\mathcal{W}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}})_{*}}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{D-mod}({\operatorname{Ran}})\overset{\Gamma^{{\operatorname{IndCoh}}}({\operatorname{Ran}},-)}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Vect},

where we can alternatively think of
\Gamma^{{\operatorname{IndCoh}}}({\operatorname{Ran}},-)
as the functor

\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{c}({\operatorname{Ran}},-):\operatorname{D-mod}({\operatorname{Ran}})\to\operatorname{Vect},

left adjoint to
k\mapsto\omega_{{\operatorname{Ran}}}
.

5.4.10.

Assume now that
{\mathcal{W}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}
is locally almost of finite type (so that
{\operatorname{IndCoh}}({\mathcal{W}}_{\operatorname{Ran}})
is defined)
and assume that
{\mathcal{W}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}\to{\operatorname{Ran}}
is a relative ind-scheme.

We claim that in this case, there exists a well-defined functor

(5.11)

\Psi_{{\mathcal{W}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}}:{\operatorname{IndCoh}}({\mathcal{W}}_{\operatorname{Ran}})\to{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}({\mathcal{W}})_{\operatorname{Ran}},

which is a variant of (
5.9
).

Indeed, we can write

{\operatorname{IndCoh}}({\mathcal{W}}_{\operatorname{Ran}})\simeq\underset{S\in\operatorname{Sch}^{\operatorname{aff}}_{\operatorname{aft}},\,S\to{\operatorname{Ran}}}{\operatorname{lim}}\,{\operatorname{IndCoh}}({\mathcal{W}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}\underset{{\operatorname{Ran}}}{\times}S),

so it is enough to define a compatible family of functors

(5.12)

{\operatorname{IndCoh}}({\mathcal{W}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}\underset{{\operatorname{Ran}}}{\times}S)\to{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}({\mathcal{W}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}\underset{{\operatorname{Ran}}}{\times}S).

Write

{\mathcal{W}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}\underset{{\operatorname{Ran}}}{\times}S\simeq\underset{i}{\operatorname{colim}}\,W^{i}_{S},

where
W^{i}_{S}
are schemes, and the transition maps
W^{i}_{S}\to W^{j}_{S}
are closed embeddings.

The functors (
5.12
) are given by the compatible family of functors

{\operatorname{IndCoh}}(W^{i}_{S})\to{\operatorname{QCoh}}(W^{i}_{S})\underset{{\operatorname{QCoh}}(S)}{\otimes}{\operatorname{IndCoh}}(S),

Serre-dual
to the tautological functors

{\operatorname{QCoh}}(W^{i}_{S})\underset{{\operatorname{QCoh}}(S)}{\otimes}{\operatorname{IndCoh}}(S)\to{\operatorname{IndCoh}}(W^{i}_{S}),

given by !-pullback along
W^{i}_{S}\to S
.

5.5.
Abstract version of Proposition
5.1.5
: the absolute case

As with Proposition
5.1.2
,
we will prove an abstract statement, of which Proposition
5.1.5
is a particular case. The general set-up involves a morphism

{\mathcal{Z}}\to{\mathcal{Y}}

of D-prestacks as in Sect.
5.3.1
. For expository purposes, we will first consider the absolute situation,
i.e., one when
{\mathcal{Y}}=\operatorname{pt}
.

5.5.1.

Let
{\mathcal{Z}}
be an affine D-scheme over
X
. For
\underline{x}\in{\operatorname{Ran}}
we will denote by
{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}})_{\underline{x}}
(resp.,
{\mathfrak{L}}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}})_{\underline{x}}
) the
scheme (resp., ind-scheme)
\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(\mathcal{D}_{\underline{x}},{\mathcal{Z}})
(resp.,
\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(\mathcal{D}_{\underline{x}}-\underline{x},{\mathcal{Z}})
).

Consider the corresponding categories

{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathfrak{L}}^{+}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}})_{\underline{x}})\text{ and }{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}({\mathfrak{L}}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}})_{\underline{x}}).

In addition, we can consider the ind-scheme
\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X-\underline{x},{\mathcal{Z}})
, and the categories

{\operatorname{IndCoh}}(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X-\underline{x},{\mathcal{Z}}))\text{ and }{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X-\underline{x},{\mathcal{Z}})).

5.5.2.

Letting
\underline{x}\in{\operatorname{Ran}}
move in a family over
{\operatorname{Ran}}
, we obtain the spaces
{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}
and
{\mathfrak{L}}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}
, where

{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}\to{\operatorname{Ran}}

is a relative scheme, and

{\mathfrak{L}}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}\to{\operatorname{Ran}}

is a relative ind-scheme. Consider also the relative ind-scheme

\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}
.

We define the categories
{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathfrak{L}}^{+}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}}))_{\operatorname{Ran}}
,
{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}({\mathfrak{L}}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}}))_{\operatorname{Ran}}
and
{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}))_{\operatorname{Ran}}

by the recipe of Sect.
5.4.7
.

5.5.3.

Consider the map

s_{{\mathcal{Z}},{\operatorname{Ran}}}:\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}\to{\mathfrak{L}}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}})_{\operatorname{Ran}},

obtained by restricting horizontal sections along

\mathcal{D}_{\underline{x}}-\underline{x}\to X-\underline{x}.

When
{\mathcal{Z}}
is unambiguous, we will simply write
s_{\operatorname{Ran}}
instead of
s_{{\mathcal{Z}},{\operatorname{Ran}}}
.

We have an adjoint pair of functors

(s_{{\mathcal{Z}},{\operatorname{Ran}}})^{*}:{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}({\mathfrak{L}}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}}))_{\operatorname{Ran}}\rightleftarrows{}{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}))_{\operatorname{Ran}}:(s_{{\mathcal{Z}},{\operatorname{Ran}}})_{*}.

5.5.4.

An abstract version of Proposition
5.1.5
(in the absolute case) case reads:

Proposition 5.5.5
.

The natural transformation

\Gamma^{{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}}(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}})_{\operatorname{Ran}},-)\circ(s_{{\mathcal{Z}},{\operatorname{Ran}}})^{*}\circ(s_{{\mathcal{Z}},{\operatorname{Ran}}})_{*}\to\Gamma^{{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}}(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}})_{\operatorname{Ran}},-)

is an isomorphism, when evaluated on the image of
\omega_{\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}))_{\operatorname{Ran}}}
along

{\operatorname{IndCoh}}(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}})_{\operatorname{Ran}})\overset{\Psi_{\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}}}{\longrightarrow}{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}))_{\operatorname{Ran}}.

The proof will be given in Sect.
B
.

5.6.
Abstract version of Proposition
5.1.5
: the relative case

In this section we will introduce a relative version of the set-up of Sect.
5.5
.

5.6.1.

Let
\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}
have the same meaning as in Sect.
5.3.1
. We will view it as a relative ind-scheme
over

\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X,{\mathcal{Y}})\times{\operatorname{Ran}}.
