Consider the corresponding category

{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}}))_{\operatorname{Ran}}.

Let
\pi_{\operatorname{Ran}}
denote the projection

\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}\to\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X,{\mathcal{Y}}).

Combined with the projection

p_{\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}}:\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}\to{\operatorname{Ran}},

we obtain a map

\pi_{\operatorname{Ran}}\times p_{\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}}:\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}\to\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X,{\mathcal{Y}})\times{\operatorname{Ran}}.

5.6.2.

Let us denote by

(\pi_{\operatorname{Ran}})^{{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}}_{*}:{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}}))_{\operatorname{Ran}}\to{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X,{\mathcal{Y}}))

the composite functor

{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}}))_{\operatorname{Ran}}\overset{(\pi_{\operatorname{Ran}}\times p_{\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}})_{*}}{\longrightarrow}{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X,{\mathcal{Y}}))\otimes{\operatorname{IndCoh}}({\operatorname{Ran}})\to\\
\overset{\Omega_{\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X,{\mathcal{Y}})}\otimes\operatorname{Id}}{\longrightarrow}{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X,{\mathcal{Y}}))\otimes{\operatorname{IndCoh}}({\operatorname{Ran}})\overset{\operatorname{Id}\otimes\Gamma^{{\operatorname{IndCoh}}}({\operatorname{Ran}},-)}{\longrightarrow}{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X,{\mathcal{Y}})).

Note that we have a commutative diagram

(5.13)

\begin{CD}{\operatorname{IndCoh}}(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{Ran}})@>{\Psi_{\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}}}>{}>{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}}))_{\operatorname{Ran}}\\
@V{(\pi_{\operatorname{Ran}})^{{\operatorname{IndCoh}}}_{*}}V{}V@V{}V{(\pi_{\operatorname{Ran}})^{{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}}_{*}}V\\
{\operatorname{IndCoh}}(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X,{\mathcal{Y}}))@>{\Psi_{\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X,{\mathcal{Y}})}}>{}>{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X,{\mathcal{Y}})),\end{CD}

where the top horizontal arrow is the functor of (
5.11
).

5.6.3.

Let us be in the situation described in
[
GLC2
, Sects. 4.5.1-4.5.2.]
, with the following change of notations:
what was denoted by
{\mathcal{Y}}
(resp.,
{\mathcal{Y}}_{0}
) in
loc. cit.
we denote by
{\mathcal{Z}}
(resp.,
{\mathcal{Y}}
).

Let us be given an affine map

s_{{\mathcal{T}},{\operatorname{Ran}}}:\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}\to{\mathcal{T}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}

that fits into a commutative (but not necessarily Cartesian) diagram

(5.14)

\begin{CD}\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}@>{s_{{\mathcal{T}},{\operatorname{Ran}}}}>{}>{\mathcal{T}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}\\
@V{\pi_{\operatorname{Ran}}\times p_{\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}}}V{}V@V{}V{{\mathfrak{L}}(f)}V\\
\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X,{\mathcal{Y}})\times{\operatorname{Ran}}@>{s_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\operatorname{Ran}}}}>{}>{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}({\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}.\end{CD}

5.6.4.

We will now make the following additional structural assumption:

Consider the fiber product

{\mathcal{T}}_{\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X,{\mathcal{Y}}),{\operatorname{Ran}}}:=(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X,{\mathcal{Y}})\times{\operatorname{Ran}})\underset{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}({\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}}{\times}{\mathcal{T}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}.

We require that there exist a
\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X,{\mathcal{Y}})
-family of affine D-schemes

{\mathcal{Z}}_{\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X,{\mathcal{Y}})}\to\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X,{\mathcal{Y}})\times X_{\operatorname{dR}},

such that:

•

{\mathcal{T}}_{\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X,{\mathcal{Y}}),{\operatorname{Ran}}}
identifies with the (relative over
\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X,{\mathcal{Y}})
) factorization space of
horizontal loops

{\mathfrak{L}}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}}_{\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X,{\mathcal{Y}})})_{\operatorname{Ran}}/\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X,{\mathcal{Y}})
;

•

The map

s^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{T}},{\operatorname{Ran}}}:\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}\to{\mathcal{T}}_{\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X,{\mathcal{Y}}),{\operatorname{Ran}}},

arising from (
5.14
), identifies with the evaluation map

s_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X,{\mathcal{Y}})}}:\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}_{\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X,{\mathcal{Y}})}/\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X,{\mathcal{Y}}))_{\operatorname{Ran}}\to{\mathfrak{L}}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}}_{\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X,{\mathcal{Y}})})_{\operatorname{Ran}}/\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X,{\mathcal{Y}}).

Finally, we require that the above data be compatible with the unital structures in the natural sense.

Remark 5.6.5
.

For our applications, we will take
{\mathcal{Z}}={\operatorname{Op}}_{\check{G}}
and
{\mathcal{Y}}=\operatorname{pt}/{\check{G}}
and
{\mathcal{T}}:={\operatorname{Op}}^{\operatorname{mon-free}}_{\check{G}}
.
In this case
\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X,{\mathcal{Y}})=\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}}
, and
{\mathcal{Z}}_{\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X,{\mathcal{Y}})}
is the D-scheme
parameterized by
\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}}
that classifies oper structures on a given local system.

5.6.6.

We have an adjoint pair of functors

(s_{{\mathcal{T}},{\operatorname{Ran}}})^{*}:{}{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}({\mathcal{T}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}\rightleftarrows{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}}))_{\operatorname{Ran}}:(s_{{\mathcal{T}},{\operatorname{Ran}}})_{*}.

5.6.7.

We claim:

Proposition 5.6.8
.

The natural transformation

(\pi_{\operatorname{Ran}})^{{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}}_{*}\circ(s_{{\mathcal{T}},{\operatorname{Ran}}})^{*}\circ(s_{{\mathcal{T}},{\operatorname{Ran}}})_{*}\to(\pi_{\operatorname{Ran}})^{{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}}_{*}

is an isomorphism, when evaluated on objects that lie in the essential image of the functor

(5.15)

{\operatorname{IndCoh}}(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X,{\mathcal{Y}}))\overset{\pi_{\operatorname{Ran}}^{!}}{\longrightarrow}{\operatorname{IndCoh}}(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{Ran}})\to\\
\overset{\Psi_{\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}}}{\longrightarrow}{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}}))_{\operatorname{Ran}}.

The proof will be given in Sect.
C
.

5.6.9.

Let us show how Proposition
5.6.8
implies Proposition
5.1.5
.

We apply Proposition
5.6.8
to the spaces specified in Remark
5.6.5
, so that

\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}={\operatorname{Op}}^{\operatorname{mon-free}}_{\check{G}}(X^{\operatorname{gen}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}.

We have a commutative diagram

(5.16)

\begin{CD}{\operatorname{IndCoh}}({\operatorname{Op}}^{\operatorname{mon-free}}_{\check{G}}(X^{\operatorname{gen}})_{\operatorname{Ran}})@>{\Psi_{{\operatorname{Op}}^{\operatorname{mon-free}}_{\check{G}}(X^{\operatorname{gen}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}}}>{}>{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}({\operatorname{Op}}^{\operatorname{mon-free}}_{\check{G}}(X^{\operatorname{gen}}))_{\operatorname{Ran}}\\
@V{(s_{\operatorname{Ran}})^{{\operatorname{IndCoh}}}_{*}}V{}V@V{}V{(s_{\operatorname{Ran}})_{*}}V\\
{\operatorname{IndCoh}}^{*}({\operatorname{Op}}^{\operatorname{mon-free}}_{\check{G}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}@>{\Psi_{({\operatorname{Op}}^{\operatorname{mon-free}}_{\check{G}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}}}>{}>{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}({\operatorname{Op}}^{\operatorname{mon-free}}_{\check{G}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}.\end{CD}

Moreover, the diagram

(5.17)

\begin{CD}{\operatorname{IndCoh}}({\operatorname{Op}}^{\operatorname{mon-free}}_{\check{G}}(X^{\operatorname{gen}})_{\operatorname{Ran}})@>{\Psi_{{\operatorname{Op}}^{\operatorname{mon-free}}_{\check{G}}(X^{\operatorname{gen}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}}}>{}>{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}({\operatorname{Op}}^{\operatorname{mon-free}}_{\check{G}}(X^{\operatorname{gen}}))_{\operatorname{Ran}}\\
@A{(s_{\operatorname{Ran}})^{*,{\operatorname{IndCoh}}}}A{}A@A{}A{(s_{\operatorname{Ran}})^{*}}A\\
{\operatorname{IndCoh}}^{*}({\operatorname{Op}}^{\operatorname{mon-free}}_{\check{G}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}@>{\Psi_{({\operatorname{Op}}^{\operatorname{mon-free}}_{\check{G}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}}}>{}>{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}({\operatorname{Op}}^{\operatorname{mon-free}}_{\check{G}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}.\end{CD}

obtained from (
5.16
) by passing to left adjoints along the vertical arrows,
commutes as well.

The conclusion of Proposition
5.1.5
follows now from Proposition
5.6.8
, by juxtaposing the commutative diagrams
(
5.13
), (
5.16
) and (
5.17
).

∎[Proposition
5.1.5
]

Appendix A
Proof of Proposition
5.3.4

The idea of the proof of Proposition
5.3.4

can be summarized by the following slogan: the unital version of the space of
rational

horizontal sections maps isomorphically to its own de Rham prestack.

We will deduce it from the main theorem of
[
Ro
]
by a rather formal manipulation.

A.1.
The unital Ran space

In order to prove Proposition
5.3.4
we will need to work with the
unital Ran space
,
which is no longer a prestack (i.e., a functor from affine schemes to
\infty
-groupoids) but rather a

categorical prestack
, i.e., a functor from affine schemes to
\infty
-categories (see

[
GLC2
, Sect. C.5]
for a more detailed discussion).

A.1.1.

Recall the notion of
categorical prestack
, see
[
Ro
, Appendix C]
. By definition, this is a functor

(\operatorname{Sch}^{\operatorname{aff}})^{\operatorname{op}}\to\operatorname{1-Cat},

where
\operatorname{1-Cat}
denotes the
(\infty,1)
-category of
(\infty,1)
-categories.

Thus, a categorical prestack
{\mathcal{X}}
assigns to an affine scheme
S
a category, to be denoted
{\mathcal{X}}(S)
,
and to a map
f:S_{1}\to S_{2}
a functor

{\mathcal{X}}(f):{\mathcal{X}}(S_{2})\to{\mathcal{X}}(S_{1}),

equipped with a datum of compatibility for compositions.

A.1.2.

Let
{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}
be the unital version of the Ran space, see
[
Ga4
, Sect. 4.2]
or
[
Ro
, Sect. 2.1]
. I.e.,

{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}
associates to an affine scheme
S
the
category
of finite subsets of
\operatorname{Hom}(S,X_{\operatorname{dR}})
,
where the morphisms are given by inclusion.

Let

{\mathsf{t}}:{\operatorname{Ran}}\to{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}

denote the tautological map.

A.1.3.

Along with the prestacks

\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}})_{\operatorname{Ran}},\,\,\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{Ran}},\,\,\left(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}\right)_{{\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{rel}}},\,\,\text{etc}

one can consider their unital versions, which are now
categorical prestacks
, denoted

(A.1)

\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}})_{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}},\,\,\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}},\,\,\left(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}\right)_{{\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{rel}}},

respectively, see
[
Ro
, Sect. 3.3.1]
.

Explicitly, for an affine scheme
S
, the category
\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}})_{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}(S)
consists
of pairs
(\underline{x},z)
, where
\underline{x}\in{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}(S)
and
z
is a horizontal section of
{\mathcal{Z}}
on

X\times S-\operatorname{Graph}_{\underline{x}}
.

A morphism
(\underline{x}_{1},z_{1})\to(\underline{x}_{2},z_{2})
is an inclusion
\underline{x}_{1}\subseteq\underline{x}_{2}
and an identification

z_{1}|_{X\times S-\operatorname{Graph}_{\underline{x}_{2}}}\simeq z_{2}.

And similarly for the other two categorical prestacks in (
A.1
).

A.1.4.

By definition, the projections from the categorical prestacks in (
A.1
) to

{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}
are
value-wise co-Cartesian fibrations in groupoids
.

Denote by
\pi_{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}
the projection from

\left(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}\right)_{{\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{rel}}}\to\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X,{\mathcal{Y}}).

A.1.5.

We will denote by
{\mathsf{t}}
the maps from the non-unital to the unital versions. We have

\pi_{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}\circ{\mathsf{t}}=\pi_{\operatorname{Ran}}.

A.2.
IndCoh on categorical prestacks

A.2.1.

Let
{\mathcal{X}}
be a categorical prestack
locally almost of finite type
, see
[
Ro
, Sect. C.1.3]
for what this means.
In this case, it makes sense to talk about the category
{\operatorname{IndCoh}}({\mathcal{X}})
(see
[
Ga4
, Sect. 2.2]
or
[
Ro
, Sect. C.3]
).

Namely, an object
{\mathcal{F}}\in{\operatorname{IndCoh}}({\mathcal{X}})
associates to an affine scheme
S
(assumed almost of finite type) a functor

{\mathcal{X}}(S)\to{\operatorname{IndCoh}}(S),

in a way compatible with !-pullback for morphisms between affine schemes.

We will denote this data as follows:

•

For an object
x\in{\mathcal{X}}(S)
, we have an object

x^{!}({\mathcal{F}})\in{\operatorname{IndCoh}}(S);

•

For a morphism
x_{1}\overset{\alpha}{\to}x_{2}
in
{\mathcal{X}}(S)
a morphism

x_{1}^{!}({\mathcal{F}})\to x_{2}^{!}({\mathcal{F}})

in
{\operatorname{IndCoh}}(S)
.

A.2.2.

We let

{\operatorname{IndCoh}}({\mathcal{X}})_{\operatorname{str}}\subset{\operatorname{IndCoh}}({\mathcal{X}})

be the full subcategory, consisting of objects
{\mathcal{F}}\in{\operatorname{IndCoh}}({\mathcal{X}})
such that for every affine
test-scheme
S
and an arrow

x_{1}\overset{\alpha}{\to}x_{2},\quad x_{1},x_{2}\in{\mathcal{X}}(S),

the resulting map

x_{1}^{!}({\mathcal{F}})\to x_{2}^{!}({\mathcal{F}})

is an isomorphism.

In other words, if we denote by

{\mathcal{X}}\overset{\operatorname{str}}{\to}{\mathcal{X}}_{\operatorname{str}}

the prestack, obtained from
{\mathcal{X}}
by inverting all arrows, the
pullback functor

{\operatorname{IndCoh}}({\mathcal{X}}_{\operatorname{str}})\overset{\operatorname{str}^{!}}{\longrightarrow}{\operatorname{IndCoh}}({\mathcal{X}})

defines an equivalence

{\operatorname{IndCoh}}({\mathcal{X}}_{\operatorname{str}})\overset{\sim}{\to}{\operatorname{IndCoh}}({\mathcal{X}})_{\operatorname{str}}.

A.2.3.

We claim:

Lemma A.2.4
.

The natural diagram of categories

\textstyle{{\operatorname{IndCoh}}({\mathcal{X}}_{{\operatorname{dR}}})_{\operatorname{str}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\operatorname{IndCoh}}({\mathcal{X}})_{\operatorname{str}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\operatorname{IndCoh}}({\mathcal{X}}_{{\operatorname{dR}}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\operatorname{IndCoh}}({\mathcal{X}})}

is a pullback square.

Proof.

Follows from the fact that for an affine scheme
S
almost of finite type, the !-pullback functor with respect to

S_{\operatorname{red}}\to S
is conservative.

∎

A.3.
A reformulation

A.3.1.

Note that the projection

\left(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}\right)_{{\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{rel}}}\overset{\pi_{{\operatorname{Ran}},{\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{rel}}}}{\to}\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X,{\mathcal{Y}})

factors as

\left(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}\right)_{{\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{rel}}}\overset{{\mathsf{t}}}{\to}\left(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}\right)_{{\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{rel}}}\overset{\operatorname{str}}{\to}\\
\to\left(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})_{{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}_{\operatorname{str}}}}\right)_{{\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{rel}}}\overset{(\pi_{{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}},{\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{rel}}})_{\operatorname{str}}}{\to}\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X,{\mathcal{Y}}),

where

\left(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})_{{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}_{\operatorname{str}}}}\right)_{{\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{rel}}}:=\left(\left(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})_{{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}}\right)_{{\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{rel}}}\right)_{\operatorname{str}}.

Hence, the pullback functor

\pi_{{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}.{\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{rel}}}^{!}:{\operatorname{IndCoh}}(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X,{\mathcal{Y}}))\to{\operatorname{IndCoh}}\left(\left(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}\right)_{{\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{rel}}}\right)

maps to

{\operatorname{IndCoh}}\left(\left(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}\right)_{{\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{rel}}}\right)_{\operatorname{str}}\subset{\operatorname{IndCoh}}\left(\left(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}\right)_{{\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{rel}}}\right).

A.3.2.

We obtain that Proposition
5.3.4
follows from the next more precise statement:

Proposition A.3.3
.

The counit of the adjunction

{\mathbf{ind}}^{\operatorname{rel}}\circ{\mathbf{oblv}}^{\operatorname{rel}}\to\operatorname{Id}

is an isomorphism, when evaluated on objects in the essential image along
{\mathsf{t}}^{!}
of

{\operatorname{IndCoh}}\left(\left(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}\right)_{{\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{rel}}}\right)_{\operatorname{str}}\subset{\operatorname{IndCoh}}\left(\left(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}\right)_{{\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{rel}}}\right).

A.3.4.

Consider the commutative diagram

(A.2)

\begin{CD}\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}@>{{\mathsf{t}}}>{}>\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
\left(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}\right)_{{\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{rel}}}@>{{\mathsf{t}}}>{}>\left(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}\right)_{{\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{rel}}}\end{CD}

This diagram is value-wise Cartesian. Hence, we have a well-defined pair of adjoint functors

(A.3)

{\mathbf{ind}}^{\operatorname{rel}}_{\operatorname{untl}}:{\operatorname{IndCoh}}(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}})\rightleftarrows\\
{\operatorname{IndCoh}}\left(\left(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}\right)_{{\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{rel}}}\right):{\mathbf{oblv}}^{\operatorname{rel}}_{\operatorname{untl}},

and both functors are compatible with their non-unital counterparts (
5.8
) via
{\mathsf{t}}^{!}
.

A.3.5.

We also have a commutative diagram

\begin{CD}\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}@>{\operatorname{str}}>{}>\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})_{{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}_{\operatorname{str}}}}\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
\left(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}\right)_{{\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{rel}}}@>{\operatorname{str}}>{}>\left(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})_{{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}_{\operatorname{str}}}}\right)_{{\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{rel}}},\end{CD}

where

\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})_{{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}_{\operatorname{str}}}}:=\left(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})_{{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}}\right)_{\operatorname{str}},

which is value-wise Cartesian. Hence, we have another pair of adjoint functors

(A.4)

{\mathbf{ind}}^{\operatorname{rel}}_{\operatorname{untl,str}}:{\operatorname{IndCoh}}\left(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}_{\operatorname{str}}}\right)\rightleftarrows\\
\rightleftarrows{\operatorname{IndCoh}}\left(\left(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}_{\operatorname{str}}}\right)_{{\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{rel}}}\right):{\mathbf{oblv}}^{\operatorname{rel}}_{\operatorname{untl,str}},

where both functors are compatible with their non-strict counterparts (
A.3
) via
\operatorname{str}^{!}
.

We can equivalently think of (
A.4
) as an adjunction

(A.5)

{\mathbf{ind}}^{\operatorname{rel}}_{\operatorname{untl,str}}:\left({\operatorname{IndCoh}}\left(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}\right)\right)_{\operatorname{str}}\rightleftarrows\\
\rightleftarrows\left({\operatorname{IndCoh}}\left((\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}})_{{\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{rel}}}\right)\right)_{\operatorname{str}}:{\mathbf{oblv}}^{\operatorname{rel}}_{\operatorname{untl,str}}.

A.3.6.

The assertion of Proposition
A.3.3
follows from the following even more precise statement:

Proposition A.3.7
.

The functor

{\mathbf{oblv}}^{\operatorname{rel}}_{\operatorname{untl,str}}:\left({\operatorname{IndCoh}}\left((\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}})_{{\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{rel}}}\right)\right)_{\operatorname{str}}\to\left({\operatorname{IndCoh}}\left(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}\right)\right)_{\operatorname{str}}

is an equivalence.

A.4.
A description of relative D-modules

In order to prove Proposition
A.3.7
, we will describe the category

{\operatorname{IndCoh}}\left((\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}})_{{\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{rel}}}\right)

à la
[
Ro
, Corollary 4.6.10]
.

A.4.1.

As a warm-up, let us fix a point
\underline{x}
, and consider the prestack

\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X-\underline{x},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}}):=\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X-\underline{x},{\mathcal{Z}})\underset{\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X-\underline{x},{\mathcal{Y}})}{\times}\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X,{\mathcal{Y}})

along with its variant

\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X-\underline{x},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})_{{\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{rel}}}:=\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X-\underline{x},{\mathcal{Z}})_{\operatorname{dR}}\underset{\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X-\underline{x},{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{dR}}}{\times}\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X,{\mathcal{Y}})\simeq\\
\simeq\left(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X-\underline{x},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})\right)_{\operatorname{dR}}\underset{\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X,{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{dR}}}{\times}\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X,{\mathcal{Y}}).

We will describe the category

{\operatorname{IndCoh}}(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X-\underline{x},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})_{{\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{rel}}})

along with its forgetful (i.e., pullback) functor to
{\operatorname{IndCoh}}(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X-\underline{x},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}}))
.

A.4.2.

Consider the map

\operatorname{add}_{\underline{x}}:{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}\to{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}},

given by

\underline{y}\mapsto\underline{y}\cup\underline{x}.

Set

\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})_{{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}},\underline{x}}:=\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}\underset{{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}},\operatorname{add}_{\underline{x}}}{\times}{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}.

Restriction along
X-(\underline{y}\cup\underline{x})\subset X-\underline{x}
gives rise to a map

(A.6)

\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X-\underline{x},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})\times{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}\to\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})_{{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}},\underline{x}}.

A.4.3.

Denote by

\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})^{\wedge}_{{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}},\underline{x}}

the formal completion of
\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})_{{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}},\underline{x}}
along (
A.6
).

The projection

\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X-\underline{x},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})\times{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}\to\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X-\underline{x},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})_{{\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{rel}}}

tautologically extends to map

(A.7)

\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})^{\wedge}_{{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}},\underline{x}}\to\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X-\underline{x},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})_{{\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{rel}}}.

A.4.4.

The following is a version of
[
Ro
, Corollary 4.6.10]
, where we allow poles at
\underline{x}
:

Theorem A.4.5
.

The functor

{\operatorname{IndCoh}}\left(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X-\underline{x},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})_{{\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{rel}}}\right)\to\\
\to{\operatorname{IndCoh}}\left(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X-\underline{x},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})\right)\underset{{\operatorname{IndCoh}}\left(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X-\underline{x},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})\times{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}\right)}{\times}{\operatorname{IndCoh}}\left(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})^{\wedge}_{{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}},\underline{x}}\right),

given by pullback along the maps
\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X-\underline{x},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})\to\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X-\underline{x},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})_{{\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{rel}}}
and (
A.7
),
is an equivalence.

Remark A.4.6
.

In fact, this theorem is a particular case of
[
Ro
, Corollary 4.6.10]
: replace the original
{\mathcal{Z}}
by its restriction of scalars along

X-\underline{x}\to X
.

A.4.7.

We will now state a version of Theorem
A.4.5
, where we let
\underline{x}
vary along
{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}
. Consider the map

\operatorname{add}:{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}\times{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}\to{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}},\quad\underline{x}_{1},\underline{x}_{2}\mapsto\underline{x}_{1}\cup\underline{x}_{2}.

Set

\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{add}}:=\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}\underset{{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}},\operatorname{add}}{\times}({\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}\times{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}),

and

\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{pr}_{1}}:=\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}\underset{{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}},\operatorname{pr}_{1}}{\times}({\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}\times{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}),

where

\operatorname{pr}_{1}:{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}\times{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}\to{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}

is the projection on the first factor. In other words,

\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{pr}_{1}}\simeq\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}\times{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}.

Restriction along
X-(\underline{x}_{1}\cup\underline{x}_{2})\subset X-\underline{x}_{1}
gives rise to a map

(A.8)

\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{pr}_{1}}\to\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{add}}.

A.4.8.

Denote by

\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})^{\wedge}_{\operatorname{add}}

the formal completion of

\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{add}}
along (
A.8
).

The projection

\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{pr}_{1}}\to(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}})_{{\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{rel}}}

tautologically extends to a map

(A.9)

\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})^{\wedge}_{\operatorname{add}}\to(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}})_{{\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{rel}}}.

The following is a version of Theorem
A.4.5
in families:

Theorem A.4.9
.

The functor

(A.10)

{\operatorname{IndCoh}}\left((\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}})_{{\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{rel}}}\right)\to\\
\to{\operatorname{IndCoh}}\left(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}\right)\underset{{\operatorname{IndCoh}}\left(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{pr}_{1}}\right)}{\times}{\operatorname{IndCoh}}\left(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})^{\wedge}_{\operatorname{add}}\right),

given by pullback along the maps

\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}\to(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}})_{{\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{rel}}}
and (
A.9
),
is an equivalence.

A.5.
Proof of Proposition
A.3.7

A.5.1.

The functor (
A.10
) induces a functor

(A.11)

{\operatorname{IndCoh}}\left((\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}})_{{\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{rel}}}\right)_{\operatorname{str}}\to\\
\to{\operatorname{IndCoh}}(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}})_{\operatorname{str}}\underset{{\operatorname{IndCoh}}\left(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{pr}_{1}}\right)_{\operatorname{str}}}{\times}{\operatorname{IndCoh}}\left(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})^{\wedge}_{\operatorname{add}}\right)_{\operatorname{str}},

where the two sides in (
A.11
) are full subcategories in the corresponding sides in (
A.10
).

Since the functor (
A.10
) is an equivalence, we obtain that (
A.11
) is fully faithful.

A.5.2.

We will prove:

Lemma A.5.3
.

The functor

{\operatorname{IndCoh}}\left(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})^{\wedge}_{\operatorname{add}}\right)_{\operatorname{str}}\to{\operatorname{IndCoh}}\left(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{pr}_{1}}\right)_{\operatorname{str}}

is an equivalence.

Let us assume this lemma for a moment and finish the proof of Proposition
A.3.7
.

A.5.4.

By Lemma
A.5.3
, we obtain that the right-hand side in (
A.11
) projects isomorphically onto the first factor.
Hence, we obtain that the pullback functor

(A.12)

{\operatorname{IndCoh}}\left((\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}})_{{\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{rel}}}\right)_{\operatorname{str}}\to{\operatorname{IndCoh}}(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}})_{\operatorname{str}},

which is the functor
{\mathbf{oblv}}^{\operatorname{rel}}_{\operatorname{untl,str}}
of Proposition
A.3.7
, is fully faithful.

It remains to show that the functor (
A.12
) is essentially surjective.

A.5.5.

Let
{\mathcal{F}}
be an object in
{\operatorname{IndCoh}}(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}})_{\operatorname{str}}
, which, by Lemma
A.5.3
,
we interpret as an object in the right-hand side of (
A.11
).

By Theorem
A.4.9
it corresponds to an object

{\mathcal{F}}_{\operatorname{dR}}\in{\operatorname{IndCoh}}\left((\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}})_{{\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{rel}}}\right)
, and
we only need to show that
{\mathcal{F}}_{\operatorname{dR}}
is strict. However, this follows from (a relative version of) Lemma
A.2.4
.

∎[Proposition
A.3.7
]

A.6.
Proof of Lemma
A.5.3

A.6.1.

We will prove that the map

\left(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{pr}_{1}}\right)_{\operatorname{str}}\to\left(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})^{\wedge}_{\operatorname{add}}\right)_{\operatorname{str}}

is an isomorphism of prestacks.

A.6.2.

We claim:

Lemma A.6.3
.

Let
{\mathcal{W}}\to{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}\times{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}
be a map of categorical prestacks, which is a value-wise co-Cartesian fibration.
Then then the induced map

{\mathcal{W}}\underset{{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}\times{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}},\Delta}{\times}{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}\to{\mathcal{W}}

induces an isomorphism

\left({\mathcal{W}}\underset{{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}\times{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}},\Delta}{\times}{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}\right){}_{\operatorname{str}}\to{\mathcal{W}}_{\operatorname{str}}.

Proof.

This follows from the fact that the diagonal map
\Delta:{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}\to{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}\times{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}
is value-wise cofinal.

∎

A.6.4.

Applying Lemma
A.6.3
, it suffices to show that the map

\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{pr}_{1}}\underset{{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}\times{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}},\Delta}{\times}{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}\to\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})^{\wedge}_{\operatorname{add}}\underset{{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}\times{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}},\Delta}{\times}{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}

induces an isomorphism on strictifications.

We claim that the above map is actually an isomorphism as-is.

A.6.5.

Since the operation of formal completion commutes with fiber products, it suffices to show that the map

\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{pr}_{1}}\underset{{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}\times{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}},\Delta}{\times}{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}\to\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{add}}\underset{{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}\times{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}},\Delta}{\times}{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}

is an isomorphism.

However, the latter is evident on the nose.

∎[Lemma
A.5.3
]

Appendix B
Proof of Proposition
5.5.5

In this section we let
{\mathcal{Z}}
be an arbitrary affine D-scheme over
X
.

We will show that the assertion of Proposition
5.5.5
essentially amounts to

[
BD1
, Proposition 4.6.5]
, combined with some
unitality
considerations.

B.1.
A reformulation in terms of unital structure

B.1.1.

Let us return to the setting of Sect.
5.4.7
. Suppose that
{\mathcal{W}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}
extends to a categorical prestack
{\mathcal{W}}_{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}
over
{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}
,
so that

{\mathcal{W}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}\simeq{\mathcal{W}}_{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}\underset{{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}}{\times}{\operatorname{Ran}},

and

{\mathcal{W}}_{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}\to{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}

is a value-wise co-Cartesian fibration in groupoids.

Effectively, this means that for
S\in\operatorname{Sch}^{\operatorname{aff}}
and a map
\alpha:\underline{x}_{1}\to\underline{x}_{2}
in
{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}(S)
we have a map of prestacks

\alpha_{\mathcal{W}}:{\mathcal{W}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}\underset{{\operatorname{Ran}},\underline{x}_{1}}{\times}S\to{\mathcal{W}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}\underset{{\operatorname{Ran}},\underline{x}_{2}}{\times}S.

We will refer to
{\mathcal{W}}_{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}
as the unital structure on
{\mathcal{W}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}
.

Let
{\mathsf{t}}
denote the tautological map

{\mathcal{W}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}\to{\mathcal{W}}_{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}.

B.1.2.

To the data as above we can attach a category
{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}({\mathcal{W}})_{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}
. Namely, the data of an object of
{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}({\mathcal{W}})_{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}

consists of an object
{\mathcal{F}}\in{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}({\mathcal{W}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}
, and for every
\alpha
as above of a map

\left((\alpha_{\mathcal{W}})_{*}\otimes\operatorname{Id}_{{\operatorname{IndCoh}}(S)}\right)({\mathcal{F}}_{S,\underline{x}_{1}})\to{\mathcal{F}}_{S,\underline{x}_{2}}

in

{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}({\mathcal{W}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}\underset{{\operatorname{Ran}},\underline{x}_{2}}{\times}S)\underset{{\operatorname{QCoh}}(S)}{\otimes}{\operatorname{IndCoh}}(S).

Let
{\mathsf{t}}^{!}
denote the tautological forgetful functor

{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}({\mathcal{W}})_{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}\to{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}({\mathcal{W}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}.

B.1.3.

Note that the space
\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}})_{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}
from Sect.
A.1.3
provides a unital structure on

\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}
.

We will deduce Proposition
5.5.5
from the following more precise statement:

Proposition B.1.4
.

The natural transformation

\Gamma^{{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}}(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}})_{\operatorname{Ran}},-)\circ(s_{{\mathcal{Z}},{\operatorname{Ran}}})^{*}\circ(s_{{\mathcal{Z}},{\operatorname{Ran}}})_{*}\to\Gamma^{{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}}(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}})_{\operatorname{Ran}},-)

is an isomorphism, when evaluated on the essential image of the functor

{\mathsf{t}}^{!}:{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}))_{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}\to{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}))_{\operatorname{Ran}}.

In the rest of this subsection we will show how Proposition
B.1.4
implies Proposition
5.5.5
.

B.1.5.

Assume that
{\mathcal{W}}_{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}
is locally almost of finite type (in particular,
{\mathcal{W}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}
is locally almost of finite type, so that
the category
{\operatorname{IndCoh}}({\mathcal{W}}_{\operatorname{Ran}})
is well-ddefined). We claim that we have a well-defined category
{\operatorname{IndCoh}}({\mathcal{W}}_{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}})
.

By definition, the data of an object of
{\operatorname{IndCoh}}({\mathcal{W}}_{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}})
consists of an object
{\mathcal{F}}\in{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}({\mathcal{W}}_{\operatorname{Ran}})
, i.e., for every

\underline{x}\in{\operatorname{Ran}}(S)
we have an object
{\mathcal{F}}_{S,\underline{x}}\in{\operatorname{IndCoh}}({\mathcal{W}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}\underset{{\operatorname{Ran}},\underline{x}}{\times}S)
, and
for every
\alpha
as above of a map

(\alpha_{\mathcal{W}})_{*}({\mathcal{F}}_{S,\underline{x}_{1}})\to{\mathcal{F}}_{S,\underline{x}_{2}}

in
{\operatorname{IndCoh}}(S)
.

Let
{\mathsf{t}}^{!}
denote the tautological forgetful functor

{\operatorname{IndCoh}}({\mathcal{W}}_{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}})\to{\operatorname{IndCoh}}({\mathcal{W}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}).

B.1.6.

Assume now that
{\mathcal{W}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}\to{\operatorname{Ran}}
is a relative ind-scheme. Then as in Sect.
5.4.10
we have a functor

(B.1)

\Psi_{{\mathcal{W}}_{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}}:{\operatorname{IndCoh}}({\mathcal{W}}_{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}})\to{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}({\mathcal{W}})_{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}},

which makes the diagram

\begin{CD}{\operatorname{IndCoh}}({\mathcal{W}}_{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}})@>{\Psi_{{\mathcal{W}}_{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}}}>{}>{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}({\mathcal{W}})_{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}\\
@V{{\mathsf{t}}^{!}}V{}V@V{}V{{\mathsf{t}}^{!}}V\\
{\operatorname{IndCoh}}({\mathcal{W}}_{\operatorname{Ran}})@>{\Psi_{{\mathcal{W}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}}}>{}>{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}({\mathcal{W}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}\end{CD}

commutes.

B.1.7.

Thus, we obtain that in order to prove Proposition
5.5.5
, it suffices to show that the object

\omega_{\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}}\in{\operatorname{IndCoh}}(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}})_{\operatorname{Ran}})

lies in the essential image of the functor

{\mathsf{t}}^{!}:{\operatorname{IndCoh}}(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}})_{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}})\to{\operatorname{IndCoh}}(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}).

However, this is true for any
{\mathcal{W}}_{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}
for which the maps
\alpha_{\mathcal{W}}
are proper,
which is the case for
\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}})_{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}
.

∎[Proposition
5.5.5
]

B.2.
The local unital structure

B.2.1.

Recall the categories
{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathfrak{L}}^{+}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}}))_{\operatorname{Ran}}
and
{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}({\mathfrak{L}}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}}))_{\operatorname{Ran}}
, see Sect.
5.4.7
. We will now introduce their
variants, to be denoted

{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathfrak{L}}^{+}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}}))_{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}\text{ and }{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}({\mathfrak{L}}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}}))_{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}},

respectively.

In order to do so, as in Sect.
B.1.2
, we must attach to a map

\alpha:\underline{x}_{1}\to\underline{x}_{2}
in
{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}(S)
functors

(B.2)

{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathfrak{L}}^{+}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}\underset{{\operatorname{Ran}},\underline{x}_{1}}{\times}S)\to{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathfrak{L}}^{+}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}\underset{{\operatorname{Ran}},\underline{x}_{2}}{\times}S)

and

(B.3)

{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}({\mathfrak{L}}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}\underset{{\operatorname{Ran}},\underline{x}_{1}}{\times}S)\to{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}({\mathfrak{L}}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}\underset{{\operatorname{Ran}},\underline{x}_{2}}{\times}S),

respectively.

B.2.2.

Recall that for a point
\underline{x}
of
{\operatorname{Ran}}
we have

{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}})_{\underline{x}}\simeq\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(\mathcal{D}_{\underline{x}},{\mathcal{Z}})\text{ and }{\mathfrak{L}}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}})_{\underline{x}}\simeq\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(\mathcal{D}_{\underline{x}}-\underline{x},{\mathcal{Z}}).

For
\underline{x}_{1}\subseteq\underline{x}_{2}
, set

{\mathfrak{L}}_{\nabla}^{\operatorname{mer}\rightsquigarrow\operatorname{reg}}({\mathcal{Z}})_{\underline{x}_{1}\subseteq\underline{x}_{2}}:=\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(\mathcal{D}_{\underline{x_{2}}}-\underline{x}_{1},{\mathcal{Z}}).

Restriction along

\mathcal{D}_{\underline{x_{1}}}\subseteq\mathcal{D}_{\underline{x_{2}}}

gives rise to a map

(B.4)

{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}})_{\underline{x}_{2}}\to{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}})_{\underline{x}_{1}}.

We define the functor

(B.5)

{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathfrak{L}}^{+}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}})_{\underline{x}_{1}})\to{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathfrak{L}}^{+}_{\nabla}(({\mathcal{Z}})_{\underline{x}_{2}})

to be given by pullback along (
B.4
).

B.2.3.

Restriction along

\mathcal{D}_{\underline{x_{1}}}-\underline{x}_{1}\subseteq\mathcal{D}_{\underline{x_{2}}}-\underline{x}_{1}\supseteq\mathcal{D}_{\underline{x_{2}}}-\underline{x}_{2}

defines maps

(B.6)

{\mathfrak{L}}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}})_{\underline{x}_{1}}\leftarrow{\mathfrak{L}}_{\nabla}^{\operatorname{mer}\rightsquigarrow\operatorname{reg}}({\mathcal{Z}})_{\underline{x}_{1}\subseteq\underline{x}_{2}}\to{\mathfrak{L}}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}})_{\underline{x}_{2}}.

The operation of *-pull and *-push along (
B.6
) gives rise to a functor

(B.7)

{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}({\mathfrak{L}}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}})_{\underline{x}_{1}})\to{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}({\mathfrak{L}}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}})_{\underline{x}_{2}}).

B.2.4.

The operations in (
B.5
) and (
B.7
) make sense when
\underline{x}_{1}
and
\underline{x}_{2}

are
S
-points of
{\operatorname{Ran}}
, and give rise to the sought-for functors (
B.2
) and (
B.3
), respectively.

We will denote by
{\mathsf{t}}^{!}
the corresponding forgetful functors

{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathfrak{L}}^{+}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}}))_{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}\to{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathfrak{L}}^{+}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}}))_{\operatorname{Ran}}\text{ and }{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}({\mathfrak{L}}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}}))_{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}\to{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}({\mathfrak{L}}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}}))_{\operatorname{Ran}},

respectively.

B.2.5.

We will deduce Proposition
B.1.4
from the following even more precise assertion:

Proposition B.2.6
.

The natural transformation

\Gamma^{{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}}({\mathfrak{L}}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}})_{\operatorname{Ran}},-)\to\Gamma^{{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}}({\mathfrak{L}}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}})_{\operatorname{Ran}},-)\circ(s_{{\mathcal{Z}},{\operatorname{Ran}}})_{*}\circ(s_{{\mathcal{Z}},{\operatorname{Ran}}})^{*}\simeq\\
\simeq\Gamma^{{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}}(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}})_{\operatorname{Ran}},-)\circ(s_{{\mathcal{Z}},{\operatorname{Ran}}})^{*},

arising from the unit of the
((s_{{\mathcal{Z}},{\operatorname{Ran}}})^{*},(s_{{\mathcal{Z}},{\operatorname{Ran}}})_{*})
-adjunction, is an isomorphism,
when evaluated on objects lying in the essential image of the functor

{\mathsf{t}}^{!}:{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}({\mathfrak{L}}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}}))_{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}\to{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}({\mathfrak{L}}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}}))_{\operatorname{Ran}}.

In the rest of this subsection we will show how Proposition
B.2.6
implies
Proposition
B.1.4
.

B.2.7.

Let
{\mathcal{F}}_{\operatorname{glob}}
be an object of
{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}))_{\operatorname{Ran}}
, and assume that
it lies in the essential image of

{\mathsf{t}}^{!}:{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}))_{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}\to{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}))_{\operatorname{Ran}}.

We wish to show that the map

\Gamma^{{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}}\left({\mathfrak{L}}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}})_{\operatorname{Ran}},(s_{{\mathcal{Z}},{\operatorname{Ran}}})_{*}\circ(s_{{\mathcal{Z}},{\operatorname{Ran}}})^{*}\circ(s_{{\mathcal{Z}},{\operatorname{Ran}}})_{*}({\mathcal{F}}_{\operatorname{glob}})\right)\to\\
\to\Gamma^{{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}}\left({\mathfrak{L}}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}})_{\operatorname{Ran}},(s_{{\mathcal{Z}},{\operatorname{Ran}}})_{*}({\mathcal{F}}_{\operatorname{glob}})\right),

induced by the
counit
of the
((s_{{\mathcal{Z}},{\operatorname{Ran}}})^{*},(s_{{\mathcal{Z}},{\operatorname{Ran}}})_{*})
-adjunction, is an isomorphism.

It is sufficient to show that the map

\Gamma^{{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}}\left({\mathfrak{L}}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}})_{\operatorname{Ran}},(s_{{\mathcal{Z}},{\operatorname{Ran}}})_{*}({\mathcal{F}}_{\operatorname{glob}})\right)\to\\
\to\Gamma^{{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}}\left({\mathfrak{L}}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}})_{\operatorname{Ran}},(s_{{\mathcal{Z}},{\operatorname{Ran}}})_{*}\circ(s_{{\mathcal{Z}},{\operatorname{Ran}}})^{*}\circ(s_{{\mathcal{Z}},{\operatorname{Ran}}})_{*}({\mathcal{F}}_{\operatorname{glob}})\right),

induced by the
unit
of the adjunction, is an isomorphism.

B.2.8.

Denote

{\mathcal{F}}_{\operatorname{loc}}:=(s_{{\mathcal{Z}},{\operatorname{Ran}}})_{*}({\mathcal{F}}_{\operatorname{glob}})\in{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}({\mathfrak{L}}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}}))_{\operatorname{Ran}}.

Thus, we have to show that the map

(B.8)

\Gamma^{{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}}({\mathfrak{L}}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}})_{\operatorname{Ran}},{\mathcal{F}}_{\operatorname{loc}})\to\Gamma^{{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}}({\mathfrak{L}}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}})_{\operatorname{Ran}},(s_{{\mathcal{Z}},{\operatorname{Ran}}})_{*}\circ(s_{{\mathcal{Z}},{\operatorname{Ran}}})^{*}({\mathcal{F}}_{\operatorname{loc}})),

induced by the unit of the adjunction, is an isomorphism.

B.2.9.

Note that the functor

(s_{{\mathcal{Z}},{\operatorname{Ran}}})_{*}:{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}))_{\operatorname{Ran}}\to{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}({\mathfrak{L}}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}}))_{\operatorname{Ran}}

gives rise to a functor

(s_{{\mathcal{Z}},{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}})_{*}:{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}))_{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}\to{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}({\mathfrak{L}}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}}))_{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}},

so that the diagram

\begin{CD}{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}))_{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}@>{(s_{{\mathcal{Z}},{\operatorname{Ran}}}u)_{*}}>{}>{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}({\mathfrak{L}}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}}))_{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}\\
@V{{\mathsf{t}}^{!}}V{}V@V{}V{{\mathsf{t}}^{!}}V\\
{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}))_{\operatorname{Ran}}@>{(s_{{\mathcal{Z}},{\operatorname{Ran}}})_{*}}>{}>{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}({\mathfrak{L}}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}}))_{\operatorname{Ran}}\end{CD}

commutes.

B.2.10.

Hence, the assumption on
{\mathcal{F}}_{\operatorname{glob}}
implies that
{\mathcal{F}}_{\operatorname{loc}}
lies in the essential image of

{\mathsf{t}}^{!}:{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}({\mathfrak{L}}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}}))_{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}\to{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}({\mathfrak{L}}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}}))_{\operatorname{Ran}}.

Hence, the isomorphism (
B.8
) follows from Proposition
B.2.6
.

∎[Proposition
B.1.4
]

B.3.
An expression for the global sections functor

In this subsection we will recall the expression for the functor

\Gamma^{{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}}\left(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}})_{\operatorname{Ran}},-\right)\circ(s_{{\mathcal{Z}},{\operatorname{Ran}}})^{*}

in terms of
factorization homology
à la
[
BD1
, Sect. 4.6]
.

B.3.1.

To any categorical prestack
{\mathcal{W}}
we can attach the prestack
{\mathcal{W}}^{\to}
that classifies arrows in
{\mathcal{W}}
. I.e.,
for a test affine scheme
S
, the groupoid
{\mathcal{W}}^{\to}(S)
classifies triples

(w_{1}\in{\mathcal{W}}(S),w_{2}\in{\mathcal{W}}(S),\alpha:w_{1}\to w_{2}).

B.3.2.

Denote

{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\subseteq}:=({\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}})^{\to}.

Denote by

\operatorname{pr}_{\operatorname{small}},\operatorname{pr}_{\operatorname{big}}:{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\subseteq}\to{\operatorname{Ran}}

the maps
that correspond to the source and the target of the arrow, respectively.

Explicitly, the groupoid
{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\subseteq}(S)
consists of

\{\underline{x}_{1},\underline{x}_{2}\in{\operatorname{Ran}}(S)\,|\,\underline{x}_{1}\subseteq\underline{x}_{2}\},

and the maps
\operatorname{pr}_{\operatorname{small}}
and
\operatorname{pr}_{\operatorname{big}}
send a point
as above to
\underline{x}_{1}
and
\underline{x}_{2}
, respectively.

B.3.3.

Denote

{\mathfrak{L}}_{\nabla}^{+}({\mathcal{Z}})_{{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\subseteq},\operatorname{small}}:={\mathfrak{L}}_{\nabla}^{+}({\mathcal{Z}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}\underset{{\operatorname{Ran}},\operatorname{pr}_{\operatorname{small}}}{\times}{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\subseteq},\,\,{\mathcal{Z}}_{{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\subseteq},\operatorname{big}}:={\mathfrak{L}}_{\nabla}^{+}({\mathcal{Z}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}\underset{{\operatorname{Ran}},\operatorname{pr}_{\operatorname{big}}}{\times}{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\subseteq}

and

{\mathfrak{L}}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}})_{{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\subseteq},\operatorname{small}}:={\mathfrak{L}}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}\underset{{\operatorname{Ran}},\operatorname{pr}_{\operatorname{small}}}{\times}{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\subseteq},\,\,{\mathfrak{L}}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}})_{{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\subseteq},\operatorname{big}}:={\mathfrak{L}}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}\underset{{\operatorname{Ran}},\operatorname{pr}_{\operatorname{big}}}{\times}{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\subseteq}.

Proceeding as in Sect.
5.4.7
, one can define the corresponding categories

{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathfrak{L}}^{+}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}}))_{{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\subseteq},\operatorname{small}},\,\,{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathfrak{L}}^{+}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}}))_{{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\subseteq},\operatorname{big}},

and

{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}({\mathfrak{L}}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}}))_{{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\subseteq},\operatorname{small}}\text{ and }{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}({\mathfrak{L}}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}}))_{{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\subseteq},\operatorname{big}}

respectively.

B.3.4.

Denote by

p_{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}}:{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}\to{\operatorname{Ran}}

and

p_{{\mathfrak{L}}_{\nabla}^{+}({\mathcal{Z}})_{{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\subseteq},\operatorname{small}}}:{\mathfrak{L}}_{\nabla}^{+}({\mathcal{Z}})_{{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\subseteq},\operatorname{small}}\to{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\subseteq}\text{ and }p_{{\mathfrak{L}}_{\nabla}^{+}({\mathcal{Z}})_{{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\subseteq},\operatorname{big}}}:{\mathfrak{L}}_{\nabla}^{+}({\mathcal{Z}})_{{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\subseteq},\operatorname{big}}\to{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\subseteq},

as well as

p_{{\mathfrak{L}}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}}:{\mathfrak{L}}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}\to{\operatorname{Ran}}

and

p_{{\mathfrak{L}}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}})_{{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\subseteq},\operatorname{small}}}:{\mathfrak{L}}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}})_{{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\subseteq},\operatorname{small}}\to{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\subseteq}\text{ and }p_{{\mathfrak{L}}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}})_{{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\subseteq},\operatorname{big}}}:{\mathfrak{L}}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}})_{{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\subseteq},\operatorname{big}}\to{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\subseteq},

the resulting maps.

We will consider the corresponding functors

(p_{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}})_{*}:{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathfrak{L}}^{+}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}}))_{\operatorname{Ran}}\to{\operatorname{IndCoh}}({\operatorname{Ran}})

and

(p_{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}})_{{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\subseteq},\operatorname{big}}})_{*}:{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathfrak{L}}^{+}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}}))_{{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\subseteq},\operatorname{big}}\to{\operatorname{IndCoh}}({\operatorname{Ran}}^{\subseteq})

as well

(p_{{\mathfrak{L}}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}})_{*}:{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}({\mathfrak{L}}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}}))_{\operatorname{Ran}}\to{\operatorname{IndCoh}}({\operatorname{Ran}})

and

(p_{{\mathfrak{L}}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}})_{{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\subseteq},\operatorname{big}}})_{*}:{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}({\mathfrak{L}}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}}))_{{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\subseteq},\operatorname{big}}\to{\operatorname{IndCoh}}({\operatorname{Ran}}^{\subseteq}).

B.3.5.

Note now that the unital structures on the categories
{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathfrak{L}}^{+}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}}))_{\operatorname{Ran}}
and

{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}({\mathfrak{L}}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}}))_{\operatorname{Ran}}
, determined by
{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathfrak{L}}^{+}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}}))_{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}
,
and
{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}({\mathfrak{L}}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}}))_{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}
, respectively,
give rise to functors

(B.9)

{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}({\mathfrak{L}}^{+}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}}))_{{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\subseteq},\operatorname{small}}\to{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}({\mathfrak{L}}^{+}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}}))_{{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\subseteq},\operatorname{big}}

and

(B.10)

{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}({\mathfrak{L}}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}}))_{{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\subseteq},\operatorname{small}}\to{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}({\mathfrak{L}}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}}))_{{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\subseteq},\operatorname{big}}.

Denote the compositions

{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathfrak{L}}^{+}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}}))_{\operatorname{Ran}}\overset{\operatorname{pr}_{\operatorname{small}}^{!}}{\longrightarrow}{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathfrak{L}}^{+}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}}))_{{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\subseteq},\operatorname{small}}\overset{\text{\eqref{e:ins vacuum}}}{\longrightarrow}{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathfrak{L}}^{+}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}}))_{{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\subseteq},\operatorname{big}}

and

{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}({\mathfrak{L}}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}}))_{\operatorname{Ran}}\overset{\operatorname{pr}_{\operatorname{small}}^{!}}{\longrightarrow}{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}({\mathfrak{L}}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}}))_{{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\subseteq},\operatorname{small}}\overset{\text{\eqref{e:ins vacuum punct}}}{\longrightarrow}{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}({\mathfrak{L}}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}}))_{{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\subseteq},\operatorname{big}}

in both instances
by
\operatorname{ins.unit}
; we will refer to this functor as the “insertion of the unit”.

B.3.6.

Let

\operatorname{diag}:{\operatorname{Ran}}\to{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\subseteq}

denote the diagonal map,

\underline{x}\mapsto(\underline{x}\subseteq\underline{x}).

In terms of Sect.
B.3.1
, it corresponds to the identity morphisms
on objects of
{\mathcal{W}}_{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}(S)
.

Note that we have pullback squares

(B.11)

\begin{CD}{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}@>{\operatorname{diag}}>{}>{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}})_{{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\subseteq},\operatorname{big}}\\
@V{p_{{\mathfrak{L}}_{\nabla}^{+}({\mathcal{Z}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}}}V{}V@V{}V{p_{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}})_{{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\subseteq},\operatorname{big}}}}V\\
{\operatorname{Ran}}@>{\operatorname{diag}}>{}>{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\subseteq}.\end{CD}

and

(B.12)

\begin{CD}{\mathfrak{L}}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}@>{\operatorname{diag}}>{}>{\mathfrak{L}}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}})_{{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\subseteq},\operatorname{big}}\\
@V{p_{{\mathfrak{L}}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}}}V{}V@V{}V{p_{{\mathfrak{L}}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}})_{{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\subseteq},\operatorname{big}}}}V\\
{\operatorname{Ran}}@>{\operatorname{diag}}>{}>{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\subseteq}.\end{CD}

Note also that we have a canonical identification

\operatorname{diag}^{!}\circ\operatorname{ins.unit}\simeq\operatorname{Id},

in both instances.

B.3.7.

Recall that
s_{{\mathcal{Z}},{\operatorname{Ran}}}
(or simply
s_{{\mathcal{Z}},{\operatorname{Ran}}}
) denotes the map

\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}\to{\mathfrak{L}}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}.

We will use the same symbol
s_{{\mathcal{Z}},{\operatorname{Ran}}}
(or simply
s_{{\mathcal{Z}},{\operatorname{Ran}}}
) to denote the map

\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X,{\mathcal{Z}})\times{\operatorname{Ran}}\to{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}.

The following assertion is a variant with parameters of
[
BD2
, Proposition 4.6.5]
:

Proposition B.3.8
.

(a)
The functor

{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathfrak{L}}^{+}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}}))_{\operatorname{Ran}}\overset{(s_{{\mathcal{Z}},{\operatorname{Ran}}})^{*}}{\longrightarrow}{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X,{\mathcal{Z}}))\otimes{\operatorname{IndCoh}}({\operatorname{Ran}})\overset{\Gamma(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X,{\mathcal{Z}}),-)\otimes\operatorname{Id}}{\longrightarrow}{\operatorname{IndCoh}}({\operatorname{Ran}})

identifies canonically with

{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathfrak{L}}^{+}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}}))_{\operatorname{Ran}}\overset{\operatorname{ins.unit}}{\longrightarrow}{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathfrak{L}}^{+}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}}))_{{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\subseteq},\operatorname{big}}\overset{(p_{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}})_{{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\subseteq},\operatorname{big}}})_{*}}{\longrightarrow}{\operatorname{IndCoh}}({\operatorname{Ran}}^{\subseteq})\to\\
\overset{(\operatorname{pr}_{\operatorname{small}})^{{\operatorname{IndCoh}}}_{*}}{\longrightarrow}{\operatorname{IndCoh}}({\operatorname{Ran}}).

Under the above identification, the map

(p_{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}})_{*}\to(p_{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}})_{*}\circ(s_{{\mathcal{Z}},{\operatorname{Ran}}})_{*}\circ(s_{{\mathcal{Z}},{\operatorname{Ran}}})^{*},

given by the unit of the
((s_{{\mathcal{Z}},{\operatorname{Ran}}})^{*},(s_{{\mathcal{Z}},{\operatorname{Ran}}})_{*})
-adjunction, corresponds to the map

(p_{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}})_{*}\simeq(\operatorname{pr}_{\operatorname{small}})_{*}\circ(p_{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}})_{{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\subseteq},\operatorname{big}}})_{*}\circ\operatorname{diag}_{*}\simeq\\
\simeq(\operatorname{pr}_{\operatorname{small}})_{*}\circ(p_{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}})_{{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\subseteq},\operatorname{big}}})_{*}\circ\operatorname{diag}_{*}\circ\operatorname{diag}^{!}\circ\operatorname{ins.unit}\to(\operatorname{pr}_{\operatorname{small}})_{*}\circ(p_{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}})_{{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\subseteq},\operatorname{big}}})_{*}\circ\operatorname{ins.unit}.

(b)
The functor

{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}({\mathfrak{L}}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}}))_{\operatorname{Ran}}\overset{(s_{{\mathcal{Z}},{\operatorname{Ran}}})^{*}}{\longrightarrow}{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}))_{\operatorname{Ran}}\overset{(p_{\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}))_{\operatorname{Ran}}})_{*}}{\longrightarrow}{\operatorname{IndCoh}}({\operatorname{Ran}})

identifies canonically with

{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}({\mathfrak{L}}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}}))_{\operatorname{Ran}}\overset{\operatorname{ins.unit}}{\longrightarrow}{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}({\mathfrak{L}}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}}))_{{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\subseteq},\operatorname{big}}\overset{(p_{{\mathfrak{L}}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}})_{{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\subseteq},\operatorname{big}}})_{*}}{\longrightarrow}{\operatorname{IndCoh}}({\operatorname{Ran}}^{\subseteq})\\
\to\overset{(\operatorname{pr}_{\operatorname{small}})_{*}}{\longrightarrow}{\operatorname{IndCoh}}({\operatorname{Ran}}).

Under the above identification, the map

(p_{{\mathfrak{L}}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}})_{*}\to(p_{{\mathfrak{L}}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}})_{*}\circ(s_{{\mathcal{Z}},{\operatorname{Ran}}})_{*}\circ(s_{{\mathcal{Z}},{\operatorname{Ran}}})^{*},

given by the unit of the
((s_{{\mathcal{Z}},{\operatorname{Ran}}})^{*},(s_{{\mathcal{Z}},{\operatorname{Ran}}})_{*})
-adjunction, corresponds to the map

(p_{{\mathfrak{L}}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}})_{*}\simeq(\operatorname{pr}_{\operatorname{small}})_{*}\circ(p_{{\mathfrak{L}}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}})_{{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\subseteq},\operatorname{big}}})_{*}\circ\operatorname{diag}_{*}\simeq\\
\simeq(\operatorname{pr}_{\operatorname{small}})_{*}\circ(p_{{\mathfrak{L}}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}})_{{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\subseteq},\operatorname{big}}})_{*}\circ\operatorname{diag}_{*}\circ\operatorname{diag}^{!}\circ\operatorname{ins.unit}\to(\operatorname{pr}_{\operatorname{small}})_{*}\circ(p_{{\mathfrak{L}}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}})_{{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\subseteq},\operatorname{big}}})_{*}\circ\operatorname{ins.unit}.

B.4.
Inputing the unitality structure

In this subsection we will prove Proposition
B.2.6
by combining Proposition
B.3.8
(b) with a
cofinality argument.

B.4.1.

Note that in the situation of Sect.
B.3.1
, the prestack
{\mathcal{W}}^{\to}
itself can also be extended to a categorical
prestack.

Applying this to
{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}
, we obtain a categorical prestack, denoted
{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\subseteq,\operatorname{untl}}
. Explicitly, the space of morphisms

(\underline{x}_{1}\subseteq\underline{x}_{2})\to(\underline{x}^{\prime}_{1}\subseteq\underline{x}^{\prime}_{2})

is

\begin{cases}&\{*\}\text{ if }\underline{x}_{1}\subseteq\underline{x}^{\prime}_{1}\text{ and }\underline{x}_{2}\subseteq\underline{x}^{\prime}_{2},\\
&\emptyset,\,\text{ otherwise}.\end{cases}

Denote by

{\mathsf{t}}^{\subseteq}:{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}\to{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\subseteq,\operatorname{untl}}

the corresponding map.

B.4.2.

The following assertion is obtained by unwinding the constructions:

Lemma B.4.3
.

The composite functor

{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}({\mathfrak{L}}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}}))_{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}\overset{{\mathsf{t}}^{!}}{\to}{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}({\mathfrak{L}}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}}))_{\operatorname{Ran}}\overset{\operatorname{ins.unit}}{\longrightarrow}\\
\to{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}({\mathfrak{L}}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}}))_{{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\subseteq},\operatorname{big}}\overset{(p_{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}})_{{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\subseteq},\operatorname{big}}})_{*}}{\longrightarrow}{\operatorname{IndCoh}}({\operatorname{Ran}}^{\subseteq})

factors via a functor

{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}({\mathfrak{L}}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}}))_{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}\to{\operatorname{IndCoh}}({\operatorname{Ran}}^{\subseteq,\operatorname{untl}}),

followed by
({\mathsf{t}}^{\subseteq})^{!}
.

B.4.4.

Note that from (
B.12
), we obtain commutative diagrams

\begin{CD}{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}({\mathfrak{L}}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}}))_{{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\subseteq},\operatorname{big}}@>{\operatorname{diag}^{!}}>{}>{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}({\mathfrak{L}}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}}))_{\operatorname{Ran}}\\
@V{(p_{{\mathfrak{L}}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}})_{{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\subseteq},\operatorname{big}}})_{*}}V{}V@V{}V{(p_{{\mathfrak{L}}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}})_{*}}V\\
{\operatorname{IndCoh}}({\operatorname{Ran}}^{\subseteq})@>{\operatorname{diag}^{!}}>{}>{\operatorname{IndCoh}}({\operatorname{Ran}})\end{CD}

and

\begin{CD}{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}({\mathfrak{L}}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}}))_{\operatorname{Ran}}@>{\operatorname{diag}_{*}}>{}>{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}({\mathfrak{L}}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}}))_{{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\subseteq},\operatorname{big}}\\
@V{(p_{{\mathfrak{L}}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}})_{*}}V{}V@V{}V{(p_{{\mathfrak{L}}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Z}})_{{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\subseteq},\operatorname{big}}})_{*}}V\\
{\operatorname{IndCoh}}({\operatorname{Ran}})@>{\operatorname{diag}_{*}}>{}>{\operatorname{IndCoh}}({\operatorname{Ran}}^{\subseteq}).\end{CD}

Hence, combining Proposition
B.3.8
(b) and Lemma
B.4.3
, we obtain that in order deduce Proposition
B.2.6
,
it suffices to prove the following assertion:

Proposition B.4.5
.

The natural transformation

\Gamma^{{\operatorname{IndCoh}}}({\operatorname{Ran}},-)\circ\operatorname{diag}^{!}\simeq\Gamma^{{\operatorname{IndCoh}}}({\operatorname{Ran}},-)\circ(\operatorname{pr}_{\operatorname{small}})_{*}\circ\operatorname{diag}_{*}\circ\operatorname{diag}^{!}\to\\
\to\Gamma^{{\operatorname{IndCoh}}}({\operatorname{Ran}},-)\circ(\operatorname{pr}_{\operatorname{small}})_{*}\simeq\Gamma^{{\operatorname{IndCoh}}}({\operatorname{Ran}}^{\subseteq},-)

of functors
{\operatorname{IndCoh}}({\operatorname{Ran}}^{\subseteq})\rightrightarrows\operatorname{Vect}
, is an isomorphism, when evaluated
on the essential image of the functor

({\mathsf{t}}^{\subseteq})^{!}:{\operatorname{IndCoh}}({\operatorname{Ran}}^{\subseteq,\operatorname{untl}})\to{\operatorname{IndCoh}}({\operatorname{Ran}}^{\subseteq}).

∎[Proposition
B.2.6
]

B.4.6.
Proof of Proposition
B.4.5

We need to show that the natural transformation

(B.13)

\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{c}({\operatorname{Ran}},-)\circ\operatorname{diag}^{!}\circ({\mathsf{t}}^{\subseteq})^{!}\to\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{c}({\operatorname{Ran}}^{\subseteq},-)\circ({\mathsf{t}}^{\subseteq})^{!},

as functors

{\operatorname{IndCoh}}({\operatorname{Ran}}^{\subseteq,\operatorname{untl}})\rightrightarrows\operatorname{Vect},

is an isomorphism.

First, as in
[
Ga4
, Theorem 4.6.2]
, one shows that the map
{\mathsf{t}}^{\subseteq}
is
universally homologically cofinal
. Hence,
the natural transformation

\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{c}({\operatorname{Ran}}^{\subseteq},-)\circ({\mathsf{t}}^{\subseteq})^{!}\to\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{c}({\operatorname{Ran}}^{\subseteq,\operatorname{untl}},-),

as functors

{\operatorname{IndCoh}}({\operatorname{Ran}}^{\subseteq,\operatorname{untl}})\rightrightarrows\operatorname{Vect},

is an isomorphism.

Consider now the composition

{\mathsf{t}}^{\subseteq}\circ\operatorname{diag}:{\operatorname{Ran}}\to{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\subseteq,\operatorname{untl}}.

It is easily seen to be value-wise cofinal. Hence, the natural transformation

\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{c}({\operatorname{Ran}},-)\circ\operatorname{diag}^{!}\circ({\mathsf{t}}^{\subseteq})^{!}\to\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{c}({\operatorname{Ran}}^{\subseteq,\operatorname{untl}},-),

as functors

{\operatorname{IndCoh}}({\operatorname{Ran}}^{\subseteq,\operatorname{untl}})\rightrightarrows\operatorname{Vect},

is an isomorphism.

Combining, we obtain that (
B.13
) is also an isomorphism, as desired.

∎[Proposition
B.4.5
]

Appendix C
Proof of Proposition
5.6.8

.

We will show that Proposition
5.6.8
amounts to a parameterized version of
Proposition
5.5.5
, combined with a fully-faithfulness assertion regarding the

localization functor

\operatorname{Loc}_{\mathcal{Y}}
.

C.1.
Localization functor in the abstract setting

C.1.1.

Let
{\mathcal{Y}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}
satisfy the following conditions:

•

The diagonal map
{\mathcal{Y}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}\to{\mathcal{Y}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}\underset{{\operatorname{Ran}}}{\times}{\mathcal{Y}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}
is affine.
Note that this formally implies that the diagonal map of
\operatorname{Sect}(X,{\mathcal{Y}})
is affine;

•

For every
S\to{\operatorname{Ran}}
, the prestack
{\mathcal{Y}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}\underset{{\operatorname{Ran}}}{\times}S
is

passable
(see
[
GaRo2
, Chapter 3, Sect. 3.5.1]
for what this means);

•

The prestack
\operatorname{Sect}(X,{\mathcal{Y}})
is passable.

C.1.2.

Note that the above conditions imply that the morphism

s_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\operatorname{Ran}}}:\operatorname{Sect}(X,{\mathcal{Y}})\times{\operatorname{Ran}}\to{\mathcal{Y}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}

behaves nicely with respect to push-forwards:

For any prestack
{\mathcal{W}}
mapping to
{\mathcal{Y}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}
, and the base-changed map

s^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\operatorname{Ran}}}:(\operatorname{Sect}(X,{\mathcal{Y}})\times{\operatorname{Ran}})\underset{{\mathcal{Y}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}}{\times}{\mathcal{W}}\to{\mathcal{W}},

the functor

(s^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\operatorname{Ran}}})_{*}:{\operatorname{QCoh}}((\operatorname{Sect}(X,{\mathcal{Y}})\times{\operatorname{Ran}})\underset{{\mathcal{Y}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}}{\times}{\mathcal{W}})\to{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathcal{W}}),

right adjoint to
(s^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\operatorname{Ran}}})^{*}
, commutes with colimits,
and satisfies the base change formula. This follows from
[
GaRo2
, Chapter 3, Proposition 3.5.3]
.

C.1.3.

Consider the resulting pair of adjoint functors

(s_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\operatorname{Ran}}})^{*}:{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}\rightleftarrows{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{Sect}(X,{\mathcal{Y}}))\otimes{\operatorname{IndCoh}}({\operatorname{Ran}}):(s_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\operatorname{Ran}}})_{*}.

Denote

\operatorname{Loc}_{\mathcal{Y}}:=(\operatorname{Id}\otimes\Gamma^{{\operatorname{IndCoh}}}({\operatorname{Ran}},-))\circ(s_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\operatorname{Ran}}})^{*},\quad{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}\to{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{Sect}(X,{\mathcal{Y}})).

The right adjoint
\operatorname{Loc}_{\mathcal{Y}}^{R}
of
\operatorname{Loc}_{\mathcal{Y}}
is thus given by

(s_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\operatorname{Ran}}})_{*}\circ(\operatorname{Id}\otimes(\omega_{\operatorname{Ran}}\otimes-)),\quad{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{Sect}(X,{\mathcal{Y}}))\to{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}.

C.1.4.

We will prove:

Proposition C.1.5
.

Let
{\mathcal{Y}}
be a D-prestack with an affine diagonal, satisfying the finiteness assumptions of Sect.
C.1.1
above.
Then the natural transformation
