\operatorname{Loc}_{\mathcal{Y}}\circ(s_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\operatorname{Ran}}})_{*}\simeq(\operatorname{Id}\otimes\Gamma^{{\operatorname{IndCoh}}}({\operatorname{Ran}},-))\circ(s_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\operatorname{Ran}}})^{*}\circ(s_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\operatorname{Ran}}})_{*}\to(\operatorname{Id}\otimes\Gamma^{{\operatorname{IndCoh}}}({\operatorname{Ran}},-)),

arising from the counit of the
((s_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\operatorname{Ran}}})^{*},(s_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\operatorname{Ran}}})_{*})
-adjunction, is an isomorphism, when evaluated on the essential image of
the functor

{\mathsf{t}}^{!}\otimes\operatorname{Id}:{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{Sect}(X,{\mathcal{Y}}))\otimes{\operatorname{IndCoh}}({\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}})\to{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{Sect}(X,{\mathcal{Y}}))\otimes{\operatorname{IndCoh}}({\operatorname{Ran}}).

This proposition will be proved in Sect.
C.3
.

C.1.6.

Combined with the contractibility of the Ran space, i.e., the fact that the map

\Gamma^{{\operatorname{IndCoh}}}({\operatorname{Ran}},\omega_{\operatorname{Ran}})\to k,

given by the counit of the
(\Gamma^{{\operatorname{IndCoh}}}({\operatorname{Ran}},-),\omega_{\operatorname{Ran}}\otimes-)
-adjunction,
is an isomorphism, from Proposition
C.1.5
we obtain:

Proposition C.1.7
.

Let
{\mathcal{Y}}
be a D-prestack with an affine diagonal, satisfying the finiteness assumptions of Sect.
C.1.1
above.
Then the counit of the adjunction

\operatorname{Loc}_{\mathcal{Y}}\circ\operatorname{Loc}^{R}_{\mathcal{Y}}\to\operatorname{Id},

is an isomorphism.

Note that Proposition
C.1.7
can be restated as:

Corollary C.1.8
.

Under the above assumptions on
{\mathcal{Y}}
, the functor

\operatorname{Loc}^{R}_{\mathcal{Y}}:{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{Sect}(X,{\mathcal{Y}}))\to{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}

is fully faithful.

C.1.9.

Note that for
{\mathcal{Y}}
being the constant D-stack with fiber
\operatorname{pt}/{\check{G}}
, we have

\operatorname{Sect}(X,{\mathcal{Y}})\simeq\operatorname{LS}_{\check{G}}.

Furthermore, we have a tautological identification

{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}\simeq{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{G}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}.

Under this identification, we have

\operatorname{Loc}_{\mathcal{Y}}\simeq\operatorname{Loc}_{\check{G}}^{\operatorname{spec}}

and

\operatorname{Loc}_{\mathcal{Y}}^{R}\simeq\Gamma_{\check{G}}^{\operatorname{spec}}.

Hence, Corollary
C.1.8
contains Proposition
1.1.4
as a particular case.

Remark C.1.10
.

As far as the actual proof of Proposition
C.1.5
is concerned, we will first establish Proposition
C.1.7
, and then deduce
the general case stated in Proposition
C.1.5
.

C.2.
Proof of Proposition
5.6.8

In this subsection we will assume Proposition
C.1.5
and deduce Proposition
5.6.8
.

C.2.1.

Along with the category
{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}}))_{\operatorname{Ran}}
,
we will consider its unital version

{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}}))_{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}},

equipped with a forgetful functor

(C.1)

{\mathsf{t}}^{!}:{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}}))_{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}\to{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}}))_{\operatorname{Ran}}.

We will show that the natural transformation

(C.2)

(\pi_{\operatorname{Ran}})^{{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}}_{*}\circ(s_{{\mathcal{T}},{\operatorname{Ran}}})^{*}\circ(s_{{\mathcal{T}},{\operatorname{Ran}}})_{*}\to(\pi_{\operatorname{Ran}})^{{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}}_{*},

induced by the counit of the
((s_{{\mathcal{T}},{\operatorname{Ran}}})^{*},(s_{{\mathcal{T}},{\operatorname{Ran}}})_{*})
-adjunction, is an isomorphism,
when evaluated on objects lying in the essential image of the forgetful functor (
C.1
).

This will imply the assertion of Proposition
5.6.8
, since the functor (
5.15
) factors as

{\operatorname{IndCoh}}(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X,{\mathcal{Y}}))\overset{\pi_{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}^{!}}{\longrightarrow}{\operatorname{IndCoh}}(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}})\to\\
\overset{\Psi_{\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}}}{\longrightarrow}{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}}))_{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}\overset{{\mathsf{t}}^{!}}{\to}{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}}))_{\operatorname{Ran}}.

C.2.2.

Recall the space

{\mathcal{T}}_{\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X,{\mathcal{Y}}),{\operatorname{Ran}}}:=(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X,{\mathcal{Y}})\times{\operatorname{Ran}})\underset{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}({\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}}{\times}{\mathcal{T}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}.

Consider the corresponding category

{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}({\mathcal{T}}_{\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X,{\mathcal{Y}})})_{\operatorname{Ran}}.

Let us denote by
\pi^{\prime}_{\operatorname{Ran}}
the projection

{\mathcal{T}}_{\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X,{\mathcal{Y}}),{\operatorname{Ran}}}\to\operatorname{Sect}(X,{\mathcal{Y}}).

A procedure similar to that defining the functor
(\pi_{\operatorname{Ran}})^{{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}}_{*}
gives rise to a functor

(\pi^{\prime}_{\operatorname{Ran}})^{{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}}_{*}:{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}({\mathcal{T}}_{\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X,{\mathcal{Y}})})_{\operatorname{Ran}}\to{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{Sect}(X,{\mathcal{Y}})).

C.2.3.

Note that the morphism

s_{{\mathcal{T}},{\operatorname{Ran}}}:\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}\to{\mathcal{T}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}

factors as

\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}\overset{s^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{T}},{\operatorname{Ran}}}}{\longrightarrow}{\mathcal{T}}_{\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X,{\mathcal{Y}}),{\operatorname{Ran}}}\overset{s^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\operatorname{Ran}}}}{\longrightarrow}{\mathcal{T}}_{\operatorname{Ran}},

where
s^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\operatorname{Ran}}}
is a base change of the map

s_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\operatorname{Ran}}}:\operatorname{Sect}(X,{\mathcal{Y}})\times{\operatorname{Ran}}\to{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}_{\nabla}({\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{Ran}},

which appears in Proposition
C.1.5
.

Thus, we can factor
(s_{{\mathcal{T}},{\operatorname{Ran}}})_{*}
as

{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}}))_{\operatorname{Ran}}\overset{(s^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{T}},{\operatorname{Ran}}})_{*}}{\longrightarrow}{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}({\mathcal{T}}_{\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X,{\mathcal{Y}})})_{\operatorname{Ran}}\overset{(s^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\operatorname{Ran}}})_{*}}{\longrightarrow}{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}({\mathcal{T}}_{\operatorname{Ran}})

and
(s_{{\mathcal{T}},{\operatorname{Ran}}})^{*}
as

{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}({\mathcal{T}}_{\operatorname{Ran}})\overset{(s^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\operatorname{Ran}}})^{*}}{\longrightarrow}{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}({\mathcal{T}}_{\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X,{\mathcal{Y}})})_{\operatorname{Ran}}\overset{(s^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{T}},{\operatorname{Ran}}})^{*}}{\longrightarrow}{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}(\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X^{\operatorname{gen}},{\mathcal{Z}}/{\mathcal{Y}}))_{\operatorname{Ran}}.

C.2.4.

Consider the natural transformation

(C.3)

(\pi^{\prime}_{\operatorname{Ran}})^{{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}}_{*}\circ(s^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\operatorname{Ran}}})^{*}\circ(s^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\operatorname{Ran}}})_{*}\circ(s^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{T}},{\operatorname{Ran}}})_{*}\to\\
\to(\pi_{\operatorname{Ran}})^{{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}}_{*}\circ(s^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{T}},{\operatorname{Ran}}})^{*}\circ(s^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\operatorname{Ran}}})^{*}\circ(s^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\operatorname{Ran}}})_{*}\circ(s^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{T}},{\operatorname{Ran}}})_{*},

arising from the
unit
of the
((s^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{T}},{\operatorname{Ran}}})^{*},(s^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{T}},{\operatorname{Ran}}})_{*})
-adjunction.

Its composition with (
C.2
) is the natural transformation

(C.4)

(\pi^{\prime}_{\operatorname{Ran}})_{*}\circ(s^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\operatorname{Ran}}})^{*}\circ(s^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\operatorname{Ran}}})_{*}\circ(s^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{T}},{\operatorname{Ran}}})_{*}\to(\pi^{\prime}_{\operatorname{Ran}})_{*}\circ(s^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{T}},{\operatorname{Ran}}})_{*}\simeq(\pi_{\operatorname{Ran}})_{*},

arising from the
counit
of the
((s^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\operatorname{Ran}}})^{*},(s^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\operatorname{Ran}}})_{*})
-adjunction.

We will show that both (
C.3
) and (
C.4
) are isomorphisms
when evaluated on objects lying in the essential image of the functor (
C.1
).
This will imply that (
C.2
) is also an isomorphism on such objects.

C.2.5.
Verification that (
C.3
) is an isomorphism

Along with
{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}({\mathcal{T}}_{\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X,{\mathcal{Y}})})_{\operatorname{Ran}}
we can consider its unital version

{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}({\mathcal{T}}_{\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X,{\mathcal{Y}})})_{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}.

Note that the functors

(s^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\operatorname{Ran}}})^{*},\,\,(s^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\operatorname{Ran}}})_{*},\,\,(s^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{T}},{\operatorname{Ran}}})^{*},\,\,(s^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{T}},{\operatorname{Ran}}})_{*}

upgrade to functors

(s^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}})^{*},\,\,(s^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}})_{*},\,\,(s_{{\mathcal{T}},{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}})^{*},\,\,(s_{{\mathcal{T}},{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}})_{*}

between the corresponding unital categories.

Hence, the functor

(s^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\operatorname{Ran}}})^{*}\circ(s^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\operatorname{Ran}}})_{*}\circ(s^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{T}},{\operatorname{Ran}}})_{*}

sends objects that lie in the essential image of the functor (
C.1
) to objects that lie in the essential
image of the corresponding functor

(C.5)

{\mathsf{t}}^{!}:{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}({\mathcal{T}}_{\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X,{\mathcal{Y}})})_{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}\to{\operatorname{QCoh}}_{\operatorname{co}}({\mathcal{T}}_{\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X,{\mathcal{Y}})})_{\operatorname{Ran}}.

We obtain that it is enough to show that the natural transformation

(C.6)

(\pi^{\prime}_{\operatorname{Ran}})^{{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}}_{*}\circ(s^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{T}},{\operatorname{Ran}}})^{*}\to(\pi_{\operatorname{Ran}})^{{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}}_{*},

arising from the
counit
of the
((s^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{T}},{\operatorname{Ran}}})^{*},(s^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{T}},{\operatorname{Ran}}})_{*})
-adjunction, is an isomorphism when evaluated on
objects lying in the essential image of the functor (
C.5
).

However, thanks to the identification

s^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{T}},{\operatorname{Ran}}}\simeq s_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X,{\mathcal{Y}})}}

(see Sect.
5.6.4
), the latter statement is a parameterized (by
\operatorname{Sect}(X,{\mathcal{Y}})
) version of Proposition
B.2.6
for the
relative affine scheme
{\mathcal{Z}}_{\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X,{\mathcal{Y}})}
.

C.2.6.
Verification that (
C.4
) is an isomorphism

As above, it is enough to show that the natural transformation

(C.7)

(\pi^{\prime}_{\operatorname{Ran}})^{{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}}_{*}\circ(s^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\operatorname{Ran}}})^{*}\circ(s^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\operatorname{Ran}}})_{*}\to(\pi^{\prime}_{\operatorname{Ran}})^{{\operatorname{IndCoh}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}}_{*}

is an isomorphism when evaluated on objects lying in the essential image of the functor (
C.5
).

However, by base change along the Cartesian diagram

\begin{CD}{\mathcal{T}}_{\operatorname{Sect}_{\nabla}(X,{\mathcal{Y}}),{\operatorname{Ran}}}@>{s^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\operatorname{Ran}}}}>{}>{\mathcal{T}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
\operatorname{Sect}(X,{\mathcal{Y}})\times{\operatorname{Ran}}@>{s_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\operatorname{Ran}}}}>{}>{\mathcal{Y}}_{\operatorname{Ran}},\end{CD}

this reduces to the assertion of Proposition
C.1.5
.

∎[Proposition
5.6.8
]

C.3.
Proof of Proposition
C.1.5

C.3.1.

We will first reduce the assertion of Proposition
C.1.5
to that of Proposition
C.1.7
, and then prove Proposition
C.1.7
.

We need to show that the natural transformation

(\operatorname{Id}\otimes\Gamma^{{\operatorname{IndCoh}}}({\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}},-))\circ(s_{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}})^{*}\circ(s_{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}})_{*}\to(\operatorname{Id}\otimes\Gamma^{{\operatorname{IndCoh}}}({\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}},-))

arising from the counit of the
((s_{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}})^{*},(s_{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}})_{*})
-adjunction, is an isomorphism.

C.3.2.

First, note that the left-lax symmetric monoidal structure on the functor

\Gamma^{{\operatorname{IndCoh}}}({\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}},-):{\operatorname{IndCoh}}({\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}})\to\operatorname{Vect},

arising by adjunction from the monoidal structure on the functor
\omega_{{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}}\otimes-
, is actually
strictly symmetric monoidal structure. Indeed, this follows from the fact that the diagonal morphism

{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}\to{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}\times{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}

is value-wise cofinal.

C.3.3.

Similarly, we obtain that the functor

(\operatorname{Id}\otimes\Gamma^{{\operatorname{IndCoh}}}({\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}},-))\circ(s_{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}})^{*}:{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}\to{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{Sect}(X,{\mathcal{Y}}))

is
{\operatorname{IndCoh}}({\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}})
-linear, where
{\operatorname{IndCoh}}({\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}})
acts on
{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{Sect}(X,{\mathcal{Y}}))
via the
symmetric monoidal functor
\Gamma^{{\operatorname{IndCoh}}}({\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}},-)
.

C.3.4.

This implies that we have a canonical isomorphism between the functor

(\operatorname{Id}\otimes\Gamma^{{\operatorname{IndCoh}}}({\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}},-))\circ(s_{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}})^{*}\circ(s_{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}})_{*}

and

\left((\operatorname{Id}\otimes\Gamma^{{\operatorname{IndCoh}}}({\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}},-))\circ(s_{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}})^{*}\circ(s_{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}})_{*}\circ(\operatorname{Id}\otimes(\omega_{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}\otimes-))\right)\otimes\Gamma^{{\operatorname{IndCoh}}}({\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}},-),

and this isomorphism is compatible with the map of both to

(\operatorname{Id}\otimes\Gamma^{{\operatorname{IndCoh}}}({\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}},-))\simeq\left((\operatorname{Id}\otimes\Gamma^{{\operatorname{IndCoh}}}({\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}},-))\circ(\operatorname{Id}\otimes(\omega_{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}\otimes-))\right)\otimes\Gamma^{{\operatorname{IndCoh}}}({\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}},-).

However, the latter map is an isomorphism, by Proposition
C.1.7
.

∎[Proposition
C.1.5
]

C.4.
Proof of Proposition
C.1.7

C.4.1.

Due to the assumption that
\operatorname{Sect}(X,{\mathcal{Y}})

is passable, self-functors on
{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{Sect}(X,{\mathcal{Y}}))
are in bijection with objects of
{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{Sect}(X,{\mathcal{Y}})\times\operatorname{Sect}(X,{\mathcal{Y}}))
, and the identity
endofunctor is given by

(\Delta_{\operatorname{Sect}(X,{\mathcal{Y}})})_{*}({\mathcal{O}}_{\operatorname{Sect}(X,{\mathcal{Y}})}).

Thus, we need to show that the map

(C.8)

(\operatorname{Id}\otimes(\operatorname{Loc}_{\mathcal{Y}}\circ\operatorname{Loc}^{R}_{\mathcal{Y}}))((\Delta_{\operatorname{Sect}(X,{\mathcal{Y}})})_{*}({\mathcal{O}}_{\operatorname{Sect}(X,{\mathcal{Y}})}))\to(\Delta_{\operatorname{Sect}(X,{\mathcal{Y}})})_{*}({\mathcal{O}}_{\operatorname{Sect}(X,{\mathcal{Y}})})

is an isomorphism.

C.4.2.

We rewrite the left-hand side in (
C.8
) as the image of
{\mathcal{O}}_{\operatorname{Sect}(X,{\mathcal{Y}})}
along the push-pull along the diagram

\begin{CD}\operatorname{Sect}(X,{\mathcal{Y}})\\
@V{\Delta_{\operatorname{Sect}(X,{\mathcal{Y}})}}V{}V\\
\operatorname{Sect}(X,{\mathcal{Y}})\times\operatorname{Sect}(X,{\mathcal{Y}})@<{}<{}<\operatorname{Sect}(X,{\mathcal{Y}})\times\operatorname{Sect}(X,{\mathcal{Y}})\times{\operatorname{Ran}}\\
&&@V{}V{\operatorname{id}\times s_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\operatorname{Ran}}}}V\\
&&\operatorname{Sect}(X,{\mathcal{Y}})\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}@<{\operatorname{id}\times s_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\operatorname{Ran}}}}<{}<\operatorname{Sect}(X,{\mathcal{Y}})\times\operatorname{Sect}(X,{\mathcal{Y}})\times{\operatorname{Ran}}\\
&&&&@V{}V{}V\\
&&&&\operatorname{Sect}(X,{\mathcal{Y}})\times\operatorname{Sect}(X,{\mathcal{Y}}).\end{CD}

By base change, we rewrite this as push-pull along

\begin{CD}&&\operatorname{Sect}(X,{\mathcal{Y}})\times\operatorname{Sect}(X,{\mathcal{Y}})\times{\operatorname{Ran}}@>{}>{}>\operatorname{Sect}(X,{\mathcal{Y}})\times\operatorname{Sect}(X,{\mathcal{Y}})\\
&&@V{}V{s_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\operatorname{Ran}}}\underset{{\operatorname{Ran}}}{\times}s_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\operatorname{Ran}}}}V\\
{\mathcal{Y}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}@>{\Delta_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}/{\operatorname{Ran}}}}>{}>{\mathcal{Y}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}\underset{{\operatorname{Ran}}}{\times}{\mathcal{Y}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}\end{CD}

of the object

(p_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}})^{*}(\omega_{\operatorname{Ran}})\in{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}.

C.4.3.

Consider the following version of the set-up of Sect.
B.3
.

Let
f:{\mathcal{Z}}_{1}\to{\mathcal{Z}}_{2}
is an affine morphism of D-prestacks.
Consider the following commutative (but non-Cartesian) diagram

\begin{CD}\operatorname{Sect}(X,{\mathcal{Z}}_{1})\times{\operatorname{Ran}}@>{s_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{1},{\operatorname{Ran}}}}>{}>{\mathcal{Z}}_{1,{\operatorname{Ran}}}\\
@V{\operatorname{Sect}(f)\times\operatorname{id}}V{}V@V{}V{f_{\operatorname{Ran}}}V\\
\operatorname{Sect}(X,{\mathcal{Z}}_{2})\times{\operatorname{Ran}}@>{}>{s_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{2},{\operatorname{Ran}}}}>{\mathcal{Z}}_{2,{\operatorname{Ran}}}.\end{CD}

The
((s_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{1},{\operatorname{Ran}}})^{*},(s_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{1},{\operatorname{Ran}}})_{*})
- and
((s_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{2},{\operatorname{Ran}}})^{*},(s_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{2},{\operatorname{Ran}}})_{*})
-adjunctions give
rise to natural transformation

s_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{2},{\operatorname{Ran}}}^{*}\circ(f_{\operatorname{Ran}})_{*}\to(\operatorname{Sect}(f)_{*}\otimes\operatorname{Id})_{*}\circ s_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{1},{\operatorname{Ran}}}^{*}

as functors

{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathcal{Z}}_{1})_{\operatorname{Ran}}\rightrightarrows{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{Sect}(X,{\mathcal{Z}}_{2}))\otimes{\operatorname{IndCoh}}({\operatorname{Ran}}).

Consider the induced natural transformation

(C.9)

(\operatorname{Id}\otimes\Gamma^{{\operatorname{IndCoh}}}({\operatorname{Ran}},-))\circ s_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{2},{\operatorname{Ran}}}^{*}\circ(f_{\operatorname{Ran}})_{*}\to\\
\to(\operatorname{Id}\otimes\Gamma^{{\operatorname{IndCoh}}}({\operatorname{Ran}},-))\circ(\operatorname{Sect}(f)_{*}\otimes\operatorname{Id})_{*}\circ s_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{1},{\operatorname{Ran}}}^{*}\simeq\operatorname{Sect}(f)_{*}\circ(\operatorname{Id}\otimes\Gamma^{{\operatorname{IndCoh}}}({\operatorname{Ran}},-))\circ s_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{1},{\operatorname{Ran}}}^{*}

as functors

{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathcal{Z}}_{1})_{\operatorname{Ran}}\rightrightarrows{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{Sect}(X,{\mathcal{Z}}_{2})).

The following is a parametrized version of Proposition
B.3.8
(a):

Proposition C.4.4
.

The natural transformation (
C.9
) is an isomorphism, when evaluated on objects lying
in the essential image of the forgetful functor

{\mathsf{t}}^{!}:{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathcal{Z}}_{1})_{\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}}\to{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathcal{Z}}_{1})_{\operatorname{Ran}}.

Corollary C.4.5
.

The natural transformation (
C.9
) is an isomorphism, when evaluated on the object

(p_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{1,{\operatorname{Ran}}}})^{*}(\omega_{\operatorname{Ran}})\in{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathcal{Z}}_{1})_{\operatorname{Ran}}.

C.4.6.

We will apply the above to

{\mathcal{Z}}_{1}={\mathcal{Y}},\,\,{\mathcal{Z}}_{2}={\mathcal{Y}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}

and
f
being the diagonal map.

Unwinding the definitions, we obtain that the map

(C.10)

(\operatorname{Id}\otimes\Gamma^{{\operatorname{IndCoh}}}({\operatorname{Ran}},-))\circ(s_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\operatorname{Ran}}}\underset{{\operatorname{Ran}}}{\times}s_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\operatorname{Ran}}})^{*}\circ(\Delta_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}})_{*}((p_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}})^{*}(\omega_{\operatorname{Ran}}))\overset{\text{\eqref{e:chir hom nat transf rel}}}{\longrightarrow}\\
\to(\Delta_{\operatorname{Sect}(X,{\mathcal{Y}})})_{*}\circ(\operatorname{Id}\otimes\Gamma^{{\operatorname{IndCoh}}}({\operatorname{Ran}},-))\circ(s_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\operatorname{Ran}}})^{*}((p_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}})^{*}(\omega_{\operatorname{Ran}}))\overset{\sim}{\to}(\Delta_{\operatorname{Sect}(X,{\mathcal{Y}})})_{*}({\mathcal{O}}_{\operatorname{Sect}(X,{\mathcal{Y}})})

identifies with the map

(\operatorname{Id}\otimes\Gamma^{{\operatorname{IndCoh}}}({\operatorname{Ran}},-))\circ(s_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\operatorname{Ran}}}\underset{{\operatorname{Ran}}}{\times}s_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\operatorname{Ran}}})^{*}\circ(\Delta_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}})_{*}((p_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}})^{*}(\omega_{\operatorname{Ran}}))\to(\Delta_{\operatorname{Sect}(X,{\mathcal{Y}})})_{*}({\mathcal{O}}_{\operatorname{Sect}(X,{\mathcal{Y}})})

of (
C.8
).

Hence, the latter map is an isomorphism by Corollary
C.4.5
.

∎[Proposition
C.1.7
]
